The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality (Guhyagarbhatattvaviniścaya)

The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, known in Sanskrit as the Guhyagarbha Tattvaviniścaya and in Tibetan as Sangwé Nyingpo De Kho Na Nyi Ngepa, is a tantra of the Net of Illusion (Māyājāla) cycle, the class of scriptures at the heart of the Ancient, or Nyingma, tradition's Mahāyoga teachings. It opens with homage to the glorious Samantabhadra and sets its scene in the boundless realm of Akaniṣṭha, where the Buddha in his enjoyment form, embodying the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas of the ten directions and four times, abides in a jewelled celestial palace of wisdom. Across a long series of chapters, this extended version of the tantra presents the view that all phenomena are primordially pure and equal, the maṇḍalas of peaceful and wrathful deities, mantra and mudrā, the commitments of practice, and the realization of mind as awakening itself. It is Toh 834 in the Kangyur, where it is placed among the Ancient Tantras, the section of the canon that preserves the tantras of the early Nyingma translation period.

In the language of Tibet: The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Homage to the Blessed One, glorious Samantabhadra.

At the very time this was explained, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, the Blessed One, the Great Enjoyment, whose nature is the vajra of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas of the ten directions and the four times, without remainder, with none left out, with nothing remaining, a nature not separate, not different, indivisible from them all, was present in this way.

In Akaniṣṭha, the abode without limit or center, upon the wheel of wisdom of immeasurable ground, pervasively luminous, stands a celestial palace blazing with various wisdom jewels. Its breadth is unbroken in the ten directions. It is square, because it is rich in immeasurable qualities. It is beautified by projecting bays of the jewels of superior wisdom. Its pinnacle is the wisdom of a single essential nature, swirling everywhere, of all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas of the ten directions and the four times without exception, not separate from one another. Its inconceivable wisdom jewels are immeasurable in their kinds and particulars of different aspects, of shape, color and the like. It is covered with inconceivable ornaments, self-arisen and billowing through the ten directions, clear and unobscured: garlands of various wisdom jewels, tassels and half-tassel ornaments, various forms, various sounds, various scents, various tastes and various tangibles. It has courtyards and gateways entered through the four doors of liberation, and porticoes of the eight liberations.

Within it, which is everywhere within, since it has no outside or inside, upon the lion throne of fearlessness, the elephant throne of power, the horse throne of miraculous power, the peacock throne of mastery, the garuḍa throne of unobstructedness, upon disks of sun and moon, naturally luminous, and upon seats of unsullied jewel lotuses, are those whose bodies have no front or back, whose faces shine forth openly in every direction, who are endowed with the major and minor marks, and who appear everywhere, throughout all that is inconceivable, with various bodies, speech and minds. From the great spontaneously present body, speech and mind, they sit in the cross-legged posture of discipline in which the two feet of method and wisdom are equal. They have six hands, the six wisdoms, blazing hands of wisdom jewels, and three heads, the inconceivable body, speech and mind. They are namely these: the Blessed One, the Tathāgata King of Consciousness, the Tathāgata King of Form, the Tathāgata King of Feeling, the Tathāgata King of Perception, and the Tathāgata King of Formations, all of them gleaming in the colors blue, white, yellow, vermilion and green. Nondual with the hosts of consorts, the supreme consorts the Expanse of Appearance, the Expanse of Solidity, the Expanse of Softness, the Expanse of Warmth, the Expanse of Movement and the rest, they pervade the whole expanse of reality without end. They dwell pervading it, filling it, like a pod full of sesame seeds.

Then there dwell, nondual, hosts of this kind beyond telling: Great Awakening Vajra Seeing, Great Awakening Vajra Hearing, Great Awakening Vajra Smelling, and Great Awakening Vajra Tasting; the host of consorts, What Is To Be Seen, What Is To Be Heard, What Is To Be Smelled, and What Is To Be Tasted; Great Awakening Vajra the Seer, Great Awakening Vajra the Hearer, Great Awakening Vajra the Smeller, and Great Awakening Vajra the Taster; the host of consorts, the Past, the Present, and the Not Yet Come; the Great Destroyer Touch, the Great Destroyer the Toucher, and the Great Destroyer the Knower of Touch; and the consorts, Not as Permanence, Not as Annihilation, Not as Self, Not as Sign, and so on.

Then the Tathāgata brought forth this very secret of those maṇḍalas themselves, nondual with the hosts of consorts, from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity:

"Oh! Oh! How wonderful!
The expanse of suchness itself, and the maṇḍala of the wisdom of mastery, the mind, and self-appearance itself, the reflection of samādhi, illusion pure and clear, is the inexhaustible precious wish-fulfilling jewel of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity that excludes nothing: it is the very abode of the wheel of ornaments, the supreme vajra."
So it was said, in secret vajra words.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the first chapter, the setting.

Then the Blessed One, the agent, Vajra Mind Samantabhadra, by the vajra of the way of the nature of all without exception, came to enter the consort, the Dharma, she who is the action, Samantabhadrī, and spoke this inspired utterance:

"oṃ The nature of reality of phenomena, the expanse of the knowable,
Samantabhadrī, mother of bliss:
the unmoving natural maṇḍala
is I, and my field of activity,
the various mudrās, shines clear each in its own place.
In the western quarter, in supreme Akaniṣṭha,
is spread a seat of the unattached lotus,
the abode where the Victors of the three times dwell.
At the four doors of liberation
it is perfectly endowed with the four mudrās.
To make clear who is empowered and who is without fortune,
it has inner and outer tiers of iron walls.
That which all the Victors keep hidden,
whose depth is hard for anyone to fathom,
Samantabhadra himself beholds."

When he had said this, he came to enter the consort, she who is the action, the Dharma, Samantabhadrī. Having been entered, the consort, she who is the action, the Dharma, Samantabhadrī, abiding in equipoise with the Blessed One, Mind Samantabhadra, in the bliss of nondual enjoyment, spoke this inspired utterance:

"Mind's very nature of mind, the chief who acts,
Samantabhadra, vajra mind,
nature of the six wisdoms:
in the east, in the inconceivable palace of Abhirati,
he who brings forth treasure like sapphire,
with the magic key of the secret assembly,
opens the doors of liberation
and bolts them within with supreme awakening.
Seated on the lotus seat of union,
he gives rise to the mind of awakening for the sake of beings,
gathers under his power the five destinies and the four kinds of birth,
and confers empowerment on the seats of body, speech and mind."

When she had said this, all became clear as the very vajra of the way of great equality.

Through that entering, all the tathāgatas of the ten directions without exception, being indivisible in a single nature, the tathāgatas themselves spoke this inspired utterance to the tathāgatas themselves:

"How wonderful! The limbs of the vajra aggregates
are known as the five perfect buddhas.
The sense sources and elements, all the many,
are the very maṇḍala of the bodhisattvas.
Earth and water are Locanā and Māmakī,
fire and wind are Pāṇḍaravāsinī and Tārā,
and space is Dhātvīśvarī, Lady of the Expanse.
The three realms of existence are awakened from the very beginning.
All phenomena without exception, every one,
are nothing other than buddhahood itself.
A phenomenon other than buddhahood itself
is not found by buddhahood itself."

When this was said, all the tathāgatas were pleased. Then the consort, she who is the action, the Dharma, Samantabhadrī, having become nondual with the Blessed One, Mind Samantabhadra, spoke this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! The thousandfold world realms of the ten directions are the solitude of wisdom;
the three realms of existence themselves are pure fields;
the five degenerations themselves are blissful;
the five aggregates themselves are manifestly awakened.
Since all is the essence of the supreme,
the Victors seek no Dharma elsewhere.
A dharma said to be other than this,
even if sought, the Victors do not find."

When this was said, the tathāgatas themselves knew all to be awakened from the very beginning.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the second chapter, raising the topic.

Then the great being of nonduality spoke this, generating as wisdom the mind awakened in wisdom:

"How wonderful, this marvelous, amazing Dharma,
the secret of all the perfect buddhas!
From the unborn, all is born;
yet in being born itself, there is no birth.
How wonderful, this marvelous, amazing Dharma,
the secret of all the perfect buddhas!
From the unceasing, all ceases;
yet in ceasing itself, there is no cessation.
How wonderful, this marvelous, amazing Dharma,
the secret of all the perfect buddhas!
From the nonabiding, all abides;
yet in abiding itself, there is no abiding.
How wonderful, this marvelous, amazing Dharma,
the secret of all the perfect buddhas!
From what is beyond reference, all is referred to;
yet in reference itself, there is no reference.
How wonderful, this marvelous, amazing Dharma,
the secret of all the perfect buddhas!
From no going and coming, there is going and coming;
yet in going and coming itself, there is no going and coming."

When this was said, all the tathāgatas and all the hosts of consorts became pervaded with delight. Then all the tathāgatas, together with the hosts of consorts, spoke this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! The Dharma secret from the very beginning:
though it appears in various ways, it is secret by nature;
by its essential nature it is utterly secret;
though it is not otherwise, it is supremely secret."

When this was said, since all the tathāgatas are indivisible, being of a single characteristic in the essential nature of primordial awakening, the great wisdom of the buddha of great compassion arose toward the ignorance that is the conceptual thought of beings, ripening inconceivably into the classes of the five kinds of beings, and they spoke this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! From the essence of the sugatas,
by the karma of their own conceptual thought, are emanated
various bodies, enjoyments,
abodes, sufferings and so on;
they grasp separately at I and mine.
No one binds them; there is no bond;
there is nothing to be bound.
It is conceptual thought, grasping at a self.
Insistently they tie knots in space.
To show the Dharma of the buddhas,
spontaneously complete from the very beginning,
without bondage and without liberation,
they make various emanations."

Thus they declared to the tathāgatas themselves.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the third chapter, generating as wisdom the ultimate and relative mind of awakening.

Then from all the tathāgatas, the six sages, beings of knowledge called Blessed by Great Compassion, were brought forth from the vajra of the body, speech and mind of the tathāgatas. Once brought forth, by the force of karma, in each and every great trichiliocosm of the ten directions, endless and limitless, in the six worlds that lie crosswise, above and below, each great sage, a blessed one, acted for the welfare of the five kinds of beings by the four modes of training. These sages, displaying birth, going forth, the practice of austerities, awakening, the subjugation of Māra, the turning of the wheel of Dharma, the showing of great miracles, passing into nirvāṇa and so on, know all four times, know everything in the mind streams of all, see everything with the miraculous eye, hear everything with the miraculous ear, act everywhere for others' benefit by miraculous means, and, being undefiled, possess the six higher knowledges that complete the conduct of Samantabhadra. They are endowed with inconceivable bodies everywhere, inconceivable minds everywhere, inconceivable faces everywhere, and inconceivable speech everywhere, and they came to appear in the ten directions, inconceivable and countless.

Having appeared in this way, all the great sages, however many, in the great state of compassion, namely, according to those to be trained, through the vehicle of gods and humans, the vehicle of the śrāvakas, the vehicle of the pratyekabuddhas, the vehicle of the bodhisattvas and the unsurpassed vehicle, taught the eighty-four thousand collections of Dharma as the remedy for the eighty-four thousand afflictions, the conceptual thoughts of ignorance. They teach them, and they will teach them. All of these culminate in teaching what is needed for the delusion that grasps at the outer and inner dependent arising of the grasped and the grasper; that karma and its result are not wasted; that one is not stained and does not become stained by karma and its result; and the unstained.

Then all the tathāgatas, in order to show the faults of saṃsāra and the qualities of nirvāṇa, spoke this inspired utterance to the tathāgatas themselves:

"How wonderful! In the inconceivable maṇḍala,
however many the unsurpassed essences,
the Victor by nature, whom no other can rob,
being free of extremes, is without self."

Then all the tathāgatas spoke this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! However many deluded phenomena of the world there are,
through the grasped and grasper of ignorant conceptual thought,
in the inconceivable thousandfold worlds
they circle in the two, outer and inner dependent arising.
The habitual tendencies of conceptual thought are dense,
and one comes to experience discordant pleasure and pain.
Reality just as it is, the expanse itself,
and the selfless nature are of a single mode;
in the nature itself there is no decline.
In the two modes, the genuine and illusion,
there is no self, no mine, no other;
since reality is the expanse of essential nature,
the pure expanse itself is of a single mode.
Self, mine and others
are nothing more than false conceptions;
there is nothing subtle and profound in them.
False conception acts only upon itself;
it does not stir elsewhere at all.
Even the stream of cause and result of false conception
is without ground or root, momentary.
Because it is the mode of the pure expanse itself,
having mastery over itself and acting on itself,
the stream of self, other and conceptual thought
is the pure, unsurpassed, supreme vehicle.
Through four vehicles there is deliverance;
they abide in the result of the one vehicle.
When it is examined well with genuine realization,
though it has no intrinsic nature, anything at all is accomplished.
The Buddha never passes into nirvāṇa,
and the Dharma will not decline;
to ripen and to train the ignorant,
the source is shown passing into nirvāṇa.
The Vinaya, the Sūtras, the Abhidharma,
commitment, practice and accomplishment,
and the tantras of body, speech and mind,
however many, in the boundless worlds,
widely renowned throughout the ten directions,
have emanated from the Secret Essence.
This Secret Essence by nature
definitively establishes the source
of all the piṭakas and all the tantras
and of the inconceivable sages who teach them.
Phenomena are mere designations by name.
In keeping with the meaning to be taught,
names are designated as words and taught;
in the teaching, the names and the words, there is no real thing."

When this was said, all phenomena became clear as great spontaneous presence by their essential nature. Then the tathāgatas brought forth this very secret of those spontaneously present nondual maṇḍalas from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity:

"Oh! The faults up to the peak of existence arise from conceiving a self.
The six classes, birth and cessation, bodies and enjoyments,
abodes, sufferings, deluded wandering and so on
are nothing other than false conception itself.
Emptiness, selflessness, primordial knowing, the self-aware mind,
beyond both object of reference and no reference, is mastery of mindfulness.
The wondrous body, speech, qualities and realms
are nowhere else: reality is just so."
So it was said, in secret vajra words.

The tathāgatas themselves knew that the countless emanations of the six sages, and what all the tathāgatas have spoken, are gathered into these.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the fourth chapter, establishing all phenomena.

Then the Tathāgata, the Blessed One, with great delight, showed that all beings possess the maṇḍala of their nature, and he also explained it as six classes of family lineage. Among these, the family of the nature of reality exists in all of them. Space too abides pervading all. Yet although the expanse of reality and space thus pervade all, if the meaning of liberation is not realized, it is like a mirror covered with rust, which cannot perform the function of showing reflections. Having said this, Vajradhara further spoke thus:

"The self-aware king who realizes the meaning of equality:
as rivers flow into the great ocean,
into these great means that realize the unsurpassed meaning
all the inconceivable vehicles of every kind are gathered without remainder.
Aho!
Realizing the two kinds of selflessness, perfecting the perfections,
the yogin who has meditated on the ten grounds in sequence
on the path that attains the state of going to the ground of a buddha
fully attains it through the two truths.
One who knows outer and inner dependent arising as illusion and mirage
enters unobstructed into substantial forms;
without a spiritual friend, beyond training, he realizes by his own awareness,
going to the ground of awakening, the supreme wish-fulfilling bliss.
Realizing the meaning of the four truths, fully abandoning,
purifying the portions of the sixteen wisdoms, utterly at peace,
having purified in sequence stream-entry and the rest,
one goes to the ground where the foes, the afflictions, are fully pacified.
Not fixed as to which vehicle it is taught in,
or else, conforming to all, entering into them widely,
or else, indefinite, not to be examined as 'this':
this, the indefinite, Vajradhara has taught.
Practicing the ten virtues and abandoning nonvirtue
is the cause of birth as gods and humans in the desire realm.
Practicing the four immeasurables and meditating on the unmoving
is the cause of birth as gods of the Brahmā abodes and so on.
Although the expanse of reality pervades all,
if one lacks the fortune to realize self-awareness,
it is like showing colors to one blind from birth:
this is the ground of the unfortunate, whose family lineage is severed.
Because the expanse of reality pervades all,
even the inconceivable unsurpassed vehicles that are taught
are merely appearances to each one's own self-aware mind.
Wandering variously in the lower realms and the six destinies
is likewise the appearance of the deluded mind, false conception;
in the expanse of suchness there is no change.
Familiarity with nonreferential self-awareness is the unsurpassed supreme.
If, through false conception, one grasps at I and mine,
various unpleasant things without reality come to appear.
If one lacks the self-aware mind of genuine knowledge,
even a sugata's field is seen as a place of the lower realms.
For one who realizes the very meaning of equality, the supreme vehicle,
the places of the lower realms themselves are the abodes of Akaniṣṭha and Tuṣita.
In the expanse of space, inconceivable,
though sun, moon, planets, stars, signs, flashing lightning,
rainbows and many clouds stir in all their variety,
the expanse of space neither changes nor declines.
In the nonreferential expanse of reality, inconceivable,
though there are the wondrous body, speech, qualities and activity,
all the chief and supreme ones of the world without exception,
and the many and varied sufferings of deluded wandering,
in suchness there is neither increase nor decrease."
So it was said, in secret vajra words.

From the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion, the fifth chapter, the brief explanation that all possess the cause that is the nature of reality, of the severing of family lineage, and of the distinctions of the eight families.

Then all the tathāgatas became of a single intent, and without moving from the samādhi in which all phenomena are awakened from the very beginning in the vajra expanse of the mode of great equality, brought forth from the vajra of body, speech and mind this, called the Wheel of the Garland of Letters, in which all phenomena abide as mere names:

"a becomes utterly stable; from the white a
extremely subtle a letters stream forth.
Having filled all directions with their clarity,
even when gathered back they remain stable, without increase or decrease.
From that all the hosts of names, clear and blazing,
stream forth and are gathered back in just the same way.
This is the cause of the wisdom of dependent arising
for vajra accomplishment."

a ka kha ga gha ṅa ca cha ja jha ña ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ṇa ta tha da dha na pa pha ba bha ma ya va ra la śa ṣa sa ha kṣa i ī u ā e ai o au

When these were brought forth, the endless ten directions of the six worlds quaked in six ways. They quaked, quaked strongly and quaked everywhere, and all phenomena came to have the character of names. Then all the tathāgatas spoke this inspired utterance:

"a is neither empty nor not empty;
there is no middle as an object of reference.
All are mere names; all the buddhas
abide in the very garland of letters.
a itself, appearing in variety
as the forty-two, ka and the rest,
gathers everything through the names of sound;
it is ascertained as the king of manifest perfection.
How wonderful! The names of the forty-five
great marvelous, amazing emanations
are the place that holds all words without exception;
they speak and teach all the various meanings.
Insubstantial, letters are the nature of mind;
though selfless, free of extremes and beyond reference,
through shapes, colors and hosts of names
they emanate and display play of every kind.
The body, speech and mind of the wisdom beings
of the thus-gone ones of the ten directions and four times,
the forty-five maṇḍalas themselves,
are complete from the first letter to kṣa.
Mind by nature is letters,
and letters have no real substance.
That nonreferential reality itself is the great wheel
of the various bodies, speech and minds.
Body, speech and mind are the marvelous,
amazing great emanation, fully summoned:
they are the expanse written by the expanse,
and therefore they are called letters.
The head of a letter is the unerring path;
the syllable dot is named wisdom;
the stroke, great means, divides it into sections.
a is unborn suchness.
ṭha is the very vajra of illusion.
ṭa is the very illusion of appearance.
ḍa is the wish-fulfilling illusion itself.
ḍha is pure illusion itself.
ṇa is illusion everywhere.
tha is manifest perfection through the net.
ta is stability through the net.
da is vividness through the net.
dha is swirling through the net.
na transforms everything through the net.
ka is the supreme mind of the eye.
kha is the supreme mind of the ear.
ga is the supreme mind of the nose.
gha is the supreme mind of the tongue.
ṅa destroys even the mind.
ca is the supreme body of the eye.
cha is the supreme body of the ear.
ja is the supreme body of the nose.
jha is the supreme body of the tongue.
ña destroys even the body.
pa is the supreme speech of the eye.
pha is the supreme speech of the ear.
ba is the supreme speech of the nose.
bha is the supreme speech of the tongue.
ma destroys even speech.
ya is arising made pure.
va is abiding made pure.
ra is destruction made pure.
la is emptiness made pure.
ṣa is impermanence made pure.
ṣa is that annihilation too does not exist.
sa is selflessness free of extremes.
ha is signlessness itself.
kṣa is the mind of the mind of wisdom.
ī is among gods as many as atoms.
ī is asuras as many as atoms.
ū is among humans as many as atoms.
ū is animals as many as atoms.
e is among hungry ghosts as many as atoms.
ai is hell-beings as many as atoms.
o destroys all.
au is all destroyed.
By this great host, the wheel of letters,
the garland of body, speech and mind is gathered.
Aho!"

Then the Tathāgata brought forth this very secret of those maṇḍalas themselves, nondual with the hosts of consorts, from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity:

"Aho!
Method and wisdom, clouds of delight, hosts of the mind of awakening, are the wheel of the cause;
the result, the ripened accomplishment of the Victors, is the forty-two marvelous hosts of names.
The unconditioned itself is the commitment that brings forth the conditioned vajra maṇḍala;
it does not transgress it: it is the great power of the gathering of causes and conditions, beyond choice.
Aho!"
So it was said, in secret vajra words, and when this was said, all the tathāgatas became the wheel of letters.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the sixth chapter, the arrangement of the Wheel of the Garland of Letters.

Then the Tathāgata, from the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion, because it is extremely secret, taught this: in the outer tantras of secret mantra and in the other inner tantras of method, it is taught as eighteen elements, and in all the treatises too it is taught as eighteen elements. Because this general scripture is extremely profound, it is taught as twenty-one elements; and the general scripture is the source from which all tantras and scriptures arise. Both the secret faculty and the secret object are declared to be extremely secret, because they are the basis from which all the sense sources and elements arise. In order to show those maṇḍalas very clearly, he spoke this inspired utterance to himself:

"How wonderful! The Dharma secret from the very beginning:
in each and every thing its nature abides.
Because its natural purity and equality are not realized,
though it is present in all, its nature is secret.
It is not elsewhere, yet it is utterly secret;
its very meaning is the cause of its secrecy.
So the One Who Possesses the Vajra of Equality has spoken:
this is the supreme essence of awakening.
Aho!"
So it was said, in secret vajra words.

All that appears as the sense faculties and their objects
is itself the maṇḍala of the bodhisattvas.
The five aggregates and the five elements
are perfected as the ten fathers and mothers of the five families.
The sense sources and the eighteen elements
are themselves the maṇḍala of male and female sattvas.
All of them, through the female bodhisattvas,
are gathered into the secret vajra faculty.
The vajra faculty is the secret skillful means;
the object of the faculty is the expanse of wisdom;
the mind of the faculty is Vajrasattva.
It is the very treasure from which all arises
and the very place to which all goes;
there is nothing that it does not produce.
It is the cause of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa:
from it everything is born and arises.
It is the cause of the mudrās of all,
the substance for accomplishing bliss and awakening.
From it they are produced, from it they are emanated;
it is the place where all accomplishments are achieved.
When the vajra faculty is joined with the lotus
and nondual bodhicitta radiates,
nonreferential bliss and enjoyments radiate:
body, speech and mind, and the five wisdoms,
the four secret goddesses,
the four perfect offering goddesses,
and the bodhicitta of entering, rising, abiding and aspiring.
Moving, stirring, rising and abiding,
summoning, holding, and likewise,
to accomplish the aim of gathering and embracing,
binding, not releasing, bringing under power:
the sweet sound of bliss resounds through the ten directions.
Supremely graceful splendor blazes;
garlands of wish-fulfilling jewels blaze;
sweet, captivating melody blazes with splendor;
beautiful forms move and stir the ten directions;
all-satisfying flavors and fragrances suffuse;
heaps of lovely divine jewels radiate;
radiating luminosity, everything blazes:
the expanse of nectar, cool, warm and soft.
a la la ho
When the secret faculty has joined the lotus,
in the manner of non-attachment
the ten perfections, cause and result, are perfected;
the ten grounds are perfected without gradual arrangement;
Universal Light and Lotus Eye are perfected as well;
the inconceivable Wheel of the Garland of Letters,
endowed with the skillful means of the genuine Great Perfection,
the unsurpassed ground of means and wisdom, is perfected too;
the great accumulations of merit and wisdom are perfected in play.
a la la ho
From the supreme secret of suchness,
the elements are twenty-one:
the eighteen are as in other tantras,
and the three are likewise the very self of all.
They are the source from which the eighteen arise,
they are also the field where awakening is accomplished,
and they are also the cause that brings forth bliss.
To state their secret characteristics:
the faculty is the vajra, king of secrets;
the object is the lotus, the expanse of supreme bliss;
the mind is the treasure from which awakening arises.
When faculty, object and mind are joined,
the great magical display of the supreme secret is emanated.
Countless maṇḍalas, as many as there are atoms,
and spontaneously present excellences
are brought forth by the supreme secret.
This is declared to be the single point.
If these supreme secret meanings
are explained to those of inferior view,
life will be exhausted before its time,
the head will split and the heart will burn.
As a magnet draws iron,
against one's will and without power,
one will pass for eons through the lower realms and states without leisure.
Therefore this should be kept utterly secret.
One of vast wisdom, whose mind is profound and spacious,
should give it to those of stable mind, great compassion and faith, firm in good conduct;
otherwise it must never be given.
Thus he spoke to himself.

This is the seventh chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on gathering into the twenty-one elements and arranging all, without exception, within the expanse.

Then, from the array of clouds of the maṇḍala of letters, he spoke this inspired utterance, this unfolding of magical display:

Mind itself, which has no root,
is the root of all phenomena.
Mind itself is the nature of letters;
letters are clouds of wish-fulfilling jewels.
Through the full manifestation of the forty-two maṇḍalas of illusion,
the maṇḍalas of the net,
all the maṇḍalas complete throughout the ten directions and four times
are accomplished.
Things turn into nectar and come forth,
the four hundred and four diseases are dispelled,
enjoyments arise and the lower realms are purified;
anything at all can be turned into something else.
Space arises as solid vajra,
blazing, fire burns,
and likewise things turn into water and pour down;
world-systems are scattered,
and all are emptied and fall.
Summoning and dismissing, binding and freeing,
healing and killing, defeat and victory:
all these are done through this samādhi.
By the wish-fulfilling names, words and forms
that are the self-appearance of the wisdom of reality,
just as light appears in darkness,
there is transformation, in the manner of the elixir that turns things to gold.
One who is diligent in honoring the Teacher, clear in realization,
who knows the samayas, mantras and mudrās without impairment
and possesses the requisites, will succeed;
one who lacks these will be ruined to no purpose.
The essence of the supreme accomplishment,
realized by the Victors of the three times,
is a wish-fulfilling treasury that is never exhausted,
arising without increase or decrease.
From non-entity, clouds of entities
arise and transform in every variety.
To realize that entities themselves are without entity
is the samādhi of mastery.
Whoever does not know the nonreferential
does not know the dharmadhātu.
Therefore, wary of entity and non-entity,
come to know the nonreferential.
Mind itself, which has no ground or root,
is not male, not female, not neuter,
not sexless, not of any caste or lineage,
not a color, not a shape,
not located anywhere, not anything at all.
It is the wisdom of the expanse of suchness,
the cause of all the mudrās of skillful means.
From means, means arise; the means are inconceivable.
Not different, yet appearing as different,
the inner and the outer:
inconceivable maṇḍalas, the play of wisdom,
the fearless supreme mudrā of Samantabhadra.
Whoever tames the mind, which is like a maddened elephant,
by resting in meditative equipoise,
and is firmly stable through mantra and mudrā,
will gain great and marvelous accomplishment.

When this had been spoken, the Tathāgatas themselves were delighted by the spectacle. Then from the vajra of the body, speech and mind of the Tathāgatas came forth these secret words:

"How wonderful! Because the path is included in the essence,
the fearless expanse itself is the supreme mudrā of Samantabhadra.
Its nature is the great body, speech and mind,
the state in which awakening is spontaneously present and all-pervading.
The essence itself, with everything included in the essence, abides everywhere.
From the state in which that is perfected, all accomplishments dawn and abide everywhere.
Aho!"
So it was said, in secret vajra words.

This is the eighth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on accomplishing the magical display.

Then, in order to make fruitful this maṇḍala through which mantra holders accomplish themselves as deities, by means of the maṇḍala that surpasses the natural maṇḍala of the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion, the Tathāgatas spoke this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! Utterly pure from the very beginning,
skillful means and wisdom abide in union.
In supreme awakening the sun and moon have risen;
that itself is declared to be the seat where they dwell.
Clear rays of light dissolve
into the expanse where all phenomena are without self.
There is no one, and no elaboration at all;
the maṇḍala is unelaborated, round in form.
The Dharma that is nirvāṇa from the very beginning
possesses wisdom as both cause and result.
The wisdom body of omniscience
is encircled all around by the wheel of Dharma.
By the radiating blessing of compassion,
the maṇḍala of the four wisdoms is emanated.
The five wisdoms and body, speech and mind,
blessings wondrous and inconceivable,
arise from the skillful means of self-appearance.
The celestial palace of vast compassion,
in which the accumulations of merit and wisdom are complete,
extends without center or edge.
Emptiness, wishlessness, signlessness and luminosity are the entrances;
therefore they are explained as the four doors of wisdom.
The way that leads there has eight steps.
Because nature is luminous from the very beginning,
thrones blaze with seats of sun and moon stacked one upon the other.
Because it is utterly pure from the very beginning,
there are seats of blazing lotuses, free of attachment.
Because it has become an inexhaustible treasure of wisdom,
there are seats of blazing wish-fulfilling jewels.
Because it never departs from equality from the very beginning,
palm trees stand there by their very nature.
Because hopes are fulfilled just as wished,
jeweled wish-granting trees blaze,
encircled with ornaments of wish-fulfilling jewels.
Because of mastery over all,
it is encircled by the great gods of the world.
From the secret ascertainment of reality,
by the forty-two great magical manifestations,
all are always joined as deities:
all without exception is the supreme maṇḍala.
This is the natural maṇḍala,
and the three maṇḍalas that surpass it as images:
the maṇḍala surpassing in samādhi,
and those that rely on bodhicitta and on the supreme gathering.
Thus they declared to themselves.

This is the ninth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter that gathers the utterly secret maṇḍala and teaches the surpassing images.

Then all the Tathāgatas, whose nature is neither one nor many, the very self of all, wishing their own great maṇḍala to arise in all the ten directions of the six worlds, spoke this inspired utterance:

Wisdom is discerned as the four directions and the center.
The inconceivable, spontaneously present maṇḍala
is enjoyed by the yogin who realizes the Great Perfection
as the great maṇḍala from which all arises.
It is a wheel of four spokes with its rim,
adorned with four corner sections and an intermediate courtyard,
square all around, with porches at the doors.
Like massing clouds of varied music,
it is adorned with the forty-two maṇḍalas.
On great thrones of lion, elephant, horse, garuḍa,
and the sky-soaring bird,
upon seats of sun, moon, lotus and jewel,
are those in the manner of perfected buddhas and of sattvas:
the kings of the wheel with their queens,
and, starting from the right, those of seeing, hearing, smelling
and tasting, together with their hosts of consorts.
In the corner sections abide the agents of seeing,
of hearing, of smelling and of tasting,
together with their consorts.
In the intermediate courtyard, the six sages are shown to abide,
and in front and behind, the doer
and the lord of deeds.
At the four porches the destroyers
abide with their hosts of consorts.
Vajra, wheel, jewel,
lotus, sword, blazing bell,
utpala flower, nāga tree and other emblems
they hold as pleasing implements in a beautiful manner.
Blue, white, golden yellow,
green and other various colors,
supple, entwined, swaying, youthful,
clear and gleaming, majestic and splendid,
great masses of radiating light,
swirling with blazing garlands:
pervading without center or edge,
the inconceivable maṇḍala is spontaneously present.
The supreme great mudrā of body,
though it never moves from the expanse of suchness,
in order to tame all beings without exception
shows the form body of genuine liberation
and various bodies in keeping with them.
The showing itself is like an illusion or a mirage;
that very manner has no movement from the expanse.
While unmoving, it appears in variety:
then, to the various discordant beings,
it appears in a form like each of them.
Though it is not contrived by suchness,
it appears individually through the force of residual karma,
like, for example, a mirror or the moon in water.
Then, to all the six classes of beings,
it appears in forms that renounce wrongdoing:
to novices, in the form of an arhat;
to pratyekabuddhas, in the manner of the rhinoceros;
and further, according to the stages of the supreme vehicle.
In the unsurpassed supreme abode of Akaniṣṭha
the body is in the form of Vairocana.
To the retinues of bodhisattvas,
likewise, without uttering supreme speech,
he teaches through the body so that they comprehend all phenomena.
Just as when a mirror is shown,
all the blemishes on the actual face are removed,
so when the retinue gazes upon the body,
the obscurations to awakening, of unfathomable depth,
appear in the body as in a mirror.
Then the ten grounds arise in sequence,
and they attain unsurpassed, perfect awakening:
the dharmakāya, immeasurable and inexpressible;
the enjoyment body, an inexhaustible treasure of jewels;
emanations by inconceivable tens of millions;
all the major and minor marks complete;
the domain everywhere a maṇḍala;
the two great accumulations perfected in play;
the supreme ground of means and wisdom;
these and others, inconceivable and supreme.
The svastika body, without birth or death,
abides in the field of all afflictions
and has become an inexhaustible treasure.
The body of the vajra knowledge holder,
the body of equality in which all is indivisible,
the wisdom body of omniscience:
then all five bodies are complete.

When this had been spoken, in all the endless and boundless ten directions of the six worlds, maṇḍalas beyond telling, as many as the atoms of the buddha fields, became clearly manifest.

Then this very secret of those maṇḍalas of great qualities of the Tathāgatas, inseparable from their hosts of consorts, came forth from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity:

"Aho!
Object of focus, without focus, unobservable, immeasurable, inconceivable:
the various maṇḍalas of self-aware wisdom are beyond all telling.
Equal and unequal, all-pervading, the expanse that pervades without pervading;
clear to all from the very beginning, the maṇḍalas are emanated, yet without elaboration.
All-pervading from the very beginning, inconceivable maṇḍalas as many as the atoms of the fields;
fully emanated from the very beginning, the essence abides in all, yet has no abiding."
So it was said, in secret vajra words.
"ao," so it was said, in secret words.

This is the tenth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on the emanation of maṇḍalas.

Then all the Tathāgatas abided in equality, and the whole inconceivable retinue as well made offerings to themselves with delight. Samantabhadra was pleased, and, dwelling in the self-arisen celestial palace that is the dwelling place of all the Tathāgatas of the ten directions and four times without exception, made of various precious jewels, inconceivable, immeasurable and vast, pervading without edge or center, its rays of light unobstructed, they spoke this inspired utterance:

Their own expanse itself radiates beyond all measure.
The maṇḍala, unbroken on every side,
is the domain of the perfect union of the three realms of existence,
arisen from the supreme cause that transcends grasping at characteristics.
The Tathāgata of utterly pure mastery,
dwelling in the abode of Akaniṣṭha,
the unsurpassed delight free of apprehended and apprehender,
the Lord honored by the chiefs of the worldly retinue,
and the others who dwell in the world-realms of the ten directions,
through the taste of nectar accomplish joy and great bliss.
Those of the past and those yet to come,
free of the stain of the three poisons, who have vanquished the hosts of Māra,
are joined in suchness.
Drawing forth the definitive meaning that unites wisdom and means,
meditate on the great means of awareness as mastery itself.
One will be adorned with inexhaustible accumulations of merit,
emptiness, wishlessness and signlessness, luminous by nature.
bhruṃ
Dwelling upon a self-arisen sun, moon, lotus and vast jewel,
those who have realized vajra equality
play spontaneously in the great perfection of equality.
The expanse of the Sugatas, abiding as suchness,
is gathered in equality with all the Victors of the four times.
It arises from Vajradhara of body, speech and mind,
who has realized all as the maṇḍala of luminosity.
Listen to the definitive meaning of the equality of the four times,
a domain that cannot be stolen, beyond all telling.
Through the power of compassion they dwell in the places and times of the six classes of beings.
Knowing the expanse in which "I" and selflessness are equal,
the aim of equality is accomplished by the Vajra Holder.
The nonconceptual body of the Vajra Holder of awakening,
by supreme wisdom, encompasses all without exception:
the Sugatas come into the state of nonabiding equality.
Through great means, unmixed and wholly concealed,
the Sugatas realize the nonreferential expanse itself,
the expanse of means and wisdom, most sublime of all.
Reaching to the very limits of the abodes of the expanse of the ten directions and four times,
holding the clear state endowed with supreme wisdom,
radiating the various blessings of compassion,
the source from which all Sugatas without exception arise,
possessing and radiating inexhaustibly like a wish-fulfilling jewel,
it will be realized by the great king of self-awareness.

Thus they spoke this inspired utterance to themselves, in secret vajra words.

This is the eleventh chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on erecting the celestial palace, the net of skillful means.

Then from the vajra of the body, speech and mind of all the Tathāgatas together with their hosts of consorts, these came forth:

bhruṃ viśva viśuddhe hūṃ vajra bhṝk oṃ jīna jīka svā ratna ṅhṝk āṃ ārolīka hāṃ brajñā ṅhṝk māṃ dhātvāśvari lāṃ dveṣarati māṃ moharati pāṃ rāgarati tāṃ vajrarati kṣīṃ hirājāya traṃ ākaśagarbhāya hrī ha hūṃ vadama jri kurupāṇa hrī hūṃ lāsye samaya stvaṃ traṃ mālye samaya hoḥ hrī gīti rāgo'haṃ a nṛdya rāgayamī mai dhāraṇi svāhā blhīṃ nissarama vāya svāhā hūṃ svarājāya svāhā muṃ śrī araṃgāya svāhā jaḥ dhūpa praviśa hūṃ puṣpa aviśa vaṃ dīpa sukhini ho gandhe cittaṃ hūṃ yamānta krit phaṭ prajñānta krit phaṭ hūṃ vighnānta krit phaṭ hūṃ padmānta krit phaṭ oṃ mahā vajradtara mahā krodheśvari jvalanī phaṭ oṃ mahā ratnadhara mahā krodteśvari jvalani phaṭ oṃ mahā rantadhara mahā krodteśvari jvalanī phaṭ oṃ mahā padmadhara mahā krodheśvari jvalanī phaṭ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ vajra citta hūṃ a a a vajra vtadra samanta a oṃ mune krīṃ svāhā oṃ mune truṃ svāhā oṃ mune spuṃ svāhā oṃ mune vraṃ svāhā oṃ mune kṣa svāhā oṃ mune ye svāhā ehyehi bhagavāna mahākārunika triśya ho samaya staṃ jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ oṃ āḥ hūṃ svāhā va va va va va jra jra jra jra jra sa sa sa sa sa ma ma ma ma ma ya ya ya ya ya oṃ āḥ hūṃ svāhā

oṃ King of wisdom, body, speech and mind,
great marvel of qualities and activity:
in reality itself, may I become
the great mudrā of union!

oṃ vajra samaya hūṃ oṃ vajra samaya stvaṃ oṃ vajra samaya ho

When these were spoken, these maṇḍalas of speech resounded, pervading all the ten directions of the six worlds.

This is the twelfth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on secret mantra.

Then the Blessed One, the male Vajra Agent and the female Vajra Action, dissolved into the nondual maṇḍala. Then, from the vajra Net of Illusion of body, speech and mind, there came forth this, called "Gathering the Wisdom and Blessings of the Great Self of the Tathāgatas of the Ten Directions and Three Times":

oṃ śūnyatā jñāna vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ mahā ādarśa śūnyatā jñāna vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ mahā pratyavekṣeṇā śūnyatā jñāna vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ mahā samanta śūnyatā jñāna vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ mahra kṛtya anuṣṭhāna śūnyatā jñāna vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ sarva tathāgata mahā kāya vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ sarva tathāgata mahā vāka vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ sarva tathāgata mahā citta vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ sarva tathāgata mahā anurāgaṇa vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ oṃ sarva tathāgata mahā pūja vajra svabhāva ātmako'haṃ

When these were spoken, they dissolved; they became clear; they blazed.

How wonderful! The marvelous and amazing Dharma,
the secret of all the perfect buddhas,
transcends all sounds, names and words,
yet various sounds arise clearly from it.
The single speech, the limbs of the maṇḍala,
inconceivable, pervades everywhere.
Sounds, names and words resound each in their own way;
all of them are the supreme mudrā of speech.
From the supreme tantra, the great tantra of the Net,
the meaning of blissful speech arises as well.
When all has become the state of the lotus,
the sounds of the path of liberation and of saṃsāra,
whatever is said, are supreme speech.
All the vehicles, high and low,
and the languages of all who go astray, without exception,
are gathered into the vajra speech of awakening.
Gathered, it resounds through the ten directions.
Sound itself is spoken in the state of non-attachment;
in the very speaking there is nothing expressed.
That inexpressible itself is various sounds,
resounding to all and understood by each in their own way.
For example, it is like the clearing of a sneeze:
by the sound of a single speech all understand.
This is the king of supreme speech.
Though the vehicles, high and low, are inconceivable,
they are not spoken apart from suchness;
as means of taming they are heard each in its own way.
However all the scriptures may be uttered,
suchness has spoken nothing.
Speech without letters, the speech of awareness,
though never brought forth on the tip of the tongue,
through the blessing of compassionate speech
makes clear the various aims of beings, each in its own way.
That clarity is the supreme vajra of speech.
Thus through speech the aims of beings are fulfilled,
though it never moves from the state of suchness,
like, for example, the sound of an echo.

This is the thirteenth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on gathering the maṇḍala and on secret mantra.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, spoke this inspired utterance, called "The Essence That Shows His Own Mudrās":

oṃ hūṃ vajrasatva hūṃ vajra prabhāna hūṃ mūṃ vajra gandha hūṃ oṃ cakra maṇḍala hūṃ āṃ lāṃ vajra ganbhāṣa lāṃ svā ratna prabhāna svāṃ oṃ vajra gandha bhāṣa āṃ oṃ padma prabhāna āṃ paṃ vajra gandha prabhāna pāṃ hāṃ vajra ga tīkṣṇa prabhāna haṃ taṃ vajra ganadha bhāṣa prabhāna taṃ śī ratna prabhāna śī hūṃ vajra gandha prabhāna ghoṣa hūṃ trāṃ khaḍga prabhāna trāṃ trāṃ vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna tri hrīḥ padma prabhāna hrīḥ vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna hrīḥ ha vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna haṃ a vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna āḥ me kāla prabhāna me hūṃ vajra gandha prabhāna bhāṣa hūṃ thāṃ prabhāna maṇḍala thāṃ svāṃ vajra gandha bhāṣa haṃ prabhāna svāṃ hūṃ ratna svaṃ sādha bhadra prabhāna svā hrīḥ vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna hrīḥ mu utpala nīla vrabhāna mu ā vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna āḥ phaṭ vajra amaḥ mutgara prabhāna phaṭ phaṭ oṃ vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna oṃ oṃ phaṭ mahā vajra prabhāna phaṭ phaṭ hūṃ mahā ratna vajra ganadha bhāṣa prabhāna hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ hriḥ padma mutgara prabhāna hrī hrīḥ oṃ mahā vajra padma vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna hrīḥ hrīḥ a karma mutgara vajra prabhāna phaṭ phaṭ a karma vajra gandha bhāṣa prabhāna hūṃ a a a

When these were spoken, the maṇḍala of mudrā and speech became clear everywhere; it blazed.

This is the fourteenth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on perfecting the maṇḍala of mudrā and speech from ultimate and relative bodhicitta.

Then the Tathāgata, from the teaching of the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion, showed that all phenomena are gathered into the expanse of space and of the Mother, and spoke this inspired utterance:

The supreme secret of the genuine meaning:
in the maṇḍala of the mind of bliss,
the forty-two of great definitive meaning,
wish-fulfilling jewels, clear and blazing, dissolve.
All without exception is the expanse of the bindu.
All the many rays of light of the five bodies,
all of them, dissolve into the expanse that has become the Mother,
and it becomes the lord of clouds, clear and blazing.
The drop of bodhicitta
pervades all and yet does not abide;
it is not color, location or shape.
The essence of supreme qualities
is never the domain of logicians.
It is the domain of those clear and steadfast
who are thoroughly trained in the profound and vast;
it is not to be sought or examined elsewhere.
All has the character of space.
The various worlds, stable and moving,
and all the manifest appearances of the Victors:
there is nowhere in the four times and ten directions
that has not become the expanse of space.
It is the domain of the Sugatas of the three times.
Into the expanse of space realized by the Sugatas of old
all things dissolve.
All is the very expanse of wisdom;
expanse is wholly joined with expanse.
Through the richness of the expanse of wisdom
it is the vajra bindu of awakening.
With the spontaneously present maṇḍala palace
and the inconceivable that is free of limits,
pervading all and yet not abiding,
it is the abode of supreme qualities.
The uncontrived moment of continuity:
there is nothing that it does not hold.
It is not one's own conceptual thought.
Those who have rightly gone, wrong conceptions,
and other hosts of many emanations,
all of them throughout the ten directions without exception:
in it there is nothing to destroy and nothing to grasp.
It is not color, shape or location.
Noble beings who desire the supreme,
always strive diligently to make it clear!
Its final result
is the single time that has no cause and result.
All, as the characteristic of name,
has been perfected in three syllables.
Gathered by the wheel of inexhaustible ornament,
the two great accumulations too are perfected in play.
One enters the expanse without arising or perishing.
All the endless, inconceivable worlds,
both transcendent and mundane,
dissolve into the expanse of the Mother of the Victors.
All without exception is gathered into the dharmakāya.
Ho! All phenomena have the character of names.

This is the fifteenth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the utterly secret chapter on all things being gathered into the expanse of the Mother.

Then all the Tathāgatas spoke this inspired utterance, called "Blessing All the Limbs as Spontaneously Present as the Maṇḍala":

Because all is fully perfected by the Net of Illusion,
all things are seals of supreme awakening.
The essential mudrā of the secret ascertainment of reality
is hard to transgress.
The letters of the unshakable sun disk
and the letters of the precious moon disk,
the two fives emanated as a pair:
joining them is the cause from which the mudrās arise.
Joining the tips of the two, oṃ and mūṃ,
crossing the eight syllables and bringing them together at the tips,
is the seal that bestows great bliss
in union with all the Victors.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
raise hūṃ, and at its tip a vajra blazes.
The moon, bending and concealing its syllables, rings the bell,
and in an embracing manner is steadfast in the Dharma.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
at the tip of mūṃ the ringing bell blazes.
The five syllables of the sun entwine in activity;
bowing, she gazes with a smiling radiance.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
raise oṃ, and at its tip a wheel blazes.
The moon, bending and concealing its syllables, rings the bell,
and in an embracing manner is steadfast in the Dharma.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
at the tip of lāṃ the ringing bell blazes.
The five syllables of the sun entwine in activity;
bowing, she gazes with a smiling radiance.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
at the tip of svāṃ a jewel blazes.
The moon, bending and concealing its syllables, rings the bell,
and in an embracing manner is steadfast in the Dharma.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
at the tip of māṃ the ringing bell blazes.
With the five syllables of the sun she entwines in activity;
bowing, she gazes with a smiling radiance.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
at the tip of āṃḥ a lotus blazes.
The moon, bending and concealing its syllables, rings the bell,
and in an embracing manner is steadfast in the Dharma.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
raise pāṃ, and at its tip the ringing bell blazes.
The five syllables of the sun entwine in activity;
bowing, she gazes with a smiling radiance.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
at the tip of hāṃ a sword blazes.
The moon, bending and concealing its syllables, rings the bell,
and in an embracing manner is steadfast in the Dharma.
Concealing four syllables, press the hand to the heart;
raise tāṃ, and at its tip the ringing bell blazes.
With the five syllables of the sun she entwines in activity;
bowing, she gazes with a smiling radiance.
The utpala and the other emblems, the nāga tree,
the jewel sprout, the blazing sword,
the white lotus, the blazing vajra,
the wheel of Dharma, the jewel,
beautiful form and the jewel garland,
the lute and the supreme dancer herself,
the flower garland, the lady of incense offering,
the lamp, the perfume and the rest:
they entwine in activity and are steadfast in the Dharma.
Their heads come forward in a bowing manner,
and like the great mudrās above
they dissolve into the state of the nondual expanse.
The great mudrās of the four gates:
raising a human head and pressing down with a vajra,
pressing down with a great garland of skulls,
and pressing down with the activity of the crossed vajra;
the hook, the noose, sphoṭa hoḥ.
Those who abide at the four gates also
bow and embrace as above.
The mudrās of the six sages are various;
in brief, there are six mudrās:
the ringing staff and the lute,
armor, the book, the jewel casket,
and they are shown as fire and water.
The Mother is the nondual dharmadhātu.
Samantabhadra, in meditative equipoise, radiates wisdom,
having become the Mother, equally perfect, the center of the lotus.
There are also various other mudrās:
either that of the Tathāgata himself,
or else the joined vajra palms.
As for the supreme assembly of great mudrās:
when means and wisdom are joined in union,
all things, unmoved and unstirred,
abide in the state of the great mudrā.
It has forty-two limbs;
it is the very self that emanates the limbs,
and blazes as the forty-two without radiating out.
By the supreme chief who is so endowed,
likewise the supreme seals of the Victors
blaze with radiating light, twelve and six,
and so on, inconceivably.
Throughout the inconceivable ten directions and four times,
they show the aims of taming, each in its own way.
To all the vehicles, high and low,
to the inconceivable ones who are perverted,
and to all the great assemblies of the peaceful and wrathful,
they appear everywhere, conforming to each.
It is like, for example, a dancer,
who shows many forms without departing from his own body.
In brief, of all the mudrās
it cannot be said, "It is this one."
They radiate as two and as three;
moving and emanating are suchness itself.
In brief, all movement and stirring
abides in the state of the great mudrā.
The abiding itself is nonabiding, and not abiding either.
Thus it was explained by the supreme pure one himself.

Thumb and index finger: the Vajra Holder.
Thumb and middle finger: the Victor Holder.
Ring finger and thumb: great passion.
Little finger and thumb: supreme goodness.
hūṃ hoḥ
The two index fingers joined: the supreme jewel.
The two middle fingers joined: the aim of splendor.
The two ring fingers joined: the crest banner of the Victors.
With the sound of laughter, join the little fingers.
traṃ te ru ha
Index fingers above and below: supreme Dharma.
Middle fingers above and below: supreme Mañjuśrī.
Ring fingers above and below: the supreme wheel.
Little fingers above and below: the speech that speaks.
hrīḥ phaṃ ṭāṃ haṃ
Index fingers crossed: supreme activity.
Middle fingers crossed: "Protect!"
Ring fingers crossed: the supreme yakṣa.
Little fingers crossed are explained as abiding.
ha truṃ a bhaṃ
Through this great symbolic language of mudrā
the maṇḍala of the buddhas appears in beauty,
the natural maṇḍala, perfect in every excellence.
With both song and mudrā
the vividly clear maṇḍala is called down.
Through the great emanation of mudrā,
the two, the apprehended and the apprehender,
are made clear as pure; therefore it is mudrā.
Through the clarity of each syllable,
transforming them into dance is nirvāṇa.
With jaḥ, hoḥ and the rest,
transforming them into dance binds one in the vajra.

This is the sixteenth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the chapter on the emanation of mudrās.

Then the Blessed One, glorious Samantabhadra, sang this song called "The Samaya of the Weapon":

hūṃ The vajra of the three, body, speech and mind,
is the wisdom of enlightened body, speech and mind.
The wisdom of enlightened body, speech and mind,
because Vajrasattva abides in it himself,
brandishes and emanates the wisdom vajra.
How wonderful! The supreme great vajra
is given as his name
because it cannot be destroyed or divided by anything,
because it is not moved or mixed by anything,
and because it has become supreme great bliss.
All, too, are sealed with the four mudrās.
In the very mind of the Victors,
in order to show it as the own sign
of all the Vajradhara kings,
it is called the Indivisible Vajradhara.
hūṃ Just as a king with his own seal
seals a royal decree,
so, abiding in the heart of great bliss
and diligent at the time of great power,
one seals all things in that way.
Because, as it is hard to transgress, it is indestructible,
it is the abode of the king's supreme seal,
the great being, the form of the sign.
hūṃ It is proclaimed "mudrā"
because one's own body is stable.

Vajra, vajra, the greatly fierce one,
and the great self that is itself,
by the king of the means of taming,
split, demolished, vanquished, and so forth,
churned up like ashes and atoms:
by this mighty, blazing radiating and gathering,
since all things are vajra itself,
the whole element of space is filled.
From that the vajra speech arises:
oṃ vajra jvalānalārka hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ The wheel of body, speech, and mind, the three,
is the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind;
the wheel of Vairocana is the hero.
Because Vairocana's wheel abides by itself,
it brandishes and sends forth the wheel of wisdom.
How wonderful! The name bestowed
upon the great and supreme wheel:
because it is in no way destructible or divisible,
because it is in no way moved or mixed,
because it has become supreme great bliss,
all, too, are sealed with the four mudrās.
From the very mind of the Victors,
in order to show it as the own sign
of all the Cakradhara kings,
it is called the Indivisible Cakradhara.
hūṃ Just as a king with his own seal
seals a royal decree,
so, abiding in the heart of great bliss
and diligent at the time of great power,
one seals all things in that way.
Because, as it is hard to transgress, it is indestructible,
it is the abode of the king's supreme seal,
the great being, the form of the sign.
It is proclaimed "mudrā"
because one's own body is stable.
oṃ Wheel, wheel, the greatly fierce one,
and the great self that is itself,
by the king of the means of taming,
split, destroyed, vanquished, and so forth,
churned up like ashes and atoms.
hūṃ By this mighty, blazing radiating and gathering,
since all things are the wheel itself,
the whole element of space is filled.
From that the wheel speech arises:
oṃ cakra jvalānalārka hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ The lotus of body, speech, and mind, the three,
is the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind;
the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind
is the Lotus Being (Padmasattva), the hero.
Because it abides by itself as the Lotus Being,
it brandishes and sends forth the lotus axe.
How wonderful! The name bestowed
upon the great and supreme lotus:
it is in no way destructible or divisible,
and because it is in no way moved or mixed,
because it has become supreme great bliss,
all, too, are sealed with the four mudrās.
From the very mind of the Victors,
by all the Padmadhara kings,
in order to show it as their own sign,
it is called the Indivisible Padmadhara.
oṃ Just as a king with his own seal
seals a royal decree,
so, abiding in the heart of great bliss
and diligent at the time of great power,
one sets the seal upon all things in that way.
As it is hard to transgress, it is indestructible,
the abode of the king's supreme seal.
hūṃ The great being, the form of the sign,
is proclaimed "mudrā"
because one's own body is stable.
Lotus axe, the greatly fierce one,
and the great self that is itself,
by the king of the means of taming,
split, demolished, vanquished, and so forth,
churned up like ashes and atoms:
by mighty, blazing radiating and gathering,
since all things are the lotus itself,
the whole element of space is filled.
From that the lotus speech was brought forth:
oṃ padma jvalānalārka hūṃ phaṭ
svāṃ The jewel of body, speech, and mind, the three,
is the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind;
the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind
is the great quality, the hero.
The Jewel Being (Ratnasattva), abiding by itself,
brandishes and sends forth the staff of wisdom.
How wonderful! The name bestowed
upon the great and supreme vajra jewel:
it is in no way destructible or divisible,
and because it is in no way moved or mixed,
because it has become supreme great bliss,
all, too, are sealed with the four mudrās.
svāṃ In the very mind of the Victors,
in order to show it as the own sign
of all the Ratnadhara kings,
it is called the Indivisible Ratnadhara.
svāṃ Just as a king with his own seal
seals a royal decree,
so, abiding in the heart of great bliss
and diligent at the time of great power,
one seals all things in that way.
Because, as it is hard to transgress, it is indestructible,
it is the abode of the king's supreme seal,
the great being, the form of the sign.
It is proclaimed "mudrā"
because one's own body is stable.
Jewel staff, the greatly fierce one,
and the great self that is itself,
by the king of the means of taming,
split, demolished, vanquished, and so forth,
churned up like ashes and atoms:
by mighty, blazing radiating and gathering,
since all things are the jewel itself,
the whole element of space is filled.
From that the jewel speech arises:
svāṃ ratna jvalānalārka svāṃ phaṭ
hūṃ The enlightened activity of body, speech, and mind, the three,
is the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind;
the wisdom of enlightened body, speech, and mind
is the Karma Being (Karmasattva), the hero.
Because it abides by itself as the Karma Being,
it wields and sends forth the sword of wisdom.
How wonderful! The name bestowed
upon the great and supreme karma:
it is in no way destructible or divisible,
and because it is in no way moved or mixed,
because it has become supreme great bliss,
all, too, are sealed with the four mudrās.
hūṃ From the very mind of the Victors,
in order to show it as the own sign
of all the Khaḍgadhara kings,
it is called the Indivisible Khaḍgadhara.
hāṃ Just as a king with his own seal
seals a royal decree,
so, abiding in the heart of great bliss
and diligent at the time of great power,
one seals all things in that way.
Because, as it is hard to transgress, it is indestructible,
it is the abode of the king's supreme seal,
the form of the sign of the great being.
It is proclaimed "mudrā"
because one's own body is stable.
haṃ Sword, sword, the greatly fierce one,
and the great self that is itself,
by the king of the means of taming,
split, demolished, vanquished, and so forth,
churned up like ashes and atoms:
by this mighty, blazing gathering and radiating,
since all things are the sword itself,
the whole element of space is filled.
From that the speech of enlightened activity arose.

hāṃ karma jvalānalārka hūṃ phaṭ
mūṃ From the bell of body, speech, and mind, the three,
enlightened body, speech, and mind themselves become fierce.
In the supreme manner of the great method of ringing,
by brandishing the vajra three times,
goddesses, nāginīs, low-born women,
oath-bound ones, and yogins come under control.
All the yogins and yoginīs that appear
gather into one
and appear manifestly before one's senses.
With the immovable vajra endowed with method,
not passing beyond oṃ and haṃ,
brandish it grandly three times and meditate on a white tent:
it becomes the supreme, vividly present maṇḍala.
Just passing beyond oṃ and haṃ,
oneself abiding as a square on every side,
whoever is diligent in brandishing it three times
will increase in life and fortune,
and enjoyments, whatever is desired, will be accomplished.
Then, with a crescent, red in color and low,
three times, with an even connection,
whoever is diligent in brandishing it wrathfully,
for him whatever the mind is set on, and all activities,
become the very maṇḍala of great power.
Then, abiding in the color of the fierce one,
by fiercely uttering phaṭ,
oneself abiding as black in color,
abiding in the midst of the blazing at the eon's end,
wrathfully, as an act of summoning,
when one hurls it far into the distance,
the activities, whatever is desired, are surely accomplished.
oṃ vajra daha jvala hana vajra paca jvala vajra hūṃ phaṭ
Holding in the hand that which is stable in the Dharma,
its hollow is the bell, the dharma-element,
to be regarded as the assembly of the mother, Prajñā.
Its sound is melodious speech, the blissful woman;
its tongue is the dancer, nirvāṇa.
From the very heart of the dharmakāya mind,
whoever is diligent in ringing it forth
should meditate on the syllables beginning with mūṃ:
vajra, wheel, and lotus blaze.
The maṇḍala of the assembly of muṃ,
all-powerful and delightful,
is accomplished in harmony, just as wished.
oṃ vajra ghaṇḍa pāśa dharma dhāra

When they said hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ, the tathāgatas themselves were delighted with the tathāgatas themselves.

This was the seventeenth chapter, the chapter of homage, the arising of the great magical display as method and wisdom, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Tathāgata entered into the absorption of the array of the vajra secret samaya of great delight, and uttered this inspired utterance:

The supreme, most subtle samaya:
on the palm of the hand of the great mudrā,
that sacred maṇḍala of the mind
should be made four finger-widths in size.
With the five grains, the five incenses, the five jewels,
the five essences and the five medicines,
and the five nectars, mixed thoroughly and evenly,
the lines should be laid out by awareness.
With hub and spokes,
the four-pointed wheel with its rim,
with four cut corners and the square,
it should be furnished with the interspace, the courtyards, and the door-frames.
On sun and moon the size of a round fingernail,
a lotus seat the size of a chickpea;
from syllables the size of a mustard seed,
draw the mudrās the size of a sesame seed.
Surround it with various fashioned cloths,
garlands of ornaments, conches, and flavors,
and offer abundantly with massed clouds
of song, dance, and music.
With the consorts, the bestowing goddesses, and the attendant goddesses,
make offerings according to the activity desired.
Having invoked the fire that has become the mouth,
endowed with the proper shape, color, and marks,
with hosts of foods, drinks, and flavors,
give abundantly the offerings of the four activities.
The maṇḍala of the wrathful ones is likewise:
by training in what is extremely subtle,
do likewise for inconceivable ones,
just to the measure that awareness can gauge.
As for the great mudrā of offering:
with the wish-fulfilling cloud masses of the mind,
the buddha fields in all the ten directions,
grounds of various jewels, soft and smooth, blissful to touch,
beautifully drawn and adorned,
blazing heaps of all-producing jewels,
well-shaped ponds, cool and of the finest fragrance,
various houses of jewels,
ornaments and groves of wish-fulfilling trees,
the sound of songs and verses,
canopies, victory banners, garments, parasols,
necklaces, armlets, and bracelets,
the finest foods and drinks,
and gods and goddesses as numerous as atoms,
adorned with their own beauty,
with inconceivable dances and the like,
fill the element of space in the ten directions,
and offer them extensively to all the maṇḍalas.
Through the clear pervasion of the fortunate one,
gradually or else all at once,
uniting evenly with the dharma-expanse,
meditate on the great mudrā of offering.
In the radiant one or in what is blessed,
in the way the trichiliocosm enters a mustard seed,
invite the maṇḍala from the expanse and make offerings.
When they are pleased, this is the supreme samaya of accomplishment.
Through the offering of the great being,
all the buddha maṇḍalas without exception are pleased,
and all beings of the three existences without exception
are pervaded by great joy.
Through stability in the Net of Illusion,
all the buddha maṇḍalas without exception
of those gone in the ten directions and the four times,
and the beings of the three realms, become manifest.
Body, speech, mind, and all phenomena
abide nowhere and are unobservable;
by joining, like an optical illusion,
with the nature of reality, equal in intrinsic nature,
meditate on space within space.
Into the great maṇḍala thus made familiar,
the all-creating lord enters by awareness.
Then the disciples should be brought in.
Then that supreme child of good family
should offer his kingdom and his own body,
his children and wife, his wealth and riches,
what is most cherished and pleasing.
Having offered the five sovereign enjoyments,
one has made offering to all the maṇḍalas;
what need to mention the nearby maṇḍala?
All faults become purified.
Having thoroughly ascertained their faith, diligence, and discipline,
the beneficial empowerments and the empowerments of capacity
should be conferred in their proper order.
Hold them with compassion, so they are not wasted.
Or else, having crossed the palms,
within the span of the great hand
make the maṇḍala one finger in size.
The wise should do it according to the rite.
Or else, on level ground,
make the maṇḍala one cubit in size,
or else the size of one body,
or else the measure of three bodies.
Pegs, measuring cords, and colored powders,
clothes, ornaments, a beautiful and pleasing companion,
and the excellent rite of laying out the lines:
one great in learning should do this with care.
Or else make it sixteen cubits,
twenty, or twenty-five.
Meditate on the maṇḍalas of the five families,
and set out the forms endowed with body, speech, and mind.
Or else, over an earshot, a league,
or the inconceivable expanse of space,
meditate on the various buddha maṇḍalas.
To show it to the deluded who have not seen,
it should be drawn with colors,
with twenty-five colors or with five.
Open it according to the rite of taming,
with the supreme, infinite music of samādhi.
Whether with syllables, seats, or essences,
or with mudrās, or endowed with body, speech, and mind,
the wondrous and marvelous blessing,
the samaya of all buddhas, will be accomplished.
Making the grounds and so forth into mudrās,
one attains liberation stage by stage.
What need to mention what is done by pure wisdom?
It is that very thing.
The maṇḍala of the directions and times, the great being,

From the unobservable maṇḍala of the mind,
all the maṇḍalas are invited.
Into the maṇḍala indivisible from self-appearance,
the characteristic of entering is union in equality.
The wisdom of the body, speech, and mind
of those gone from all directions and times
is the undaunted Samantabhadrī.
Because Samantabhadrī abides by herself,
she rings and sends forth the bell of wisdom.
How wonderful! The name bestowed
upon the great mother of the buddhas:
she is in no way destructible or divisible,
and because she is in no way moved or mixed,
because she has become the mother of great bliss,
all, too, are sealed with the five mother mudrās.
mūṃ From the very mind of Samantabhadrī,
in order to show it as the own sign
of all the great vajra mothers,
she is called the Indivisible Bell-Holder.
mūṃ Just as, having arranged the royal seat,
a king issues a great decree,
so, abiding as the mother of great bliss
and diligent at the time of great power,
one seals all things in that way.
Because, as it is hard to transgress, it is indestructible,
it is the abode of the king's supreme seal,
the great mother herself, the form of the sign.
It is proclaimed "mudrā"
because one's own body is stable.
mūṃ Bell, bell, of melodious renown,
and the great self that is the mother herself,
by the queen of the means of taming,
split, demolished, vanquished, and so forth,
churned up like ashes and atoms:
by this mighty, blazing radiating and gathering,
since all things are the bell itself,
the whole element of space is filled.
From that the speech of the dharmakāya arises.

When mūṃ gha jvalānalārka mūṃ was said, it appeared as an assembly of mudrās.

This was the eighteenth chapter, the song of the mudrās of the weapons and samayas, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Blessed One, the glorious Samantabhadra, uttered this inspired utterance called "The Arising of Method and Wisdom":

Then, in harmony with mind and Dharma,
the mudrā of the mind is Vajradhara,
the bell of wisdom is stable in the Dharma,
and the essences of hūṃ and mūṃ blaze.
From that a ten-pointed vajra blazes:
regard it as supreme with oṃ and mūṃ.
Wisdom is the sun's rays, method the full moon;
with a blazing mass it relies on the Dharma.
From that, by the two ways of holding,
the five grains become the very mudrās.
From that, holding a vajra in the hand, one brandishes it;
from that, holding a wheel in the hand, one brandishes it;
from that, one holds a jewel in the hand;
from that, holding a lotus in the hand, one grasps;
from that, holding a sword in the hand, one cuts.
That very one, holding the vajra in the hand,
brandishes it properly in the six great methods.
From that come the two ways of holding the vajra:
as the symbols of oṃ and mūṃ,
the ten-pointed vajra, the great method,
blazes in the manner of being stable at the heart.
The buddha Vajrasattva
holds the fearless one in his hand at his heart.
From mūṃ comes the great mudrā:
with the blazing ring of the bell, the mother of the buddhas
holds it as relying on the Dharma, the object of offering.
All, in every way, belongs to the mudrā:
method and wisdom are Vajradhara;
body, speech, and mind, all of them,
and qualities and activities are wholly complete.
Visualize the buddha Vajradhara,
awakened through the Net of Illusion.
From that comes the one holding the supreme vajra;
from that, the path of the immovable sun.
From the qualities of body, speech, and mind,
holding at the tip the oṃ hūṃ svāṃ,
in the manner of being truly generated from itself,
examine it closely as the mother, Prajñā.
Firmly supported by the blazing vajra,
the mind of great method is the father of the buddhas.
It is not at all one or many;
from that dawns the activity for the welfare of beings.
From the great manner of the king Vajradhara,
thoroughly examined as mind and Dharma,
brandishing the vajra fiercely three times,
by reciting the fierce mantra and generating it,
the maṇḍala of approach is fully completed.
This is the supreme samaya of drawing near.
Wish-fulfilling trees and wish-fulfilling
jewels, riches, and all the elements
do not exist as substances;
they are the stability of the merit of one's own mind.
Wondrous, miraculous, and marvelous dharmas
do not come into being from elsewhere;
wisdom itself, relying on method,
arises in such a state.
Because the suchness of the expanse of wisdom
has become the mudrā of method,
wisdom plays within wisdom.
Whatever amasses that very play of wisdom,
the manifest arising of the Victors, the protectors,
and the teachers of blessing are likewise:
by the splendor of the power to accomplish acts,
it is the supreme, mighty illusory vajra.
Having completed the five rites,
endowed in full with the five requisites,
through the completion of the five limbs of mantra,
one who strives to engage without lapse
will, in six times thirty days,
or in twelve, fourteen, or sixteen,
accomplish the sacred one of the family of mastery.
Then, within sixteen lifetimes at most,
the five kāyas are spontaneously complete:
in that very bindu of wisdom
the bindu of wisdom itself appears,
inconceivable, infinite, supreme.
The inconceivable pure realms
fully manifest in the ten directions and the four times,
the immeasurable palaces free of limits,
the wheels, ornaments, and hosts of music,
all the inconceivable maṇḍalas without exception:
having seen these, one is intimately pleased,
and from the vast speech the meaning arises.
The harmonious wisdom is self-appearing.
Suchness, free of one and many,
without edge or center,
is not seen even by the buddhas themselves;
self-arisen wisdom appears without location.
Wrong conceptions and imputations are purified,
and because it is not other than the expanse of wisdom,
because it is connected through great compassion,
the six classes of beings, their times and abodes, all appear.
