The maṇḍala of great bliss:
having made two or else five consort pairs,
if one with faith thoroughly enters,
desire becomes delightful and equal.
That wisdom of equal fortune,
if one pursues it, the great wonder
of intimate equality will surely arise;
if one without faith lapses, one will come to ruin.

Thus was the inspired utterance.

This was the nineteenth chapter, the secret samaya of the vajra array.

Then the Tathāgata spoke this method for receiving the accomplishment of the maṇḍala of the image, the special teaching from the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion.

In the maṇḍala of primordial equality,
having joined method and wisdom,
by the king of self-aware wisdom,
all without exception, the maṇḍala,
in the immeasurable palace of suchness,
through that which has arisen as awareness,
meditate on the great mudrā of the dharmakāya,
which is equal yet does not abide in equality.
Although the maṇḍala of intrinsic nature abides
in all the world, the stable and the moving,
through the noose of the concepts of apprehended and apprehender,
self-awareness is obscured and bound as a self.
So that all may truly realize it,
the image of the dharmakāya is shown in this way.
For the sake of concepts purified, nonconceptual self-awareness,
and the complete meaning of the true great method,
various jewels and the like,
and all things made from the four elements,
all of them are causes of supreme awakening.
Forms and the like drawn as pictures:
the great wisdom, in its all-pervading reach,
has never said they do not become meaningful.
Likewise, through the maṇḍala of samādhi,
having gathered everything into the expanse
and dissolved it into one's own heart,
all is the great mudrā of the dharmakāya.
By purifying the three kinds of maṇḍala,
in the maṇḍala of the expanse of space,
through the gathering and radiating of self-aware wisdom,
all is the great mudrā of the dharmakāya.
Through the distinction of true realization,
having meditated clearly on the objects of samādhi,
in the continuous flow of Māmakī
is the true great mudrā of the dharmakāya.
Having meditated clearly on samādhi in this way,
so as to complete the supreme aim without exception,
in the maṇḍala radiating through the sky
the stainless mudrā of the dharmakāya is clear.
With the expanse and suchness not two,
having gained mastery through samādhi,
transform it into colors, shapes, and forms,
and various activities are fully displayed.
The supreme essences of meaning, too,
the factors of awakening, are fully completed,
surrounded by the supreme lords of the world.
ahoḥ From those mudrās of the dharmakāya,
accomplish the maṇḍala of the five activities.
If one gains mastery through samādhi,
it is accomplished just as one focuses on it.
In order to tame the violent and fierce,
having meditated on oneself as wrathful,
with the black dharmakāya blazing with light,
one should press down the liṅga of the obstructors.
Even the fierce and ferocious will be destroyed,
and the adverse side, too, is crushed to dust.
In this life and in all destinies,
one comes to possess exceedingly great and fierce power.
In order to gather all into the nondual expanse,
meditate on oneself as Hayagrīva.
Having meditated clearly on the red dharmakāya,
spread out the adverse ones as a seat.
If one blesses it through clarity of samādhi,
all without exception are brought under control.
Even the mighty follow behind
and will perform whatever acts are desired.
In order to increase all qualities,
oneself as the supreme bull among the Victors,
meditate on the mudrā the color of gold,
and set whatever is to increase as the seat of the dharmakāya.
If one blesses it through such purity,
qualities equal to the limits of space,
life and enjoyments, will increase,
and it is accomplished just as one focuses on it.
Through the blessing of great compassion,
in order to pacify all the fierce and wrathful without exception,
meditate on the white and shining dharmakāya,
with whatever is fierce and wrathful as the seat of the peaceful one.
If one blesses it with samādhi in this way,
even the fierce and ferocious will be pacified.
Others, too, just as one focuses on them,
become peaceful and tamed.
Through the wisdom of omniscience,
gathering the fourfold maṇḍala into the expanse,
engage in the maṇḍala without act or agent:
the spontaneously present maṇḍala is complete from the beginning.
ahoḥ The great mudrā of the dharmakāya
surely bestows whatever accomplishment is wished.
Whatever close deity one practices,
if one writes its mudrā essence and places it inside,
whatever one meditates on is accomplished without doubt.
That is equal to all the Victors;
without doubt it will surely be accomplished.
If one practices a worldly deity,
whatever deity's essence it may be,
if one writes it and places it inside the maṇḍala,
that deity will be intimately accomplished.
If one writes the dharmatā of dependent origination,
one will realize the meanings of the Dharma,
and having realized the specific and general meanings,
one realizes that there is no birth, aging, sickness, or death.
On those great mudrās,
if one writes without error the meaning of the essence
that purifies afflictions and karmas,
the suffering of the lower realms will cease to be.
Moreover, inconceivable maṇḍalas,
immeasurable great mudrās,
body, speech, and mind without interruption,
and pure realms equal to koṭis of atoms.
aho So it is, in the words of the vajra secret.

This was the twentieth chapter, the method of taking accomplishment through the image, the special teaching from the Net of Illusion: the chapter on establishing the stūpa.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called "Great Delight, the King Who Bestows Empowerment," and uttered this inspired utterance:

From the mudrā of wisdom and method,
the clear stream of the mass of seeds of bliss,
from the vajra path into the center of the lotus,
dissolving from the tip, transforms the maṇḍala.
In the immeasurable palace of the wheel of hearing,
the very essence of the clear bindu,
from the supreme assembly of the mudrās of traṃ,
the jewel maṇḍala clearly radiates and dissolves.
traṃ The great secret of the secret, the supreme secret,
and all the secrets are to be kept alike.
Apart from realizing the very meaning of the secret,
it is not to be spoken of otherwise.
In the immeasurable palace of the wheel of clarity,
the very essence of the clear bindu,
from the supreme assembly of the garland of letters,
the maṇḍala of the family clearly radiates and dissolves.
oṃ Great heir of the Victors of the three times,
pair of vajras of body, speech, and mind,
pleasing and making offerings to all the Victors,
unite equally with all the Victors.
In the immeasurable palace of the wheel of the hands,
the very essence of the clear bindu,
from the supreme assembly of the mudrās of hūṃ,
the karma maṇḍala clearly radiates and dissolves.
oṃ Since you are vajra karma,
perform all actions without exception!
All the many messengers gathering and radiating
are doing whatever you need.
In the immeasurable palace of the wheel of speech,
the very essence of the secret bindu,
from the supreme assembly of the mudrās of hrīḥ,
the wheel of Dharma clearly radiates and dissolves.
oṃ With the unsurpassed nectar of Dharma,
satisfy persons who have faith!
To those of superior, middling, and lesser minds,
proclaim the secret in harmony with them, and not otherwise.
In the immeasurable palace of the wheel of great movement,
the very essence of the clear bindu,
from hūṃ oṃ svaṃ aṃ haṃ,
meditate on the five maṇḍalas and the garland of wrathful ones.
By all of the ten directions and four times,
the great vajra of body, speech, and mind,
the maker of all the maṇḍalas,

enjoys the bestowal of all the maṇḍalas.
Without having pleased the master,
and without having obtained the empowerments,
those who undertake listening and the rest
will be without result and come to ruin.
If one confers empowerment with the crown ornament, the diadem, the garland,
the armor, the victory banner, the mudrā,
the parasol, the vase, food and drink,
and the five essences,
from this time on that heir of the Victors,
being free of all the lower realms
and holding the skill that has become their antidote,
has long life and perfect happiness.
Named Samantabhadra and so forth,
he becomes lord of the higher realms and of liberation.
The ground together with body and aggregates
is to abide on the ground of the vidyādharas.
The ground that abides on the ground of the vidyādharas
becomes the unsurpassed Samantabhadra.
Upon consorts, charming goddesses, and the rest,
that very one bestows blessing.
The many messengers, gathering and radiating,
do not transgress the command of the buddhas
and perform whatever he needs.
Superimposition, denigration, and the like
will swiftly be destroyed.
He has long life and perfect happiness,
and becomes the lord of the higher realms and of liberation.

Thus was the inspired utterance.

This was the twenty-first chapter, the conferral of empowerment, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Tathāgata, abiding in great delight, in equality, spoke this method for swiftly taking accomplishment through the special samādhi:

In order to unite with suchness,
one should meditate by means of the ultimate meaning.
Abide holding to the side of awakening.
Abiding in the field of supreme bliss,
meditate spontaneously on the Great Perfection of equality,
and hold it firmly as the mind of awakening.
From non-action, perform act and agent.
Meditate on abiding in the abode of non-abiding.
In the center of the bhaga, the liṅga and a:
having meditated on them as exceedingly shining and clear,
with radiating, blazing light pervading everything,
and having burned the abodes of the lower realms of the three times,
the radiated light dissolves into the center of the bhaga.
Having dissolved, light radiates and blazes from the expanse.
From the mother the moon and sun blaze,
dispelling the darkness of confusion in the ten directions,
the medicine that cools affliction and suffering.
The blessing of compassion is inconceivable;
the mudrās of display are inconceivable.
Though there are many rainbow clouds in the sky,
all are gathered into the expanse of space.
Though omniscience itself is inconceivable,
all gather into the dharmadhātu.
All becomes the syllable hūṃ.
That very hūṃ is the center of secret compassion.
By radiating light rays it becomes three syllables,
or else light rays of five syllables,
the fivefold meaning elaborated into forty-five,
and other supreme, inconceivable maṇḍalas as well.
Abiding in the uncontrived state,
meditate on the maṇḍala of superior wisdom.
A full moon the size of a round fingernail
should be placed in the center of the heart.
In the full disk of the sun,
an immeasurable palace, a house blazing with light rays,
a lotus seat and a jewel;
in its center abides the syllable hūṃ,
exceedingly subtle, a blazing mustard seed.
Meditate on it thoroughly in one place,
in three places, and in five places.
Becoming familiar with the eye of wisdom,
so that it could be seen even with the eye of a man (nṛ),
meditate on it as exceedingly subtle and clear.
Having become very clear-minded and familiar,
into the world systems of the ten directions, as numerous as atoms,
radiate the inconceivable blessings of compassion.
Though they are radiated, nothing is lost from the hūṃ;
though they are gathered, the ten directions do not diminish.
From suchness, without radiating or gathering,
it dissolves into the syllable muṃ.
Having dissolved, the mudrā blazes from the expanse:
exceedingly clear and blended,
meditate clearly on the very body of the mudrā.
That body, full of color and blazing with light,
the seal endowed with method and wisdom:
meditate on it radiating and blazing with light and acting for beings' welfare.
Or else, as the great mudrā,
imagine oneself with one face and two arms,
or meditate always with three faces and six arms.
The immeasurable palaces and the thrones
are in the subtle manner, as before.
Endowed with all the major and minor marks,
with a crown ornament of five letters,
the vajra, wheel, and sword blaze,
and the jewel, lotus, and bell blaze.
From those great mudrās,
the immeasurable palaces of all ten directions without exception,
sun, moon, planets, stars, maṇḍalas, seats, and ornaments:
meditate on all the maṇḍalas of the sugatas without exception.
The blessings of compassion act for the welfare of beings.
It should be known that the peaceful ones radiate in one direction.
It purifies the ten grounds equally
and perfects the retinue of the sugatas.
That supreme one of the family who practices in this way,
even though the result has not manifestly ripened,
will never meet with any suffering,
will fully attain the complete marks of gods and humans,
will never be born into the Lesser Vehicle,
and will not be parted from the arising of the sugatas:
he has entered the dhāraṇī of awakening.

Thus he spoke to himself, in the words of the vajra secret.

This was the twenty-second chapter, the accomplishment of Vajrasattva, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Tathāgata entered into the absorption called "Great Delight, Transforming the King of the Net of Illusion," and uttered this inspired utterance:

The single cause, the mode of the syllables,
blessing, and direct perception:
through these four kinds of realization,
all is manifestly perfected as the great king.
Limbs, sense faculties, and all the consciousnesses
should be known as ripened into oṃ.
Meditate on them as the perfect maṇḍala itself,
or else meditate on the wrathful maṇḍala.
Form, sound, smell, taste, touch, and the rest
should be known as ripened into muṃ.
Meditate on them as the maṇḍala of the consorts,
or else meditate on the maṇḍala of the wrathful goddesses.
Clothing, ornaments, food and drink, songs and words,
the massed clouds of dance and gesture:
if one enjoys them fully, knowing them as hūṃ,
the wondrous and marvelous
maṇḍalas will all, without exception, be pleased.
Goddesses, nāginīs, women of low caste,
whether they are distinguished or not distinguished,
in approach and close approach,
in accomplishment and great accomplishment,
into the lotus maṇḍala of the consort
emanate the maṇḍala of blissful mind.
Into all the cloud-masses of buddhas without exception
absorb it through the supreme gift of equal delight.
That accomplished essence of sun and moon,
those very beings of the six destinies, the times and the places,
are joined in delight as a great offering;
take it up with the vajra tip of the maṇḍala.
Traveling in the sky, radiant blazing, long life, and the rest:
one becomes the lord of wish-fulfilling clouds.
Those who have become fields, the chief ones and the lesser,
the skilled one places in the center of sun and moon.
With the hand of pride, meditate on their formations;
by turning into hūṃ they are completely purified.
Dissolving into the expanse, they blaze as the mudrā,
become the body, majestic with radiating light.
Meditate on Uṣṇīṣavijaya.
How wonderful, the means of liberation from the lower realms!
Primordially unborn suchness,
illusory appearance, in the manner of a mirage:
although all the acts of union and liberation are performed,
not even an atom's worth has been done.
Realizing the vessel, its contents, and the continua as pure,
through equality and superior equality,
the maṇḍala, the field of Samantabhadra,
becomes the two great accumulations in deed and conduct.
Then, with limbs undiminished and requisites complete,
through the maṇḍala of the gathering of yogins
who know the rite in its entirety,
the aim of definitive good is accomplished.
Arranging the deities in five clusters,
five combined with five,
ten wrathful males and ten wrathful females,
meditate on them in the manner of a wheel.
The tathāgata and vajra families,
the lotus family, and body, speech, and mind,
together with the host of wrathful ones:
meditate on the deities of three clusters.
The family of families, chief of the families,
the mind of minds, supreme among minds,
together with the host of wrathful ones:
meditate on the deities of a single cluster.
The great host of wrathful males and wrathful females,
and the hosts of messengers, servants, and attendants,
those who ask, "What shall we do?" and the rest,
bring the activities of accomplishment to completion.
The accomplishment of the yogin, the mantra holder,
abides in the state of equal perfection, spontaneously present,
unobstructed in any conduct whatsoever,
all primordially indivisible.
In the vajra family, meditate intensely on all
as the maṇḍala of radiating, blazing light.
Absorb it in a manner free of attachment.
Having become nondual, it is the great mudrā.
Or else, as body, speech, and mind themselves:
all without exception is body, speech, and mind.
In clear samādhi light rays radiate
and dissolve into the state of the nondual expanse.
One will attain the supreme accomplishment of a vidyādhara.
All maṇḍalas without exception are accomplished, blazing.
It dispels afflictions and suffering.
It is the state of birth into the vajra family.
For the genuine accomplishment of the mantra holder,
through all skillful means and wisdom,
meditate on the five maṇḍalas and the garland of wrathful ones.
In a manner free of attachment
emanate light rays of many kinds,
and absorb them nondually.
The essence of supreme accomplishment is achieved.
Through the yoga of skillful means and wisdom,
meditate on oneself as the tathāgatas and the consorts;
meditate on oneself as the male and female sattvas,
each mantra, each mudrā,
and each genuine samādhi individually clear.
With dwelling and enjoyments as one finds comfortable,
fully endowed with an undiminished intellect,
act in accordance with the pledges first received.
Without laziness or apathy,
if one practices without doubt,
all maṇḍalas will be accomplished,
and one attains the sublime, supreme vajra secret.
The days and dates of practice
should be as stated in the tantras.
So it is, in the words of the vajra secret.

Thus the Tathāgata himself uttered this inspired utterance to the Tathāgata himself.

This was the twenty-third chapter, the gathering maṇḍala, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Tathāgata, in great delight, sent forth clouds of music and entered into the absorption of the array of ornaments, and uttered this inspired utterance:

The samādhi that rests on the maṇḍala
manifestly perfected through the Net of Illusion:
through the gathering and radiating of the mudrās of dance,
one comes to travel the magical path through the sky.
Through the mudrā of the sounds of songs and words,
one attains the accomplishment of the Dharma.
Through the mudrā of ornaments and clothing,
one unobstructedly accomplishes the blazing king.
Through the mudrā of food and drink,
one accomplishes the wish-fulfilling body and nectar.
Through the mudrā of the vowels and consonants,
being endowed with supreme body, speech, and mind,
all, all is accomplished.
The dhāraṇī of knowing, entering, and attaining the characteristics,
the cause and conditions that ripen the result:
whoever has become endowed with this potency
is renowned as a vidyādhara in the field of the Victors.
Although such a one takes pure births
as a human, a god, or Brahmā,
he abides on a distinguished ground.
The perfections are completed in every respect.
The thirteen distinguished grounds,
supreme among the supreme of skillful means and wisdom,
cause and result distinctively, are spontaneously perfected.
The expanse of space, the clear expanse, sun and moon:
meditate on the king of wisdom in union,
and all the maṇḍalas of the Victors, every one,
will have been meditated upon without exception.
Nowhere in the four times and ten directions
will a perfect buddha be found.
Mind itself is the perfect buddha;
do not seek the buddha elsewhere.
By applying oneself fully to all maṇḍalas,
all maṇḍalas are accomplished.
If the rite has something extra or is incomplete,
the fault itself is pure, and there is no wrongdoing.

When this inspired utterance had been spoken, there was delight through the clouds of music.

Delighted by the clouds of music, he brought forth these words of the vajra secret from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities, and activity:

a hoḥ
Through attachment to the delusion that does not see,
phenomena are merely designated by names.
The six destinies, the times and places, the distinct groupings of names that clearly appear,
the domain of the profound and vast, wrongly understood, are mere designated names.
The vowels and consonants and the rest are the very essence of desire.
Mudrās, songs and words, body, speech, and mind:
the buddha perfect in qualities, the great mind of minds,
is itself vividly clear in the mind of equality.
Union pervading equality is the supreme quality, and the activities of mantra are complete.
So it is, in the words of the vajra secret.

This was the twenty-fourth chapter, accomplishing the gathering, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then all the maṇḍalas of the vajra body, speech, and mind of the tathāgatas of the ten directions and four times gathered into one, and in great delight entered into the absorption of the array of clouds of the extremely secret essence of the pledge, in which all phenomena are spontaneously present as primordially the Great Perfection, and uttered this inspired utterance:

Not realizing, realizing wrongly,
realizing in part, and not realizing the genuine itself:
the meanings of what is secret for the sake of discipline, secret in intent, concealed,
and secret by its nature
are fully indicated by words that rest on letters,
on superimposed groupings of names.
Drawing out the hidden, concealed meaning from within,
the Teacher abides in the vajra mind.
By the maṇḍala that possesses the maṇḍala,
meditate on the maṇḍala within the maṇḍala.
The maṇḍala arisen from the maṇḍala,
the maṇḍala of mind, is the supreme maṇḍala.
The secret bindu, the expanse of the maṇḍala,
possessing all of body, speech, mind, and awareness,
is the complete essence of all the sugatas.
The elements are the consorts of the wisdom family;
the great ones are the tathāgatas of the families;
the bodhicitta is the vajra host.
The faculties, objects, times, and awareness:
on this maṇḍala of Samantabhadra
one gazes with the five wisdoms
of the superior mind of the great self.
Through the union of essence and bindu,
with the music of blissful wisdom,
offer delight to wisdom that rejoices.
Through undefiled merit,
wisdom, illusion, appearing of itself,
is the infinite, supreme maṇḍala of music.
The expanse of the secret bindu itself
is the very substance of all buddhas.
To see the face of the very self, without exception,
of the body, speech, qualities, activity, and mind
manifestly perfected in the ten directions and four times
is the sublime, supreme mastery.
Abiding in the maṇḍala, the maṇḍala itself,
the perfect maṇḍala, is spontaneously present.
Continually one holds all maṇḍalas without exception.
From union with the perfect maṇḍala
the maṇḍala of mind arises completely.
The protectors who tame through the maṇḍalas of the directions and times,
by liberating the illusory maṇḍala,
enact the maṇḍala without act or agent.
In the maṇḍala of perfect wisdom,
through the union of hearing, reflection, and meditation,
the self-arisen is spontaneously present in every way.
This is the supreme pledge
that all the buddhas do not transgress.
Whoever has devotion to this,
all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas
throughout all directions and times
will regard as a close heir,
as one who has approached all maṇḍalas without exception.
Thoroughly trained and firmly established
for as many eons as there are atoms in the realms,
the result of approaching all maṇḍalas without exception
is this great secret.
All the maṇḍalas of the Victors, without exception,
who have appeared in the past and present
in the ten directions of the six worlds,
having obtained it, spontaneously perfected the five bodies.
All who are to come in the future
will be spontaneously accomplished through this.
This is the result of the definitive great secret
become the path.
In all the maṇḍalas of the Victors without exception,
there is no definitive secret meaning other than this;
even if the Victors searched, they would not find one.
It is the supreme great mudrā of all.
It is to be held by those endowed with the eye of wisdom,
thoroughly trained in hearing, reflection, and meditation.
Give it to a worthy vessel, steadfast in good character,
who offers up body and wealth.
Otherwise, never give it.
If one gives it to the deluded out of display,
one's life will be exhausted untimely,
and one will dwell long in burning and starvation.

Thus he uttered this inspired utterance to the Tathāgata himself:

By the maṇḍala that possesses the maṇḍala,
meditate on the maṇḍala within the maṇḍala.
The maṇḍala arisen from the maṇḍala,
the maṇḍala of mind, is the supreme maṇḍala.

This was the twenty-fifth chapter, the extremely secret essence of the oral instructions, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then, from the state of great delight, the Blessed One, the glorious Samantabhadra, uttered this inspired utterance, called "The Emergence of the Secret":

How wonderful! All the supreme sugatas of the three times without exception
are the pure bodhicitta, unborn from the very beginning.
Their nature is indivisible, not separating beginning from end;
being primordially equal, they are an ocean of Victors.
Without going or coming, the three times are perfected all at once.
Because there is no dividing or removing, there is no good or bad, nothing to adopt or reject.
Within conceptual thought itself is the nature of wisdom;
not producing concepts, wisdom is inexpressible.
Supreme beings without self, trained in the past,
may perhaps arise, a few in ten thousand.
Those supreme beings, manifest buddhas,
indivisible from those of former generations and those to come:
how could a few faithful ones, whose wisdom and minds have been trained for a few eons, be able to match them?
Not seeing themselves, their mental continua untrained,
deluded sentient beings who search elsewhere
do not hear it though it is taught, and do not see it though it is pointed out.
We do not awaken by searching elsewhere.
Never parted from it, they do not see the Teacher;
wishing to travel there, they cannot arrive.
What the mental continuum is, by what it is recognized,
and the acting that simply rests, exalted beyond act and agent:
whoever investigates this with the intellect attains awakening.
Unabandoned, free of all signs, through existence and nonexistence,
one does not reject what brings recollection and does not cut the stream of the cycle.
Undegenerate and undivided, in the manifest buddha,
investigating with the awareness of the intellect, and through diligence,
through offering and reliance, awakening is clearly attained.
By grasping at space, it comes to be covered by clouds.
When one knows that there is no space,
it is impossible for a cloud to arise and cover it.

In the buddha, the indestructible swastika of conceptual thought,
all things primordially abide in equality and are pure.
Abiding vividly without conception, it conceives;
being beyond description, variety arises.
The great self without self is the treasury from which all arises;
moving from the unobstructed, yet not arising,
all is the state of mind, yet it cannot be designated "mind."
Unfabricated, spontaneously abiding, primordially awakened,
since there is nothing to attain, it is primordially the Sugata.

When this had been said, the Tathāgata himself knew all the elements of phenomena and of wisdom to be naturally awakened. Having known this, from the Net of Illusion of the vajra body, speech, and mind of all the tathāgatas, the great self of things just as they are, he brought forth these words of the vajra secret:

How wonderful! The great accomplishment not accomplished,
the great accomplishment, the great wisdom,
the great ground with nothing to adopt or reject,
the dhāraṇī, unmoving, without meeting or parting,
the wisdom of conception, the wish-fulfilling treasure:
it is like a mirror for all things,
yet though one looks, no reflection appears.
Wherever this great secret may be,
though across a pit of smoldering embers
packed with the edges of razors,
climb over, children, and go to it!
Within the innermost of what lies within,
seal it in an iron casket.
In order to practice the expanse itself within itself,
practice in the supreme place of the expanse itself.
That itself is nirvāṇa without abiding.
So it is, in the words of the vajra secret.

This was the twenty-sixth chapter, establishing the oral instructions of the great secret, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Tathāgata, in great delight, dwelling in the maṇḍala of equality, displayed everything without exception in his own body, and uttered this inspired utterance:

Past, present, future,
and the time whose arising is not certain:
that these times are equal, without before or after,
was genuinely taught as having no fixed nature.
The transcendent and the worldly,
taught as particular and general,
and all that is stable and moving and the rest,
are in the manner of illusion and mirage.
A person fully endowed with keen intellect
continually unites with this,
and those among all sentient beings who have become fields
he unites into the nondual expanse.
All is the characteristic of skillful means;
means are applied to means.
That which arises from means and means
is the supreme vajra of awakening.
However many oral transmissions are spoken,
the essence of the genuine meaning
is not found wherever one seeks it:
it is self-awareness, unobscured and pure.
Continual, diligent familiarization with it
is the sublime approach.
That this is the unerring path
is fully gathered by a into bhodhi.
The expanse of suchness itself
cannot be expressed as "one" or as "this."
As three, it arises and radiates in variety:
supreme blessing, inconceivable and infinite.
The meaning is summed up, like the twelve branches of scripture.
To speak of a meaning beyond this
is not the pure path;
it is the delusion of thinking with one's own intellect.
All is the self-aware bodhicitta;
this is explained as the authority of scripture.
It is the meaning set down according to its measure.
Meditating in the manner of the three purities,
gathered into five syllables, is the great means.
Self-awareness abides in all beings.
Where there is no doing, nothing has been done.
The wish-fulfilling treasure with no time of exhaustion,
the excellent vase, the wish-fulfilling store,
the casket, and the wish-fulfilling king of jewels
do not come from somewhere else.

This was the twenty-seventh chapter, on approach, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the lord of the vajra body, speech, and mind of all the tathāgatas, by the essential nature of all the buddhas, manifested as the maṇḍala of the wrathful ones. Why was this? There were those who were fixated on what is imagined by an understanding deluded about a self, who were parted from the genuine path, who did not realize the concealed secret and clung to the hidden secret, and who were deluded about cause and result. Propelled by the rough seeds of existence, they were born in the uninterrupted hell of Extreme Heat. Tormented there by the intense suffering of extreme heat, and tormented by longing, the moment they thought, "Even somewhere far colder than this, how could it be worse?" they were racked by the intense sufferings of freezing and of cracking open like lotuses. They experienced such sufferings in the eight hells beginning with Extreme Heat and the eight beginning with Extreme Cold, passing from one world system to another, for twelve thousand great eons.

Then, when that ripening was exhausted, they were born for six great eons as hungry ghosts, racked by the sufferings of hunger and thirst. The things they desired became repugnant to them and turned into all kinds of harmful and harsh things; they became destitute, extremely emaciated and parched, and their bodies, limbs, and faculties became misshapen.

Then, when that ripening of rough karmic obscuration had gradually thinned, they took birth through the link of their former existence as great preta-rākṣasas, extremely vicious and fierce. Some had a hundred heads on one body, some various heads, some a hundred bodies with one head, some various bodies, many limbs, or various limbs. They carried all kinds of vicious weapons and had immense retinues. They had all kinds of terrifying forms and roared all kinds of terrifying roars. Their form, taste, smell, and breath made everyone bristle with horror. With terrifying cold winds and hot breath they threw the elements into turmoil in every direction. They struck beings with the four hundred and four diseases. Through a power that intoxicates and brings ruin, they brought under their control the realms of the nāgas, the realms of the asuras, the realms of the gods, the realms of Brahmā, Ābhāsvara, Śubhakṛtsna, and everything up to Bṛhatphala.

Seeing them through the power of their former connection and through omniscience, he set about taming them with compassion, and all sang this song that shows the characteristics:

How wonderful! All beings are not apart from the sugatas;
it appears as illusory emanations that burn away suffering.
This nature of great and marvelous qualities
is the friend and protector of all who suffer.
Fully dispelling delusion and confusion, crushing aversion,
it is the origin, bliss, the supreme vajra body.
Through inconceivable activities that bring the age of perfection,
by the power of great compassion it crushes the māras.
With bodies of various colors and inconceivable skillful means,
in playful sport it acts for the benefit of beings,
and through bodhicitta it establishes them on the path.
The one bliss above all beings,
a hoḥ it establishes them in the very joy of delight.
Through every blessing, bliss itself appears.

When this had been said, the Tathāgata himself was known to be utterly victorious over all, and there was delight.

Then, from the Net of Illusion that is the great vajra array of the Tathāgata, in order to display the great pride and splendor that tames the lords of the three realms of existence in the ten directions of the six worlds, the Blessed One, Great Delight, lord of the vajra body, speech, and mind of all the tathāgatas, entered into the absorption of the king of the Net of Illusion, in which great clouds of the maṇḍala of the king of wrathful ones radiate. From the expanse of suchness he brought forth the great Krodheśvarī, and with the radiant splendor of delight, crying "hi hi," he made the lotus blossom. Dissolving in the delight of nondual embrace, from the clouds of bodhicitta he uttered:

hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ viśva vajra krodha jvalana maṇḍala phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ hala hala hala hūṃ

When he had uttered this, hosts of the wrathful maṇḍala, each the size of a trichiliocosm and as many as the atoms of the ten directions, came forth, pervading all the infinite ten directions of the six worlds. Then all the ten directions of the six worlds moved, moved strongly, and moved strongly everywhere; they rumbled, rumbled strongly, and rumbled strongly everywhere; they quaked, quaked strongly, and quaked strongly everywhere; they shook, shook strongly, and shook everywhere; they scattered, scattered strongly, and scattered everywhere. All the beings of great power and great might who dwell in the thousandfold realms of the ten directions of the six worlds, the great Maheśvara (Śiva) and the rest, fainted and utterly fainted.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight himself, became the great terrifier, exceedingly fierce, who makes the flesh crawl: the great blazing Heruka, the blood drinker, dark maroon in body, able to turn into anything, with heads, arms, and legs as many as the atoms of a thousandfold realm, holding all kinds of weapons. He then took the form of three heads, six arms, and four legs. Delighting in the great charnel ground, in the middle of an ocean of blood, upon a Mount Meru of great skeletons, in the center of a wheel within a vast expanse of great blaze, he sat with one leg extended and one bent, on a seat formed of Maheśvara and the great haughty lord of the charnel grounds, each together with his consort.

Of those cloud-masses of the wrathful maṇḍala, some became the glorious great Vajra Heruka and sat in the east. Some became the glorious great Ratna Heruka and sat in the south. Some became the glorious great Padma Heruka and sat in the west. Some became the glorious great Karma Heruka and sat in the north. All of them, in the garb of the great terrifier, with fierce roars, in the expanse of blaze, with three heads, six arms, and four legs, sat on seats made of gandharvas, yakṣas, Yama, and others, together with their consorts. The great blood-drinking queens also sat, each embracing the body of her own lord.

Gathering the Herukas of the five families by absorbing them into one, they displayed the wonder of one taming many. Then the great haughty one and the others, with savage minds, displayed all kinds of vicious miracles, and with roars as from a single throat they spoke in coarse, savage words: "Hey, hey, lord of compassion, why do you act like this?" Taunting him, they menaced him with utterly savage minds and raged.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, the self of all, abiding with nine heads, eighteen arms, and eight legs, with a fierce voice, became utterly wrathful through the means of taming with compassion, and uttered:

hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ ha ha ha khāhi khāhi

Then he tore out the hearts and sense organs of that host of great vicious ones, Maheśvara and the rest. He pulled out all their entrails. He cut and chopped all their limbs and drank all their blood. He fashioned all their bones into ornaments.

Then, when he uttered "hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ e ārali jaḥ jaḥ," he gathered the world systems of the ten directions, together with space, so that they fit into the size of a single mustard seed.

Then he gathered also the wives of the great haughty king, the king of all bhūtas without exception, and the rest; the wives of all the bhūtas; and the sovereigns of the sovereigns of all the female bhūtas: the great rākṣasī Manurākṣasī, Brahmāṇī, Raudrī, Indrāṇī, Vaiṣṇavī, Kaumārī, the Red One, Amṛtā, Śāntī, Daṇḍī, Rākṣasī, Bhakṣasī, Ratī, Rudhiramadī, the intoxicated blood drinker, Ekacāriṇī, Manohārikā, Siddhikarī, Vāyudevī, the Slayer, Agnāyī, Vārāhī, Cāmuṇḍī, the Noseless One, Varuṇānī, Mahākālī, the great red-yellow Goat-Headed One, the great blue-black Kumbhakarṇī, the great yellow-black Belly-Holder, and the rest, together with their servants and the servants of their servants, their attendants and the attendants of their attendants, retinues as numerous as the atoms of the realms.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, the glorious great Heruka, through his means of taming, again made the terrifiers of the five families appear. All of them entered into the absorption called "The Arising of Clouds of the Maṇḍala of Joyous Play," and brought forth these words from the vajra body, speech, and mind:

oṃ hūṃ aṃ vajra praveśa alala hoḥ

When this was uttered, the minds of the bhūta queens were stirred with intense passion, and their lotus maṇḍalas contracted and expanded. Then, just as iron gathers to a magnet, the great rākṣasī Manurākṣasī, the Red One, Ratī, the Slayer, the great Mahākālī, the great Red-Yellow One, the great Blue-Black One, and the great Yellow-Black One embraced the body of the Blessed One, Great Delight, the glorious Vajra Heruka. Brahmāṇī, Raudrī, Indrāṇī, Vaiṣṇavī, and Kaumārī embraced the body of the Blessed One, the glorious great Heruka. Amṛtā, Śāntī, Daṇḍī, Bhakṣasī, and Rākṣasī embraced the body of the Blessed One, the glorious Ratna Heruka. Rudhiramadī, Ekacāriṇī, Manohārikā, Siddhikarī, and Vāyudevī embraced the body of the Blessed One, the glorious great Padma Heruka. Agnāyī, Vārāhī, Cāmuṇḍī, the Noseless One, and Varuṇānī embraced the body of the Blessed One, the glorious great Karma Heruka.

Then, when those wrathful maṇḍalas of the Blessed One, Great Delight, uttered "hūṃ hūṃ," the hosts of the lotus maṇḍala were drawn in and became utterly contracted. Then, delighted, they uttered "he," and from the clouds of bodhicitta the hosts of Gaurīs, the hosts of Caurīs, the hosts of Pramohās, the hosts of Vetālīs, the hosts of Pukkasīs, the hosts of Caṇḍālīs, the hosts of Ghasmarīs, and the hosts of mātṛkās came forth, wondrously equipped with their own implements. Having come forth, they took their places, starting from the east, around the great blazing wheel, in terrifying forms and holding their own implements.

Then, in great delight, they uttered "he," and the hosts of great lion-faced goddesses, the hosts of great tiger-faced goddesses, the hosts of great fox-faced goddesses, the hosts of great dog-faced goddesses, the hosts of great vulture-faced goddesses, the hosts of great kaṅka-faced goddesses, the hosts of great crow-faced goddesses, and the hosts of great owl-faced goddesses came forth, wondrously equipped with their own implements. Having come forth, they took their places with fierce radiance, starting from the east, around the outside of the great blazing wheel.

Then, in great delight, pervading all the realms of the ten directions without exception, they uttered "phaṭ," and the hosts of vajra long-striding goddesses, the hosts of vajra-faced goddesses, the hosts of vajra world goddesses, and the hosts of vajra vetālīs came forth, wondrously equipped with their own implements. Having come forth, they stood at the gates of the blazing maṇḍala in exceedingly fierce forms.

Then, when "phaṭ" was proclaimed from the clouds of great delight in the ten directions, all became wrathful, and all the mātṛkās too, each in an instant, took up her own place.

Then all those glorious great Herukas of the Blessed One, Great Delight, entered into the absorption called "The Arising of the Nectar of Great Compassion," and brought forth these words from the vajra body, speech, and mind:

oṃ vajra mahā amṛta mahā krodhe aṃ aṃ aṃ

When this was uttered, Maheśvara and all the others came out through the noses of those great wrathful Blessed Ones. They had been thrust into an ocean of filthy muck, and from the sky Ucchuṣma Krodha was sent forth, who drank up all the muck. Regaining their memory, they saw all those hosts of the wrathful maṇḍala, with nine hundred heads, one thousand eight hundred arms, and eight hundred legs, sitting in the midst of great blazing bodies.

At that time all the lords of arrogance throughout all existence in the ten directions of the six worlds were tamed at once by the inconceivable variety of means of taming, each appearing in its own way. Then, trembling violently and in terror, with utterly cowed voices, they said: "We become your servants! We become your servants! If we do not earnestly serve you,
may our heads and bodies split into a hundred pieces,
may our hearts too split and be hacked apart,
may we rot and decay and burn and perish,
and fall into hell and cry out!"
Thus they swore an oath to become servants, and were placed as seats of the maṇḍala.

All of them spoke again with one voice:
"Our wives,
and our mothers, sisters, and daughters,
please accept into the great maṇḍala;
heroic lord, please accept them.
We, with each of our retinues,
will take up even the bare name
of this great maṇḍala of the gathering
like a blazing jewel at the crown,
receiving it with minds free of deceit and full of devotion,
placing it on the crowns of our heads, and honoring it.
Whatever the purpose of that may be,
we will accomplish it completely, without exception.
If here, in the presence of the hero,
we do not accomplish it as we have said,
may our heads, bodies, and hearts
split, be hacked, and be torn into pieces."
So they spoke.

Then all the hosts of their wives and the hosts of their sisters and daughters came in an instant, and all spoke with one voice:
"We, with all our retinues,
become the servants of the great hero.
Great hero, please bestow on us
the accomplishments of activity."
So they spoke.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, placed a vajra in their hands, conferred on them the empowerment of a name, and set them outside the maṇḍala.

This was the twenty-eighth chapter, the emanation of clouds of the maṇḍala of variegated nature, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then, so that these great assemblies of the maṇḍala would be accomplished through the samaya commitments, the Blessed One, Great Delight, drew forth these maṇḍalas of speech from the awesome vajra body, speech and mind of those hosts of tathāgata wrathful ones, the great Herukas, together with their consorts:
oṃ tathāgata mahāśrī heruka mahācaṇḍa sarva duṣṭāṃ taka hana daha paca hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra mahāśrī heruka mahācaṇḍa sarva duṣṭāṃ taka hana daha paca hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ratna mahāśrī heruka mahācaṇḍa carva duṣṭāṃ taka hana daha paca hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ padma mahāśrī heruka mahācaṇḍa sarva duṣṭāṃ taka hana daha paca hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ karma mahāśrī heruka mahācaṇḍa sarva duṣṭāṃ taka hana daha paca hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ tathāgata mahākrodheśvari jvalanī sarva duṣṭāṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ mahāvajradhāra mahākrodheśvari jvalanī hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ mahāsūryaratna mahākrodheśvari vidhama hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hriḥ mehā sārasvata mahāpadma krodheśvari khāhi hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sarva amogha mahākrodheśvari viśva hūṃ phaṭ
vajra gaurī haḥ vajra caurī haḥ vajra pramohe haḥ vajra vetālī haḥ vajra caṇḍalī haḥ vajra pukkasī haḥ vajra ghasmarī haḥ vajra smaśānī haḥ
vajra siṃhamukhī he vajra vyāghramukhī he vajra sṛglamukhī he vajra śvanāmukhī he vajra ghṛḍhamukhī he vajra kādhamukhī he vajra khagamukhī he vajra ulūkamukhī he
vajra āryatejatedā jaṃḥ vajra amaka hūṃ vajra lokā vaṃ vajra bhaśmi palaya vato hoḥ
bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo bhyo
rulu rulu hūṃ ehyehi ānaya jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ rāṃ
oṃ vajra krodha samaya hūṃ oṃ vajra krodha samaya phaṭ

oṃ You who pacify the wrathful through wrath,
assembly of awesome glorious ones, wrathful out of compassion,
grant me, right here in that very state,
your marvelous blazing blessings.

oṃ vajra krodha samaya stvaṃ oṃ vajra krodha samaya ho ali ule tali tapati daṃṣṭaṃ gonāro trikharaṃ yoginī khāhi hoḥ hūṃ ha he phaṭ

When this was spoken, all the ten directions of the six worlds were scorched, utterly scorched, utterly scorched, scorched everywhere. They blazed, blazed intensely, blazed everywhere. They became filled, completely filled, filled through and through with hosts of maṇḍalas blazing in every way.

This was the twenty-ninth chapter, the emanation of the speech maṇḍala of the great host of wrathful ones, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Tathāgata entered into the meditative absorption called "the arising of the mudrās of the blessing of the great wrathful one," and spoke this inspired utterance:

"Those who know the objects of the senses,
being endowed with skillful means and wisdom,
if they clearly realize the very state of sameness,
everything is the seal of great bliss.

In the celestial palace of the wheel of great movement,
if one meditates on all the wrathful ones, male and female,
as clear and radiating rays of light,
every motion and movement is a mudrā."

So spoke the Great Peerless One.

"The limbs and the heart as five-pronged, nine-pronged and eleven-pronged,
meditate thoroughly on the activities, each one individually.
The feet endowed with skillful means and wisdom
will always become blazing vajras.
If one sustains each great means,
for each there arises a lotus free of attachment.
When that vajra and lotus are joined,
all sentient beings
will be born in the field of unsurpassed awakening.

Meditating even the deities of the maṇḍala of intrinsic nature
as the maṇḍala of the wrathful ones,
if one moves and stirs the circle of the head,
all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas quake.
If one sets the formations of the hands in motion,
all the realms of the vidyādharas quake.
If one stamps and leaps in the great stance of the feet,
all unbearable sufferings
and all the Māras and vināyakas are crushed.
If one shakes and quivers all the limbs,
from each and every pore of the body hair
countless wrathful ones, as numerous as atoms, stream forth.
Through their countless emanations,
in the many realms of the ten directions, as numerous as atoms,
the deeds of the wrathful ones, as many as particles of dust,
tame through compassion and dissolve all into the expanse.
Everything gathers completely
into the maṇḍala of the wrathful ones and the maṇḍala of the wrathful goddesses.
Everything blazes intensely as vajra rock plateaus
and as maṇḍalas of fire mountains.
This is the great general seal.

To tame the obstructors above:
the vajra wheel, with a human head held aloft.
To tame the obstructors of the world below:
the vajra plough, with a human head raised,
the great vajra pestle, and the bell.
Taming the obstructors of the east:
the intensely blazing vajra
and the great staff of stira.
Taming the obstructors of the south:
the jewel staff topped with a human head,
and seizing the great Yama by the leg.
Taming the obstructors of the west:
the staff of serpents and stira,
and the great garuḍa beats with its wings,
cutting the great oceans through at the waist.
Taming the obstructors of the north:
the jewel cudgel
and the cudgel bearing three human heads,
annihilating the yakṣas and grahas.
Taming the obstructors of the four intermediate directions:
they will be tamed by those of the four directions.
If they are not tamed by that,
taming the obstructors of the northeast:
the vajra trident, with a human head held aloft,
and Īśa himself held in the hand,
all those bhūtas are utterly subdued.
Taming the obstructors of the southeast:
the vajra blazing with flames of wisdom,
which burns even fire itself,
and the sage himself held in a mudrā,
Agni, the fire god, with his retinue of grahas, is brought under control.
Taming the obstructors of the southwest:
the vajra and the head of a rākṣasa,
the blazing, razor-sharp sword,
and Nairṛti himself held in the hand,
the hosts of rākṣasas are brought under control.
Taming the obstructors of the northwest:
the banner of the wind, with a human head held aloft,
the splendor of a most fierce elephant,
and the Guardian of Words himself held in the hand,
all distractions are brought under control.
To tame the obstructors of the world below,
there is the mudrā of great Mount Meru,
and the great boar held in the hand:
this tames the obstructors below."

"Furthermore, by the maṇḍalas of sun and moon
the sufferings of heat and cold are dispelled.
By the great maṇḍala of fire
all without exception is burned.
By the great maṇḍala of water
all without exception is washed away.
By the great maṇḍala of wind
all without exception is scattered.

Furthermore, the sword together with the wheel,
the bow and arrow, the great hammer, the great knife,
the vajra, the great spear, and the battle-axe,
bared fangs, the razor, and teeth,
the arrow, the spear, and the stira,
and various other mudrās besides.

The mudrā of Heruka in the center:
the great stira cudgel
and the great, intensely blazing wheel;
from a great conch filled with red blood,
stirred with a vajra, he drinks deeply.
The mudrā of the wrathful one of the east:
the great stira staff
and the great, intensely blazing vajra;
he drinks from a human skull filled with much red blood.
The great mudrā of the south:
the great stira staff,
and in a celestial mansion of wish-fulfilling jewel
he drinks deeply the red argha offering.
The mudrā of the wrathful one of the west:
the great stira staff
and the intensely blazing lotus free of attachment;
he drinks from a great conch filled with red blood.
The mudrā of the wrathful one of the north:
the great stira staff
and the intensely blazing crossed vajra;
he drinks from a great conch filled with red blood.
The wrathful goddesses too accord with the wrathful ones.
The mudrās of the sacred places and lands
are explained in accord with the other tantras.
The mudrās of the mātṛkās
are explained in the great transcendent sūtra."

This was the thirtieth chapter, the emanation of mudrās, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the Net of Illusion.

Then, to reveal their maṇḍala, the Blessed One, Great Delight, spoke this inspired utterance:

"The blazing maṇḍala with four spokes
is adorned with four beveled corners.
Square, with four gateways,
it is beautified by two blazing intermediate courtyards.
Resplendent with suns and various emanated skulls,
many blazing radiances swirl.
Bull, buffalo, leopard and tiger,
and the fierce bear, gripping with their paws,
hold down Īśvara, the Great God (Śiva), and the others.
Maroon-black, blue-black, yellow-black,
red-black and green-black, their bodies terrifying,
with three heads and six arms, four legs spread wide,
wearing fresh hides and various garments,
roaring great awesome sounds terrifyingly,
with snakes and garlands of skulls, with sun and moon,
with the world above and its encircling rim,
the vajra and the great conch filled,
the sword, the axe, the plough and so forth,
holding their own various implements,
they are embraced by the hosts of consorts who hold them.
With the mudrās of the sacred places and lands,
the four gates beautiful with their mudrās,
the chiefs of the hosts of handmaidens and serving women,
beautified by twelve and by eight,
with their own seats and implements,
remain ready, saying, 'What shall we do?'"

When this was spoken, the blazing maṇḍala became clearly visible everywhere, throughout all the ten directions of the six worlds.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, drew forth these vajra secret words from the vajra Net of Illusion of his own body, speech and mind:

a ho
Arising together with the eon, blazing with the hosts of poisons,
the maṇḍala wheel of all-stirring afflictions, hard to express,
inconceivable as atoms, adorned with as many worlds as there are,
having sent forth all allies, the hosts of corpses greedy for blood,
the venomous women of attachment, exceedingly hard to make clear:
from the invisible expanse, through inconceivable miracles of form,
arising together with the degenerate age, they overpower all rebirth-linkings with awesome splendor.
The terrifying ones who utterly overpower are beyond conception.
Likewise, that very thing, as the antidote, arises everywhere and is clear everywhere.
ho
However many phenomena ripen the six classes of beings is beyond telling.
Those who dwell in delusion at the end of time are beyond telling.
And however many and as long as there are actions clinging with desire,
the great fierce and raging wind is beyond conception.
That they come with bliss and are nothing other is beyond telling.
The great poisons gathered over eons, conceptualizing and wholly conceptualizing:
to overpower them, the Heruka's body and speech become terrifying.
Taking them as conditions, as fruit ripens and fully ripens from the husk,
the Blessed One, Great Delight, with his many hosts,
immeasurable, inconceivable, spontaneously present,
is exceedingly clear in taming them thoroughly.
ho
The nature of the wrathful one is not cruel and frightening,
nor do the peaceful and beautiful merely abide in a way that pleases everyone's mind.
That very thing, nondual, pervades everything with inconceivable miracles.
Displaying the terrifying play of the Herukas of the five families, numerous as atoms,
it is the expanse of pure, supreme awakening, taught in the Secret Essence; it is the very ascertainment, clearly and perfectly abiding.
ho
So it is, in vajra secret words.

This was the thirty-first chapter, the revelation of the maṇḍala of the wrathful ones, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, spoke this inspired utterance called "Revealing His Own Blessings and Qualities":

oṃ The great yoga of the buddhas
is the very supreme essence of the wisdom
of all the buddhas of the three times:
it is the Buddha Samantabhadra himself.
It is this, and is found nowhere else.
Having gained mastery over all play,
all existence and all the worlds
are buddhahood itself throughout the three times,
the essence, unmoving and spontaneously present.
From the qualities of the greatness of intrinsic nature,
for beings deluded through not realizing it,
he is fully perfected through the Net of Illusion,
and through the samaya of compassionate attachment
he displays the great body of pride itself.
In the three realms, cruel and fierce,
he thoroughly engages the fields to be tamed
and perfects the four kinds of enlightened activity.
Through the wondrous fierce majesty of wisdom,
the great view of the four pure dharmas,
the wisdom body is especially great.
Its sign being the hosts of passion,
it is the body of great attachment itself.
Because it is marked by fierceness, awe and cruelty,
it is the body of great aversion itself.
Because it thoroughly purifies the fields of those to be tamed
and rises from the sameness of the nature of reality,
it is the body of great delusion itself.
Because it is the domain of the pure world and its inhabitants,
radiating from mind, body and speech,
and is marked by me, the Victor,
it is the body of great pride itself.
The forty-two of the secret essence
are the bindu, the seminal sphere, gathered into the mind itself;
because it is marked by not giving to others,
that itself is the perfect body of jealousy.

The great thousand of the thousand of the three thousand,
the many and various world realms,
marked as passing beyond reckoning
in atoms, koṭis and the like:
that itself, appearing and existing,
is the expanse of the body, speech and mind of the terrifying one.
In the nature of dharmakāya wisdom,
the supreme bindu of the Net of Illusion,
the essence, the bindu, inconceivable itself,
the terrifying one with nine heads and eighteen arms,
is the net marked by illusion.
That itself is assembly, maṇḍala and mantra,
supreme mantra, three in one;
it is the sense objects and the elements themselves.
Self-awareness and the benefit of others are the essence itself,
with the hand emblems of skillful means and wisdom,
the great wisdom of nondual awareness.
Name and form, becoming heads,
are imagined as arms and legs.
The body that radiates from imagining
appears as anything at all out of nothing whatsoever.
The supremely fortunate illusion,
the maṇḍala of the net, is fully perfected.

The eyes of the nature of reality that overpower imagining:
the eyes are the sameness of the nature of reality,
engaging the domain of the expanse just as it is.
With eyes of anger in the sport of play,
they look at nothing other; they look at the object.
The coiled lotus itself is body and speech;
it destroys things grasped as signs.
From the wide-open eyes of great wisdom,
the wrathful frown turning into a thousand lightnings,
the lightning of masses of fire that pierces others:
because it is applied to the field of amṛta,
he is the wrathful one of supreme amṛta.
From the perfection of the sameness of the body itself,
fully displaying the arising of variety,
is the great power of wisdom's illusion.
From the gathering and radiating of the supreme unmoving one,
the wings filled with vajras
work their magic upon the hosts of wisdom,
and through the blessing of the net of compassion,
the surrounding worlds, however many, without limit,
move into the bliss of the expanse itself.
Radiating out for the purpose of bringing under control,
through the miraculous power of the horse's dance
not even one is lost.

In the boundless maṇḍala of illusion
of the sameness and non-sameness of the dharmadhātu,
the mudrā of supreme play
is the posture of the feet extended and drawn in,
the meaning of the play of skillful means and wisdom.
Through the self-powered king of great means,
the body and speech of all the buddhas
are perfected in the place of the supreme bindu.

Overpowering Īśvara, the Great God, and the rest,
those with wrathful hand emblems,
holding many kinds of sharp weapons,
cut off the arrogant, cruel aversion.
Delusion, the dharmakāya, the illusion,
the entire compassion of the net,
through the compassion of the wrathful samaya
establishes them in the bindu of sameness:
this is Yamāntaka, the king over the cruel,
truly the supreme liberator of the hosts of the cruel.
Because he has become the protector of beings,
the snakes with which the cruel adorn the dharmakāya,
in which poison and amṛta are no different,
are set as ornaments of wisdom, signs of empowerment.
With charnel-ground substances and drinking blood,
he wears the great tiger skin as the ground of good fortune.
The samaya of terrifying compassion
that cuts the root of birth and death
is supremely proclaimed as Acala the Protector.

Forming gestures with the hand of pride,
the samaya of stealing away pride,
in the domain of Samantabhadra,
the very essence of mantra:
with hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ he gathers, eats and drinks.
Through the blessing of the vajra mind
he is fully proclaimed as Hūṃkara.
The great delusion that does not spread,
in order to drink up the ocean of afflictions,
appears to drink from the great expanse of the bhandha skull cup.
The great blazing cloud of the world,
overpowering the karma of the eon,
brings forth the blaze of vajra fire
and dries up the ocean of afflictions.
Through the samaya of terrifying means,
inconceivable worlds with their encircling rims
are gathered into the immeasurable mansion of his own body.
The wisdom of the Net of Illusion
is the overpowering charnel-ground continent.
Great deities of the two clusters,
through the blessing of the vajra body,
from the unmoving bliss of the bindu,
through the great play of intrinsic nature,
hosts of vajra sorceress-messengers,
perfectly tame the three realms!

ha is the bindu, peace itself.
ha is the bindu, peace and wrath in equipoise.
ha is the bindu, the great power.
ha is the bindu, wrath itself.
ha is the bindu, play itself.
ha through the bindu is activity itself.
ha through the bindu tames the power of passion.
ha is the bindu, all actions perfected.
he is the bindu, the mind of wisdom.
he through the bindu is melodious speech.
he through the bindu is the tongue of fierce majesty.
he is the bindu, play itself.
he is the bindu, the sign of empowerment.
he through the bindu is ferocious.
he through the bindu perfects the great mind.
he through the bindu is the maṇḍala,
the supreme illusion of the terrifying one;
overpowering with the net, it blocks the path.
hūṃ is the mind of the mind of wisdom.
hūṃ is the mind and body of wisdom.
hūṃ is the mind, body and speech of wisdom.
hūṃ is the wish-fulfilling activity of wisdom.
hūṃ is the bindu, wisdom.

phaṭ is the mind of the mind of the body.
phaṭ is the mind of the speech of speech.
phaṭ is the mind of the mind of mind.
phaṭ is the mind of the mind of the dharmakāya.
phaṭ is the mind of the speech of the dharmakāya.
phaṭ is the mind of the mind of the Dharma mind.
Delighting through the Net of Illusion, ho!
Īśvara delighting through passion, ho!
Delighting in the play of the dance, ho!
Delighting, endowed with body, speech and mind, ho!
Delighting through qualities and activities, ho!
Delighting as messengers gather and radiate, ho!
Delighting by appearing to all, ho!
Delighting through blessing, ho!
Delighting in the dharmakāya, ho!
Delighting in the play of wisdom, ho!
Delighting by being nothing whatsoever, ho!
Delighting by gathering in the harmful, ho!
The dharmadhātu itself delighting, ho!

Through this, Great Delight himself became supremely delighted in himself, and all phenomena became of a single nature.

This was the thirty-second chapter, revealing the blessings and supreme qualities of Great Delight, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, in one hundred thousand.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, entered into the meditative absorption called "giving the merit of the terrifying one," and spoke this inspired utterance:

"In the wheel, the intermediate courtyards and the gates,
meditate on the terrifying one, lord of the wheel family,
together with the hosts of consorts.
Pacifying and wrathful, magnetizing and enriching:
at the center of that great terrifying host,
in the manner of fashioning oneself as the wrathful one,
meditate on the maṇḍala of body, speech and mind.

In the wheel, the intermediate courtyards and the gates,
meditate on the terrifying one, lord of the jewel family,
together with the hosts of consorts who embrace him,
endowed with the hosts of wrathful goddesses.
Pacifying and wrathful, magnetizing and enriching:
at the center of that great terrifying host,
having blessed body, speech and mind,
in the manner of fashioning oneself as the wrathful one,
meditate on the maṇḍala of body, speech and mind.

In the wheel, the intermediate courtyards and the gates,
meditate on the terrifying one, lord of the jewel family,
together with the hosts of consorts who embrace him,
endowed with the hosts of wrathful goddesses.
Pacifying and wrathful, magnetizing and enriching:
at the center of that great terrifying host,
having blessed body, speech and mind,
in the manner of fashioning oneself as the wrathful one,
meditate on the maṇḍala of body, speech and mind.
Or else, it is the maṇḍala of the lord of the family.

In the wheel, the intermediate courtyards and the gates,
meditate on the terrifying lord of the activity family,
together with the hosts of consorts who embrace him,
endowed with the hosts of wrathful goddesses.
Pacifying and wrathful, magnetizing and enriching:
set out the maṇḍala of the four activities in full.
At the center of that great terrifying host,
having blessed body, speech and mind,
having consecrated oneself as the wrathful one,
meditate on the maṇḍala of body, speech and mind.

The maṇḍala complete with the five terrifying ones:
meditate on the twenty-five, body, speech, mind,
qualities and activities, as complete.
Ornaments, attire and the rest:
just as the terrifying host appears,
meditate on the attire of great fierce majesty.
Songs, dances and mudrās,
knowing them as wisdom and skillful means,
the offerings of the mind of awakening,
the qualities of the symbolic signs and the rest,
food, things to chew, drink and clothing,
everything endowed with the five enjoyments:
in short, actually accomplish the feasts."

This was the thirty-third chapter, the samaya of giving the merit of the Great Terrifying One, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then, so that the rite and the maṇḍala might be fully understood, the Blessed One, Great Delight, spoke this inspired utterance:

"The body, speech and mind of the tathāgatas
are the maṇḍala of the completely pure dharmadhātu.
From the general maṇḍala of secret mantra,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the mother for knowing and entering.
Measuring an earshot, a yojana and so forth,
or one body-length, two or three,
as far as one's own awareness is able,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion.
Expel the harmful ones, subdue the ground and bless it.
With a sapling, a slender twig, a single stem,
and with the colors of ignorance, just as they are,
bring down the meditative absorption that is to be cultivated.
At the place for reciting the maṇḍala of the terrifying one's body and speech,
the sites, the consorts and the substances
are set out, focusing on them as the supreme of the feast.
First, worship with sublime offerings.
The form, the great cast image, the supreme conch:
I set them out in whatever appearance I wish.
Having entered the power of the terrifying one,
I make as many wrathful goddesses as there are into my retinue.
Turn back obstacles with the supreme meditative absorption.
Having arranged it as the secret essence itself,
joining the one who has passed beyond equally with oneself,
inviting him with the mantra of the mind,
in the manner of entering one's own body,
absorb his form, holding it supremely.
By saying jaḥ three times,
that one gains a body of merit
and bestows the accomplishments of the five wisdoms.
The many messengers who gather and radiate
will do whatever is needed for him.

Or else, on perfectly level ground,
the mudrās, forms and essence syllables
should be drawn in five colors.
The white elixir, the red elixir, the great scent and the great flesh;
fill the vajra and the great conch with the red elixir.
Conches and life-poles, new and old,
the supreme and the lesser transformed fields,
all the substances there may be:
employ them with the meditative absorption of the Great Terrifying One
and with a mind that does not lapse.
In the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion,
with a resolute attitude,
accomplish it thoroughly, without expectation.
Through approach and close approach,
accomplishment and great accomplishment,
when the days are complete and the signs have arisen,
apply it in conformity with the maṇḍala.
For six periods of thirty days,
or for sixty, or for ninety-six,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Great Terrifying One.
Offer your own body to the terrifying host.
Regard that itself as the maṇḍala.
They do not transgress; from the samaya
they never transgress."

This was the thirty-fourth chapter, the samaya of the maṇḍala of Great Delight, from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, uttered this essence of mantra:

akiduna jvala maṇḍala phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ
oṃ sarva tathāgata mahāśrī heruka mahākrodheśvari hūṃ phaṭ
rulu mahāsamaya hūṃ rulu rulu hūṃ bhyo hūṃ
oṃ mahāvajra śrīheruka vajrakrodheśvari sarva duṣṭān hūṃ phaṭ
rulu mahāsamaya hūṃ rulu rulu hūṃ bhyo hūṃ
oṃ ratna mahāśrī heruka ratna mahākrodheśvari viṅhamukha hūṃ phaṭ
rulu rulu mahāsamaya hūṃ bhyo hūṃ
oṃ hrīḥ padma mahāśrī heruka padma mahākrodheśvari khāhi hūṃ phaṭ
rulu mahāsamaya hūṃ rulu rulu rulu hūṃ bhyo hūṃ
oṃ karma mahāśrī heruka sarva aga mahākrodheśvari viśvarūve hūṃ phaṭ
rulu mahāsamaya hūṃ rulu rulu rulu hūṃ bhyo hūṃ
oṃ mahā sarva tathāgata vajra ratna padma amogha mahāvajra śrī heruka mahāsamaya ha he hūṃ phaṭ
rulu rulu rulu rulu hūṃ bhyo hūṃ
oṃ sarva tathāgata mahāpadma śrī heruka mantra jvala mahāmaṇḍal kāya vāka citta
oṃ oṃ oṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ svāṃ svāṃ svāṃ āṃ āṃ āṃ hāṃ hāṃ hāṃ

ho Abiding perfectly in the bindu of mind itself,
the forty-two maṇḍalas are themselves complete.
Buddha Vajra Heruka,
inconceivable clustered maṇḍala,
join me to body, speech and mind!

oṃ vajra krodha samaya hoḥ oṃ vajra krodha samaya stvaṃ oṃ aṃ hūṃ sur stvaṃ kamala mahāsukha mālai manu saman jvala nidhana ava yoga khāhi ala ala hoḥ

This was the thirty-fifth chapter, called "the arising of the essence, the mantra recitation of the Great Terrifying One," from The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.

Then the Blessed One, through the great samaya of Great Delight, entered into the meditative absorption of giving away the aggregates, elements and sense sources, and drew forth from his vajra body, speech and mind this mantra called "blessing the hosts of the cruel":

oṃ sarva tathāgata padma samyoginī jaḥ

When this was spoken, in all the ten directions of the six worlds, the essence of the body, speech and mind of the tathāgatas everywhere became a lotus, and from the one with nine heads and eighteen arms, heads, arms and legs as numerous as the atoms of a thousand world realms came to stand, reaching up to the peak of existence.

Then again he entered into the meditative absorption called "the perfection of the great maṇḍala," and rolled up great Mount Meru by stretching and bending his legs. By his saying oṃ abhicara vaśīn, the hosts of gods, the hosts of nāgas, the great yakṣas, the rākṣasas, humans, and nonhumans, however many there were, all came, without any power of their own, to lie beneath his pressing feet.

Then again, by his saying haha avira catiṣṭha, some, with their whole bodies, came to lie beneath great Mount Meru, and some, in plain sight, came to lie pinned beneath great Mount Meru.

Then he knew that a king called King of the Vast Earth, cruel and of great aversion, without faith, a reviler of the Vajra Vehicle, was dwelling in the world at large. Knowing this, he arose from that meditative absorption, and by his saying oṃ padma samayogini rulu rulu rulu hūṃ, everything became the very nature of body-passion, the attire of speech-passion, mind-passion, great jewel-passion and great activity-passion. And in the east, the south, the west, the north and the great center, in the vast surge of blood upon the great Mount Meru of great skeletons, that very thing appeared at all times.

Moreover, all around, inconceivable terrifying forms of the five bodies, inconceivable roars of the five speeches, inconceivable terrifying ones, the play of the five minds, terrifying with inconceivable faces, came to stand upon the great clusters of the five families.

From that, the great supreme essence became the head of mahāratnatilaka, and from that, in the immeasurable palace thoroughly adorned with body, speech and mind, the very bindu-essence of the Great Terrifying One abides with three heads and six arms, dwelling nondually.

Then, right there, inconceivable Great Terrifying Ones were seen covering everything, with bodies, heads, arms, legs and retinues as numerous as the atoms of a thousand realms.

Then the great Māra, the king of the Earth-Holders, said, "Great being, lord of the Earth-Holders, hey, hey! Why do you act like this?"

Then again, by the very nature of Great Delight, oṃ abhicar vaśīna hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ was spoken. At that, the inconceivable hosts and retinues who revile the sacred teaching, the sacred teaching of the Vajra Vehicle, and who harm the yogins among beings, and those great Māras together with their servants and sub-servants, tormented in their own minds, fainted utterly; unseeing, from a state of delusion, without any power of their own and without mindfulness, they went to the place beneath the feet of the great Mount Meru of skeletons.

Then the Great Terrifying One said tiṣṭha vajra aṃ aṃ, and they were parted from their cruel intent; their unfitting bodies were exchanged, and they came to abide as great universal monarchs who tame the hosts of the great gods.

Then Great Delight, the glorious Great Terrifying One, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Great Magical Display of their maṇḍala, and from the body, speech, mind, qualities and activities of all the tathāgatas he drew forth, out of the Net of Illusion, the samaya of the great vajra drop of this very mind maṇḍala called the Great Drop.

oṃ Amid the swirling billows of a sea of blood,
a hundred thousand Mount Merus of heaped skeletons,
mountains of five gathered clusters;
a blazing mansion, a wheel of four spokes,
most majestic, adorned with four projecting corners,
square, with four gateways,
beautified by two blazing intermediate courtyards,
majestic with skulls, snakes, varied ornaments, suns and moons.
The many seats rest upon great gods.
Great Delight, Samantabhadra,
with eight hands and feet,
majestic with skulls, snakes, varied ornaments, sun and moon:
the Great Terrifying One, the Heruka,
vajra essence, mind of the drop,
with three heads, six arms, and four legs spread wide.
On the four sides, worshipped by four wrathful goddesses,
he holds the manner of great delight.
In the four directions together with the corners,
the emanated yoginīs, the animal-headed goddesses,
dwell upon Mount Meru and upon great corpses.
Blazing, the great ones hold in their hands
a vajra and a great conch filled full,
a lotus palace, and beat small drums;
they bind with nooses of corpses,
hurl khaṭvāṅgas and embrace,
together with hosts that hold consorts.
The mudrās of the sacred places and lands,
and at the four gates the most beautiful mudrās,
the many great hosts of female servants,
abide in terrifying forms.

This is the thirty sixth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, called Blessing the Hosts of the Wicked as the Samaya of All the Tathāgatas.

Then all the tathāgatas became of one intention, and the Tathāgata himself uttered this inspired utterance to the Tathāgata himself:

From the maṇḍala of the path of illusion
of the supreme vajra of activity,
to enter the manifestly perfect maṇḍala of the Net
is the intention of the entering mind.
Trained in what is extremely subtle,
it is beautified by forty two maṇḍalas.
By the distinction of the two clusters,
emanating and gathering are likewise.
On delightful ground,
lay the lines with the soles of the feet and draw the maṇḍala.
Meditate on the maṇḍala of oṃ and mūṃ.
From that, fill it with subtle vajras.
Subtly gathering the earth, hold it beneath.
Imagined with the knowledge that it is the host of mothers,
any land whatever comes under control.
Imagining it as a hostile form,
the gathering and radiating of the Net, like that body,
imagine it as haṃ, like a cast image.
From that, like light, hold it beneath.
In secret, make the path of speech haṃ.
The wise one places it upon wind and moon.
By the fierce application of the tongue,
send it far away to other continents and realms.
Oneself, together with the host of hūṃ,
meditate on the unborn letter.
The host of that activity, the dharma element,
blessed as mūṃ, dissolve into the letter.
Through the radiating and gathering of the steadfast a,
meditate thoroughly as on space.
Having become stable, recite the essence of a.
When one has not yet gained experience,
join the practice with the entering mind,
knowing it as the first state,
and equal in its many aspects.
From that, the invisible, peaceful form,
the line that abides in the depths of the mind,
is never to be shown to others,
not for lending, use, sale and the like,
nor applied to enjoyments,
nor for the sake of material wealth.
To the deluded, the arrogant, and those weak in diligence,
it is taught that it must never be given;
like a despised treasure in a corpse,
it is taught that no one should approach it;
like the talk of the crow among living creatures,
it must never be spoken of.

The activity vajra, the host of the genuine,
forty four, the path of the four continents,
Samantabhadra, in the manner of skillful means:
meditate on the deities of the three clusters.
The dharma element, activity, the host of a,
the forty two that set it forth,
Samantabhadrī, in the manner of wisdom,
the genuine, primordial wisdom, the mind of minds:
meditate on the deities of the three clusters.
Dissolve these into the dharma element, a.
The sphere of the dharma of dharmas, of activity,
is Samantabhadrī, the mother of bliss:
within her state, imagine all the hosts.
The elements of earth, water and fire
arise from her sphere;
let one who so desires imagine this thoroughly.
Unabiding, to the limitless reach of space,
one who wishes to become habituated will attain this:
to walk upon water as upon earth,
to dwell in the midst of blazing fuel,
and, lifted by the hot wind,
to travel to all the ten directions.

This is the thirty seventh chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the Vajra Essence of All Activities.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, in order that this Secret Essence be held firmly, uttered this inspired utterance:

Through union with suchness, the dharmadhātu,
one is blessed as the lord of all buddhas.
Like the colors of kṣa lam a,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion.
Just as when metal is poured into a mold,
that itself is the supreme accomplishment of the path of mind.

Through union that makes suchness clear,
one is blessed as the lord of all buddhas.
Like the colors of oṃ lāṃ a,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion.
Just as when metal is poured into a mold,
