that itself is the supreme accomplishment of the path of form.

Through union of suchness with equality,
one is blessed as the lord of all buddhas.
Like the colors of svāṃ laṃ a,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion.
Just as when metal is poured into a mold,
that itself is the special, supreme path.

Through union that realizes the dharmadhātu,
one is blessed as the lord of all buddhas.
Like the colors of oṃ laṃ a,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion.
Just as when metal is poured into a mold,
that itself is the supreme accomplishment of the three paths.

Through union with the activity of the dharmadhātu,
one is blessed as the lord of all buddhas.
Like the host of hāṃ laṃ a,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion.
Just as when metal is poured into a mold,
that is the primordial wisdom of enlightened activity.

From that, meditate on the samādhi of illusion.
The supreme maṇḍala of the Net itself:
meditate on the mudrā of the ten-pronged family.
That itself is ha and kṣaḥ;
meditate on the deities of the host of mūṃ.
From the supreme illusion of the buddhas
comes the manifestly perfect maṇḍala of the Net.
That itself holds in hand the path of hūṃ,
fully adorned with the ten-pronged ornament.
Or else, the five-pronged mind of minds:
from the center, Locanā of hūṃ;
binding the mudrā of holding the hook,
three times with hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ
bless your own body, speech and mind.
Urge on the deities of the host of mūṃ.
Bind the mudrā of the five syllables and bless.
By brandishing the generation through to completion,
draw the maṇḍala of the forty two.
Through pacifying and enriching three times,
the rites one desires are accomplished.
The tenth, the fierce one, deep dark blue,
brandishing with a haughty dance,
stamping the dance to the sweet sound of speech,
when joined in the eight mudrās,
brandishing with phaṭ phaṭ hūṃ,
holding in hand the emblem of the heart,
from the king of body, speech and mind,
those who destroy the body of merit come under control,
and he grants the attainment of whatever is wished and hoped.
Adorned and graceful messengers,
Brahmā, Indra, and the Lord of Desire,
in short, the lords of the twelve continents,
the haughty ones of fierce power,
spellbound by mantra, faint and lose their wits,
and will offer the excellent vase.
Adorned goddesses and nāginīs,
horses and elephants for mounts,
dwellings, supports and clothing,
the jewel that rests upon their crowns,
whatever is wished, whatever the mind conceives, they offer.
They will act toward him like servants.
All of his needs
will be accomplished, entirely and without exception.

This is the thirty eighth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, Holding the Great Illusion Firmly, the Source of Nectar.

Then the Tathāgata, from the Net of Illusion, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called Taming Beings through Compassion, the Source of Nectar, and uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! With regard to phenomena pure from the beginning,
wrong conceptions are established in two kinds.
All things are born from the five elements.
From what is wholly pure and stainless,
discordant conceptions arise, each apart.
What is medicine for some is poison for others;
there is nothing that has not become medicine.
Everything without exception, from a single cause,
appears separately with discordant distinctions.
No one made them; who is there to make them?
Each is bound by the noose of conception.
All suffering without exception, the machinery of delusion,
the three realms and the four inversions,
the three times and all five classes of beings,
all afflictions and all sufferings, it purifies.
It brings the view of liberation to completion.
The ten grounds are completed, and the ground of buddhahood is completed too.
Therefore this medicine should be prepared,
since it is a medicine that benefits all.
Therefore do not cling to "I" and self.
Cast off the notions of clean and defiled.
Practice everything as equality.
Realize the root, the mind of awakening itself.
Through skillful means, practice the perfections.
Through compassion, let nothing go to waste.
The supreme nectar is the sphere of equality.
The meaning of nectar is the absence of birth and death.
The abode of nectar is the mind of awakening.
The action of nectar is to purify conceptions.
It gathers one's own glory and accomplishes the welfare of beings.
The blessing of the medicine is inconceivable.
It purifies the lower realms and accomplishes the higher realms.
Like the elixir that turns things to gold, and like the dispeller of darkness,
it accomplishes various other aims as well.
Poisons arising from conditions,
root poisons and compounded poisons,
nāga poison, poison sent by sorcery, and the rest:
through the blessing of great compassion,
in order to subdue all poisons,
meditate on the maṇḍala, whole and complete.
In the five places dissolve the five syllables,
oṃ hūṃ trāṃ hrīḥ a.
Or else do it with the letter āṃ.
Or else send it forth from the letter hrīḥ.
Transform the poisons into nectar.
Let them dissolve into the sphere of equality.
a hoḥ: this is in the secret words of the vajra.

hūṃ, by nature the vajra of aversion,
through compassion subdues the self of conception;
it joins "I" and selflessness in the sphere,
and is endowed with a mind of loving-kindness.
oṃ, by nature great delusion,
is a state of clear rays of wisdom light,
of the wheel from which all things arise.
trāṃ, by nature pride, is quality:
through compassion it increases the welfare of beings;
it joins the great peaceful mind in the sphere,
an immeasurable ocean of qualities.
hrīḥ, by nature desire, is supreme speech:
it purifies afflictions and karma.
Afflictions and sufferings are completely purified;
the mind free of attachment is awakening.
a, by nature the Karmasattva,
accomplishes all aims without exception,
the supreme tamer of all wrong conceptions,
radiating through the ten directions and four times.
mūṃ, by nature wisdom and luminosity,
dispels laziness and distraction;
arising from swift compassion,
it is unobstructed everywhere among the victors.
It purifies afflictions and karma,
and brings great relief as well.
The purifier of poisons is the three bodies;
the purifier of the five paths is the five wisdoms;
the weapon of wisdom cuts saṃsāra at its root.
Gathered into hūṃ and scattered by hoḥ,
burned by raṃ and hurled away by phaṭ,
let them dissolve into the sphere of the drop
in which skillful means and wisdom are joined.
Then, having become the nondual sphere,
meditate on the body of the wisdom being.
Meditate on one syllable, three syllables, five syllables.
All the forty two without exception
and the buddha maṇḍala, meditate on them complete.
With no "I" and no self, partake of it as the mudrā.
Then meditate on all the five enjoyments
as the five wisdoms, the five families,
the five goddesses and the five medicines.

By reciting oṃ hūṃ trāṃ hrīḥ aḥ oṃ vajra amṛte kuṇḍali hana hana hūṃ phaṭ oṃ viśuddhe amṛte hūṃ phaṭ oṃ dharma amṛte hūṃ phaṭ oṃ āḥ hūṃ, everything becomes pure. It becomes nectar.

Earth, water, fire, wind and space:
in order to analyze them as without intrinsic nature,
divide each one into five,
and dissolve them into the sphere of mūṃ.
Upon those great elements
generate the superior samādhi.
Awareness is the mudrā of skillful means:
meditate on them as the five tathāgatas,
and the characteristic of each element
should be meditated on as the five mothers.
Their family is the five wisdoms themselves.

Taken as the cause, form and the rest, the forty two,
all things outer and inner, the vessel and its contents,
within the very sphere of suchness,
all that has thus come about and been born,
what need is there to say it, are that itself.
a hoḥ How wonderful! Through the superior blessing,
meditate that the deities are gathered into yourself.
Then, being nondual with the deity,
yourself and the letters oṃ and mūṃ,
the two and the self, dissolve into the sphere.
Meditate on yourself as Amṛtakuṇḍalin,
his body's color like the sun rising on snow,
upon a throne seat of moon and lotus,
having a throne seat of a thousand suns,
surrounded by the hosts of gods and goddesses,
together with the maṇḍala of wrathful gods and goddesses,
beautified by the mudrās of the sacred places and lands,
surrounded by hosts of attendants and handmaids.
a ho A wheel of four spokes with its rim:
in the center, Amṛtakuṇḍalin;
on the eastern spoke, Vajra;
on the southern spoke, Ratna;
on the western spoke, Amitābha;
on the northern spoke, Amoghasiddhi.
Meditate on them as endowed with skillful means and wisdom.
Meditate clearly on the mudrās as well.
Recite the essence syllables and words clearly.
There, the mudrā of nectar
is a full vase, blazing like a wish-fulfilling jewel.
It fulfills hopes just as one wishes,
it dispels afflictions and sufferings,
it endows one with the qualities of the five sense pleasures:
it is the great, unsurpassed accomplishment.
It makes nectar fall like rain.
Together with the mudrās of the four gates,
the very cause of amṛta is
the mind of awakening and the great flesh,
urine and excrement together with maṃsa,
padma rakta and the like,
and other various beneficial substances as well.
Whether separating them or not separating them,
bless them as nectar itself.
In order to purify the habitual tendencies of conception,
purify them beyond clean and unclean.
Dissolve them once again into the sphere.
Meditate on the mudrā blazing with rays of light.
It dispels the four hundred and four diseases,
frees one from the three poisons and from suffering,
lets one live for many long aeons,
and one will attain the great accomplishment:
it is truly said to complete the two accumulations.
Having yourself become the letter hūṃ,
meditate that all things endowed with the five enjoyments
become oṃ āḥ hūṃ.
Light radiates and blazes, emanating to the ten directions;
gathered back again, the light radiates and blazes as nectar.
Meditate one-pointedly, with perfect clarity.
Moreover, seal it with the host of letters.
If it is drunk, the obscurations of the lower realms are purified.
tra ta Relied on constantly, it accomplishes even the supreme accomplishment.
It dispels the four hundred and four diseases.
If one constantly makes offerings of it to the deity,
the deity becomes nondual with oneself.
If one makes a torma of it for the hostile side,
even the fierce and violent become gentle.
Moreover, all accomplishments
are achieved just as one intends.
Since one attains the state of buddhahood,
what need is there to mention gods, spirits and the rest?
Practice without doubt.
Practice constantly and dissolve into the sphere.

This is the thirty ninth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, from the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion, on Accomplishing Medicine.

Then Great Delight uttered this inspired utterance called The Arising of Their Blessings:

Here, the sacred offering
is the forty five great magical displays themselves,
abiding in the constituents and the elements.
That itself is illusion, skilled in means;
through the Net, body, speech and mind are manifestly perfect.
All without exception are mudrā itself,
having become the support of the supreme dharmakāya:
meditate on them clearly, filling the ten directions.
That itself, filling the whole path of speech,
meditate on fully as the host of hūṃ.
Dissolve the body itself into the host of hā.
Taking up the form of the one to be acted upon,
with powdered pigment of black color,
enter yourself into his heart,
and reciting the mantra called jaḥ,
drive him with iron to go swiftly.
Keener of scent and swifter than before,
he carries out whatever task is assigned.
If you wish to come forth from his body,
take up your own form through pacifying,
and gathering slowly with hūṃ,
you will enter into your own body.

All without exception are mudrā itself,
having become the support of the supreme dharmakāya:
meditate on them clearly, filling the ten directions.
That itself, filling the path of speech and the body,
meditate on intently as the host of oṃ.
Dissolve the body itself into the host of aḥ.
Taking up the form of the one to be acted upon,
with powdered pigment of white color,
enter yourself into his heart,
and reciting the mantra called hūṃ,
he becomes a great king honored by many,
of sharp intelligence, wise mind and fair face,
his enjoyments gather more than before,
and he becomes as one who delights to remain.
If you wish to come forth from his body,
take up your own form through pacifying,
and gathering slowly with oṃ,
you will be brought into your own body.

All without exception are mudrā itself,
having become the support of the supreme dharmakāya:
meditate on them clearly, filling all directions.
That itself, filling the whole path of speech,
meditate on thoroughly as the host of svāṃ.
Dissolve the body itself into the host of svāṃ.
Taking up the form of the one to be acted upon,
with powdered pigment of yellow color,
enter yourself into his heart,
and reciting the mantra called svāṃ,
a minister of great wealth and wise mind,
of sharp intelligence, gathers enjoyments more than before;
enjoying them among his retinue,
he becomes as one who delights to remain.
If you wish to come forth from his body,
take up your own form through enriching,
and slowly saying svāṃ,
you will enter into your own body.

All without exception are mudrā itself,
having become the support of the supreme dharmakāya:
filling the ten directions, meditate on them clear and blazing.
Filling those and the body,
meditate on them intently as the host of āṃḥ.
Dissolve the body itself into the host of āṃḥ.
Taking up the form of the one to be acted upon,
with powdered pigment of red color,
enter yourself into his heart,
and reciting the mantra called āṃḥ,
queens, ministers of the maternal clan and the mighty
come under your power just as you wish.
Enjoying among his retinue,
he becomes as one who delights to remain.
If you wish to come forth from his form,
take up your own form through magnetizing,
and slowly saying āṃḥ,
you will enter into your own body.

All without exception are mudrā itself,
having become the support of the supreme dharmakāya:
meditate on them clearly, filling all directions.
That itself, filling the path of speech and the body,
meditate on intently as the host of hāṃ.
Dissolve the body itself into the host of hāṃ.
Taking up the form of the one to be acted upon,
with powdered pigment of green color,
enter yourself into his heart,
and reciting the mantra called hāṃ,
the great family of activity takes whatever form is desired,
with many troops, mighty forces, and fine armor and weapons.
Those full of aversion will be subdued,
those full of desire will gather,
and the greatly proud will bow down.
He will uphold his teaching.
Enjoying among his retinue,

he becomes as one who delights to remain.
If you wish to come forth from his body,
evenly take up the wrathful form,
and slowly saying haṃ,
you arise in an invisible form
and enter into your own body.
In islands or in charnel grounds,
through my wish, by the Lord of Dharma,
one holds the supreme maṇḍala of illusion,
and the maṇḍala of the Net arises completely.

This is the fortieth chapter of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, The Arising of the Blessings of All the Tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One, the glorious Samantabhadra, in order to show his own great qualities, for the sake of sentient beings, brought forth from the vajra of body, speech and mind this sphere of suchness:

The mother of the supreme family of glory itself:
on the image into which the deity is to be brought down,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, imagine it as mūṃ.
Through familiarity with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of mūṃ.
From that very freedom from extremes, from non-apprehension,
holding to that moonlike manner,
meditate on Buddha Vajra canaka
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
Entering the path, fill her body.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Likewise meditate on the families as oṃ.
The vessel and its abode are filled with bliss,
and the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
contemplate them in accordance with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast āḥ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of wish-fulfilling letters.
When the mudrā is perfectly clear,
this is proclaimed to be close approach.
Radiating and gathering in the number fitting the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind the mudrā of the supreme hook.
By that, things become just as you wish.

Body and speech by nature, unconditioned,
not dependent on another, the vajra family,
the glorious mother, Vajra Illusion,
proclaimed thoroughly by the Dharma:
for one who wishes for clarity and is to be made to speak,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed into the five syllables, imagine it as lāṃ.
Through familiarity with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of lāṃ.
From union with Buddhalocanā,
all the victors bestow blessing.
From that very freedom from extremes, from non-apprehension,
having the form of the supreme nature,
meditate on Buddha Vajra canaka
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
From that, blessed as the vajra,
the secret lotus is the means of entry.
Entering the sheath, fill her body.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Meditate on the vajra families as hūṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with bliss,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
contemplate them intently in accordance with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast hā,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of wish-fulfilling letters.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in the number fitting the family;
in meditation, recitation and direct presence,
it is taught to urge on with the gaze of the hook.
With the mudrā of passion,
meditate on radiating and gathering in accordance with the family.

Treasure of qualities, of body and speech, wish-fulfilling,
the glorious, supreme great lady of Illusion:
the image, dressed and adorned,
on tree saplings and the like,
performing the supreme act of bringing down,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, imagine it as oṃ.
Through familiarity with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of oṃ.
From union with the buddha of fire,
all the victors bestow blessing.
From that very freedom from extremes, from non-apprehension,
having the form of a treasury of qualities,
meditate on Buddha Ratna canaka
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
From that, blessed as the vajra,
the secret lotus is the means of entry.
Entering into the sheath, fill her body.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Meditate on the jewel families as svāṃ.
The vessel and its abode are filled with bliss,
and the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
contemplate them intently in accordance with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast svāṃ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of wish-fulfilling letters.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind with the supreme noose of samaya.
With the four mudrās of passion,
meditate on gathering and radiating in accordance with the family.
Healing and killing, defeat and victory,
going elsewhere and staying,
coming at will, or speaking, as you please;
from many weapons and terrors,
from acts of binding and from weapons, one is freed.

The glorious mother of the supreme lotus,
holding the form of great bliss, the lord of dance:
the image into which she is to be brought down,
on tree saplings and the like.
First bless the five places;
then meditate on wisdom in the five places.
Mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
imagine them as vaṃ, the seed of the four activities.
Through familiarity with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of vaṃ.
From union with the buddha of water,
the vajra speeches bestow blessing.
From that very freedom from extremes, from non-apprehension,
having the form of vajra speech,
meditate on Buddha Great Speech canaka
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
From that, blessed as the vajra,
the secret lotus is the means of entry.
Entering into the sheath, fill her body.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Summon with the name together with the essence.
Meditate on the vajra speeches as oṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with bliss,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
contemplate them intently in accordance with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast oṃ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of letters of the Lord of Dharma.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind with the supreme chain of passion.
With the four mudrās of great passion,
meditate on them as in accord with the family.
Pacifying, magnetizing, wrathful and enriching:
whatever one wishes, these are highly praised.

The glorious lady of Illusion, Accomplishment:
the image into which she is to be brought down,
on the best and middling trees.
Mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, imagine it as hāṃ.
That is the host of e.
Through familiarity with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of fire.

Those are the deities above.
From selflessness, freedom from extremes, non-apprehension,
holding to that manner of Amoghasiddhi,
meditate on the supreme canaka of Amoghasiddhi
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
From that, blessed as the crossed vajra,
the secret lotus is the means of entry.
Entering into the sheath, fill her body.
Filled with the colors of the crossed vajra,
summon the names too with their respective essences.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Meditate on the vajra deities as hāṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with bliss,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
meditate on them as in accord with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast hāṃ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of accomplished illusion.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
delighting with the melodious speech of dance,
with the four mudrās of passion,
meditate on them as in accord with the family.
Going to the directions and snatching away,
cutting short life, and the activities,
the supreme power of saying whatever one wishes directly:
one sports with the mother of the family.
Exalted beyond existence, nirvāṇa
will be accomplished through this samādhi.

When this had been said, he knew the host of ripenings to be the very great qualities of the tathāgatas. Having known this, he brought forth from the sphere of suchness, the vajra of body, speech and mind, these vajra secret words:

a hoḥ What is to be thought, once it has been thought,
is not grasped by thought; it is inconceivable.
Beyond thought, spontaneously present, unmoving, immeasurable, inconceivable.
The very sphere known as the condition joined with the cause is inexhaustible.
Wondrous! The path of existence, free of extremes, without reference:
that itself is the wheel of supreme qualities, the supreme path.
a hoḥ So it is in the vajra secret words.

This is the forty first chapter, called The Blessed One, the Glorious Samantabhadra, Showing Great Qualities from the Sphere of Suchness.

Then the Blessed One, the glorious Samantabhadra, brought forth from the vajra of body, speech and mind this rite, together with its ritual procedure, called Blessing the Maṇḍala of Speech of the Great Illusion That Shows Great Qualities:

The maṇḍalas of the supreme bringing down,
with their colors and numbers, without confusion:
to one of knowledge, of great intellect and broad mind,
who does not weary of practicing the profound,
keeping to proper food and clothing, give the samaya food.
Cool the disciples with water in a conch vessel.
Teach the samādhi suited to be a support,
and teach empowerment and the supreme accomplishment as well.
Impart the secret samaya of silence.
Having shown the mind of oṃ and mūṃ,
in secret, purify from the five signs.
The forty two maṇḍalas of illusion:
join them with the aggregates, elements and sense bases.
Make the offering endowed with the five enjoyments.
Recite the great speech, the vajra path of speech:

oṃ sarva tathāgata jīna oṃ oṃ vajra kula hūṃ vajra oṃ sarva tathāgata svāṃ ratna svāṃ oṃ padma tathā hṛdaya ārolik hā prajñā hāṃ dhātvīśvari mūṃ dveṣarati lāṃ moharati āṃ rāgarati pāṃ vajrarati tāṃ mūṃ mūṃ mūṃ lāṃ lāṃ lāṃ āṃḥ āṃḥ āṃḥ pāṃ pāṃ pāṃ tāṃ tāṃ tāṃ e vaṃ ma ya va va va va va jra jra jra jra jra sa sa sa sa sa ma ma ma ma ma ya ya ya ya ya stvaṃ hoḥ

When this was said, throughout the boundless ten directions of the six worlds, the supreme of all samayas, the very essence of the body, speech and heart of all the tathāgatas, appeared everywhere as the very nature of the great Dhātvīśvarī, the great Locanā, the great Māmakī, the great Pāṇḍaravāsinī, and the samaya noble lady, the great Tārā.

This is the forty second chapter, called The Wheel of Speech for Bringing Down, the Essence of the Vajra of Body, Speech and Mind of All the Tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One, the glorious Samantabhadra, entered into equipoise in the samaya called Gathering the Blessings of the Great Illusion, and brought forth from the vajra Net of Illusion of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas:

Gathering the blessings of Lāsyā, the offering goddess,
the one who shows form, who is to be brought down:
on an image in accord with the family,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, bestow ya itself.
That itself is the lotus maṇḍala.
Familiar with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of ya.
Those are the deities of hūṃ:
from selflessness, freedom from extremes, non-apprehension,
holding to their own manner,
meditate on Buddha Vajra canaka
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
From that, blessed as the vajra,
the secret lotus is the means of entry.
Entering into the sheath, fill her body.
Filled with the colors of the vajra,
call out the names too with their respective essences.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Summon with the name together with the essence.
Meditate on the vajra deities as hūṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with Lāsyās,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
contemplate them as in accord with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast hā,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of Buddha Vajras.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind the mudrā of the four gatekeeper goddesses.
With the four mudrās of passion,
meditate on them as in accord with the family.

Gathering the blessings of the Jewel Garland goddess,
the one who shows form, who is to be brought down:
on an image in accord with the family,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, bestow e itself.
That itself is the lotus maṇḍala.
Familiar with that, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of e.
Those are the deities of svāṃ:
from selflessness, freedom from extremes, non-apprehension,
holding to their own manner,
meditate on Buddha Ratna canaka
as joined with the mother, Prajñā.
From that, blessed as the vajra,
the secret lotus is the means of entry.
Entering into the sheath, fill her body.
Filled with the colors of the jewel,
summon the names too with their respective essences.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, emanate and dissolve.
Meditate on the jewel deities as svāṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with Garland goddesses,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
contemplate them as in accord with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast svāṃ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of Buddha Ratnas.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind the mudrā of the four gatekeeper goddesses.

With the four mudrās of passionate attachment,
meditate in accord with the family.
The gathering-in of the blessing of the lotus expanse,
causing it to descend upon a likeness, is this:
on an image in accord with the family,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart.
Blessed by the five syllables, imagine it as baṃ.
Meditate on that very thing as the lotus maṇḍala.
Growing familiar with it, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of baṃ.
These are the deities of oṃ:
from selfless, extreme-free non-objectification,
holding to that natural state of one's own,
meditate on Lotus buddhas the size of chickpeas
as united with the mother, Prajñā.
Then, blessed as vajra,
this is the means of entering through the secret lotus:
having entered the sheath, fill that body,
filling it with the host of the lotus,
and with their individual essence mantras summon even humans.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, radiate and dissolve;
meditate on the lotus deities as oṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with Song goddesses,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
meditate on them as in accord with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast hāṃ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of Buddha Ratnas.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind the mudrā of the four gatekeeper goddesses.
With the four mudrās of passionate attachment,
meditate in accord with the family.

The gathering-in of the blessing of the dancers of the Karma family,
causing it to descend upon a likeness, is this:
on an image in accord with the family,
mark the crown, the mouth and the heart.
Blessed by the five syllables, bestow the syllable ma itself.
Meditate on that very thing as the lotus maṇḍala.
Growing familiar with it, marking the secret place,
meditate on the deities of the host of ma.
These are the deities of hāṃ:
from selfless, extreme-free non-objectification,
holding to that natural state of one's own,
meditate on Lotus buddhas the size of chickpeas
as united with the mother, Prajñā.
Then, blessed as vajra,
this is the means of entering through the secret lotus:
having entered the sheath, fill that body,
filling it with the host of karma deities,
and with their individual essence mantras summon even humans.
Familiar with that, the mind blissful and clear, radiate and dissolve;
meditate on the karma deities as hāṃ.
With the vessel and its abode filled with Dancer goddesses,
the host of mothers too abides clearly.
The mudrā, the essence, the form and the rest:
meditate on them as in accord with the family.
Like the radiating and gathering of the steadfast hāṃ,
imagine the consciousness in the state of blessing.
From the very mind without ground or root,
meditate on clouds of Buddha Karmas.
The mudrā made clear is close approach;
radiating and gathering in accordance with the family,
having meditated, when recitation is directly present,
bind the mudrā of the four gatekeeper goddesses.
With the four mudrās of passionate attachment,
meditate in accord with the family.

The deities of the forty-two maṇḍalas appearing manifestly,
the many gatekeepers, male and female,
the supreme goddesses of the four times caused to descend,
together with the hosts of gods and nāgas:
it is fitting to make them speak manifestly.
Kingdoms and the seizing of retinues,
gathering and bringing under control,
slaying and expelling, with all the rest,
oneself going and arriving swiftly,
the wealth one desires, and whatever is to be done:
all this is accomplished by this samādhi.
At no fixed time, one meets with offerings.

When this was spoken, all the ten directions of the six worlds became a great offering of the gathering-in of blessings.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the forty-third chapter, called "The Compassion of the Net of Illusion, which Gathers In Blessings and Causes the Samaya to Descend."

Then Great Delight entered the samādhi called the Vajra Essence of Great Enjoyment, and brought forth this, called "The Enjoyment of the Samaya Offerings of the Great Illusion," from the vajra Net of Illusion of the body, speech and mind of the tathāgatas.

What those of superior, middling and lesser intellect
should definitely meditate upon is this:
one who desires the foremost of royal Earth-Holders,
on a drawn image or on a support,
should mark the crown, the mouth and the heart,
and habitually meditate on jaḥ, red in color.
Familiar with that, draw it inward through the secret place,
and hold one's own body as red in color, in that state.
Mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, imagine it as baṃ.
Familiar with that, meditate on the maṇḍala of dance.
Saying jaḥ, bind the mudrā and draw them in,
and habitually, in the manner of entering through the secret place,
gather them by way of the path into the Lord of Speech.
From oṃ, meditate on buddhas the size of chickpeas
filling the vessel, the abode and the maṇḍala.
Through habitual repetition of the name and the essence mantra, summon.
When it has become directly and clearly present,
array the offerings of passionate attachment
marked by the hook and the rest.
When habituation in the mind is clear,
the one toward whom it is done comes under control.

For ministerial Earth-Holders, array the offerings.
When habituation in the mind is clear,
mark the mouth and heart of the one toward whom it is done,
and habitually meditate on jaḥ, red in color.
Familiar with that, draw it inward to the secret place,
and hold one's own body as red in color, in that state.
Mark the crown, the mouth and the heart;
blessed by the five syllables, imagine it as baṃ.
Familiar with that, meditate on the maṇḍala of dance.
Saying jaḥ, bind the mudrā and draw them in,
and habitually, in the manner of entering the secret place,
gather them by way of the path into the supreme of speech.
From āṃḥ, meditate on buddhas the size of chickpeas
filling the vessel, the abode and the maṇḍala.
Through habitual repetition of the name and the essence mantra, summon.
When it has become directly and clearly present,
array the offerings of passionate attachment
marked by the hook and the rest.
When the task is assigned to it,
the one toward whom it is done comes under control.

Goddesses, nāginīs, and women of low caste,
those whose desires are accomplished for a moment or for always:
all this is accomplished by this samādhi.
Upon Īśvara, the Great God (Śiva), Indra lord of the gods,
the great kings and the many guardians of the directions,
their wives, daughters and the rest are marked;
those whose minds are set on mastering sensual enjoyment:
all this is accomplished by this samādhi.
All their aims, without exception,
will be completely and perfectly accomplished.

When this was spoken, all the ten directions of the six worlds shook violently and became completely luminous, and the great enjoyment of the tathāgatas appeared everywhere.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, the vajra nature of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, this is the forty-fourth chapter, called "The Enjoyment of the Samaya Offerings of the Great Illusion."

Then the Great Sage, the Blessed One, appeared to the inconceivable host of nāgas as the great king, the Buddha, the Blessed One himself. Having tamed the host of nāgas of malicious deeds, for the sake of their devotion to the great accomplishment, he spoke these words.

In a place filled with inconceivable hosts of nāgas, of the second class, of the third class, of various classes, and those made into a retinue from the second direction, apart from one's own greater or lesser approach practice, whatever one does to please the mind will not succeed, and they will create obstacles. Therefore this, called the Fifth, the Removal of Obstacles, came forth, and it was spoken for this purpose.

So that one who sits in practice is not unsettled,
for mastering the ground as its owner,
for whatever substances of practice there may be,
for removing venomous snakes,
so that those belonging to the retinue, many or few,
are not seized by the malicious ones,
and so that malicious ones do not dwell
in trees, saplings and the like,
it is highly praised as the Fifth.
So that I may be honored by the malicious ones,
from the samādhi of the Net of Illusion,
oneself, dark blue, the color of the malicious ones,
holding in the hands a noose and an utpala,
upon the head the hoods of ten great snakes,
twenty, a hundred, a thousand or ten thousand,
countless great snakes,
black in color, with three blazing tongues.
Because the hosts of malicious nāgas are many,
with a great retinue, terrifying and hair-raising,
one should form this supreme mudrā.
When the syllables of Akṣobhya and Ratna
are separated and made fitting,
the malicious snakes will spread themselves out beneath.
Say: nāga pukala nāga phaṭ.
Whoever strives in this habitually,
if he takes up this guise of mine,
like a heap amid a mass of flames,
as the great king of great kings,
appearing clearly to all,
they will flee helplessly,
and become like servants to him.

Making oneself owner of the ground is this:
first, on ground that is perfectly level,
fill it with hosts of oṃ and muṃ.
From the maṇḍala of the jewel palace,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the entering host of deities.
Then, spreading the wings wide,
clap the vajra palms fiercely.
oṃ vajra krodha duna mune dunā gaccha
Then utterly destroy the syllables.
Then raise up the final syllable.
Then, the syllable that generates magical display:
taking up this guise of mine,
meditate on the maṇḍala of the level ground.
They become utterly terrified
and flee in all ten directions,
and become like servants to him.

Those belonging to the retinue are my retinue.
However many in a great retinue perform the supreme,
because many malicious ones work harm,
set fire well to the substances,
and it comes forth as my essence mantra is recited.
If one recites the six syllables for them,
the malicious ones cannot harm them,
and will honor and revere them,
and their illnesses will be healed.
For an image appearing directly,
for those of supreme, lesser and middling kind
to whom the hosts of malicious ones cling:
at the heart are the syllables aṃ,
red in color, within a blaze,
and all that appears as form abides there.
Having meditated on that state of the Sage,
imagine the letters of the jewel syllable.
Upon it, stretch out in great terror.
The first and last letters are the eyes,
the others blazing as a host of aṃ.
Akṣobhya himself, below, holds a club,
equipped with a skull staff.
Through mindfulness of the mantra upon the tongue,
empowered by all the victors,
the malicious nāgas will be tamed,
and flee in all ten directions,
and make offerings to him and become his servants.
oṃ mune padma samayogi hūṃ hūṃ jaja
Whoever strives in this habitually,
taking up this guise of mine,
will make offerings to those worthy of honor,
and all accomplishments will come swiftly.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the forty-fifth chapter, the Taming of the Malicious Deeds of the Hosts of Nāgas by the Great Sage, the Blessed One.

Then the Blessed One, the glorious Samantabhadra, spoke this utterance for the sake of this, called "Overpowering the Hosts of Bhūtas."

From the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion,
meditate on the deities of the two clusters.
Having arranged the place where I abide,
for a year, or for three or five months,
with a great vajra conch that is filled,
holding the vajra in hand, and the bell,
approach through mantra recitation and habituation in samādhi,
as dye changes the color of a substance,
until the signs become manifest.
Buddha Vajra, holding the vajra in hand,
transforming his own nature
into the great wrathful one, Vijaya,
eyes glaring, fangs bared, hateful eyes blazing,
legs extended and bent, menacing:
meditate on him upon the field.
When it becomes violently trembling,
recite his essence mantra:
that is the completion of his approach.
The signs of accomplishment in this way are these:
before whoever is the object,
looking with averted face and eyes,
say "phaṭ" fiercely upon the tongue.
They will faint; from aṃ let red elixir descend.
From that, the mind called hūṃ,
when it gathers into its first place,
if one wishes them to leap up, they will rise.

From the maṇḍala of the Net of Illusion,
meditate on the deities of the two clusters.
Having arranged the place where I abide,
for a year, or for three, five or seven months,
with his great conch that is filled,
holding the vajra in hand, and the bell,
approach through mantra recitation and habituation in samādhi,
as dye changes the color of a substance,
until the signs become manifest.
Buddha Vajra, holding the vajra in hand,
as the great wrathful one, Yama the King,
joined with the queen of wrathful Yamas,
transforming his own nature into that,
eyes glaring, fangs bared, with hateful eyes,
legs extended and bent, menacing:
meditate on him upon the field.
When it becomes violently trembling,
reciting his essence mantra as well,
cut at the waist with oṃ,
and meditate that thereby they come under control.
Earth into gold, water into earth,
just as is pleasing to the mind,
in whatever way, it will be accomplished.
From that, it is certain, according to one's wish:
that is the completion of his approach.
The signs of certainty in this way are these:
in the presence of the name, the form, or the person directly,
shaking the head, look with fierce, hateful eyes.
At the tip of oṃ say "phaṭ".
They will faint; from oṃ let red elixir descend.
From speech, by means of what is called aṃ,
together with the recitation, dissolve that into oneself.
Then it is certain, according to one's wish:
that is the practice of his approach.
The signs of certainty in this way are these:
in the presence of the name, the form, or the person directly,
looking with averted face and eyes,
by saying "phaṭ" fiercely from oṃ,
they will faint; from aṃ let red elixir descend.
From that comes what is called hūṃ.
When it is gathered into its first place,
if one wishes them to live, they will rise.

The manifest awakening of the Net of Illusion,
the whole essence of supreme awakening,
the mudrā, the duration of abiding and the rest,
the samādhi of the single part:
with a great vajra conch that is filled,
holding the vajra in hand, and the bell,
approach through mantra recitation and habituation in samādhi,
as dye changes the color of a substance,
until the signs become manifest.

Buddha Vajra, holding the vajra in hand,
transforming his own nature
into the great wrathful one, glorious Paramāśva,
eyes glaring, fangs bared, with hateful eyes,
legs extended and bent, menacing:
meditate on him upon the field.
When it becomes violently trembling,
reciting his essence mantra as well,
cut at the waist by means of oṃ,
and meditate that thereby they come under control.
Earth into gold, water into earth,
just as is pleasing to the mind,
in whatever way, it is certain to be accomplished;
it comes about for him according to his wish:
that is the completion of his approach.
The signs of his accomplishment are these:
in the presence of the name, the form, or the person directly,
shaking the head, look with fierce, hateful eyes.
By saying "phaṭ" fiercely from aṃ,
they will faint; from oṃ let red elixir descend.
From that, the mind called hūṃ,
when it is gathered into its first place,
if one wishes them to leap up, they will rise.

From that very Great Illusion,
Buddha Vajra, holding the vajra in hand,
transforming his own nature
into the great wrathful one, Amṛtakuṇḍalin,
eyes glaring, fangs bared, with hateful eyes,
legs extended and bent, menacing:
meditate on him upon the field.
When it has become violently trembling,
and goes swiftly off in some direction,
reciting his supreme essence mantra,
urging it on with hūṃ,
make it go back to its first place.
Meditate that thereby they come under control.
From that, whatever various things one desires,
each and every one is accomplished:
that is the completion of his approach.
By saying "phaṭ" fiercely at oṃ,
having visualized the one toward whom it is done,
by means of a snap of crossed fingers,
they will faint; from oṃ let red elixir descend.
By meditating in the mind, they will rise.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the forty-sixth chapter, the Arrangement of the Samaya Offerings that Make the Great Illusion Utterly Victorious.

Then, in the presence of the Blessed One, Great Delight, the great haughty ones, Maheśvara (Śiva), the Four Great Kings, and those who had become guardians of the directions, together with their commanders and wives, with their servants, attendants and sub-attendants, with minds filled with great terror, respectfully made a pledge, and also spoke with one voice their respective maṇḍalas and these, called their essence mantras:

"The offering of the maṇḍala of the haughty ones and their retinue,
and of our body, speech and mind, is this:
our retinue and attendants, six hundred million,
dwell in places of fresh and old skeletons,
feast on heaps of moist flesh and blood,
drink from skull cups and eat without limit;
with short spears, swords and axes,
nooses, clubs and hurled weapons,
drums and ḍamarus, scattering blood,
they are surrounded by all the hosts of the malicious.
Whoever merely holds the names
of these maṇḍalas of delight,
meditates on this at the soles of the feet,
and recites his own essence mantra,
we will act as servants to him.
Whatever his need may be,
we will accomplish it completely, without exception.
Having sat down, offer the essence mantra thus:
khaṃ dhu ha svāhā

"The offering of the maṇḍala of Īśvara, the Great God (Śiva),
and of our body, speech and mind, is this:
our retinue and attendants, eighty thousand koṭis,
with scattered flowers strewn about,
with many layered cushions of cloth,
with jewels laid out and drums beaten,
music, song and dance most melodious;
in the abodes of jeweled houses
we enjoy many goddesses,
we drink from jeweled vessels,
covered above by spreading parasols;
we appear in beautiful forms;
precious flowers and the like,
and flowers of the gods, we scatter in all directions,
surrounded by all the many hosts of gods.
Whoever merely holds the names
of these maṇḍalas of delight,
draws this at the soles of the feet,
and recites his own essence mantra,
we will act as servants to him,
place him on our crowns and honor him;
whatever his need may be,
we will accomplish it completely, without exception;
we will show him our forms and appear before him.
oṃ sarva devā mahā upaha

"King Vaiśravaṇa and Dhṛtarāṣṭra,
with our many thousands of hosts of retinue,
offer our body, speech and mind thus:
our retinue and attendants, a hundred koṭis of thousands,
with scattered flowers strewn about,
many ornaments and ointments spread upon the ground;
our commanders, twenty koṭis in number,
proclaim whatever is excellent;
with great ceremony and respectful reverence,
we make offerings in the proper manner:
such are we, together with our retinue.
With jewels laid out and with drinks,
adorned with melodious music, song and dance,
I dwell in jeweled houses,
with mounts and many troops all around.
Drinking beer from jeweled vessels,
covered above with adorning parasols,
we appear in beautiful forms;
we scatter precious flowers in all directions,
surrounded by all the many hosts of kings.
With the lord of the gandharvas and the lord of fire,
with Īśāna and the lord of the wind,
in this way we act with respect.
Whoever merely holds the names
of these maṇḍalas of delight,
having drawn them at the soles of the feet,
and recites his own essence mantra,
we will act as servants to him,
place him on our crowns and honor him;
whatever his need may be,
we will accomplish it completely, without exception;
we will show him our forms and appear before him.
oṃ siddha dhi dhi dhu ho svāhā

"King Virūpākṣa and Virūḍhaka,
with our many tens of thousands of hosts of retinue,
offer our body, speech and mind thus:
our hosts of retinue, a hundred koṭis of tens of thousands,
with scattered flowers strewn about,
many ornaments and ointments spread upon the ground;
our commanders, twenty koṭis in number,
proclaim whatever is excellent;
with great ceremony and respectful reverence,
we make offerings in the proper manner:
such are we, together with our retinue.
With jewels laid out and with drinks,
adorned with melodious music, song and dance,
I dwell in jeweled houses,
with mounts and many troops all around.
Drinking beer from jeweled vessels,
covered above by spreading parasols,
we appear in beautiful forms;
we scatter precious flowers in all directions,
surrounded by all the many hosts of kings.
With the hosts of yakṣas and rākṣasas,
the lord of the waters, and Yama's great retinue,
in this way we act with respect.
Whoever merely holds the names
of this great maṇḍala of delight,
having drawn it at the soles of the feet,
and recites his own essence mantra,
we will act as servants to him,
place him on our crowns and honor him;
whatever his need may be,
we will accomplish it completely, without exception;
we will show him our forms and appear before him.
oṃ dhaka bhidu bhavi śupaśe oṃ hūṃ"

When they had spoken thus with one voice, all that exists, as much as there is, was tamed by this great maṇḍala of mind, and the Blessed One, Great Delight, brought forth this secret vajra word from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity: a ho.

The Tathāgatas, inconceivable, their essence inconceivable,
beyond the scattering and gathering of seeds, so hard to approach,
by a single unerring great action, thoroughly tame us,
and I cannot transgress the command of their praise.
Through the compassion of the mind, for as long as the unerring path itself remains,
to The Secret Essence, Ascertaining, the supreme Illusion itself,
I myself shall bow: what need is there to speak of others?
That itself is the very mind emanated from the mind.
ho: in secret vajra words.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the forty-seventh chapter, the Abodes of Those Who Were Thoroughly Tamed and the Offering of Their Essence Mantras.

Then, in the presence of the Blessed One, the embodiment of Great Delight, the glorious great Heruka, the great haughty ones, Maheśvara (Śiva) and the rest, the host of worldly beings, their minds filled with great longing through the blessing of the Tathāgata, spoke with one voice this song of praise for that purpose:

"How wonderful! Glorious one of great and marvelous magic,
nature of Buddha Vajra,
for whom poison and nectar are no different,
the yogin's mind whose nature is pure,
Samantabhadra, vajra body,
chief of the wrathful kings of the five wisdoms,
blood-drinking, terrifying Heruka,
infinite buddha who tames an ocean of beings,
inconceivable compassion, chief of all the wrathful kings,
most exalted of all blood drinkers, wish-fulfilling treasury,
yellow-black, dark blue-black, green-black, red-black,
and blue-black, body majestic as Mount Meru,
dwelling in the center of the mountain of fire at the end of the eon,
fierce-faced in the midst of heaps of many corpses,
body gathered from merit, inexhaustible vajra,
like the light of sun and moon amid a host of stars,
with great might and haughtiness you crush all hosts of terrors,
displaying excellent, unequalled miracles,
abiding in the manner of the dance of the lord of desire.
Devouring with greed, the faces of mind, body and speech
dry up the endless ocean of blood and crush the spines;
wrathful frowns, drawing out hearts, terrifying, flicker like lightning;
the dark brown hair of a hundred thousand charnel grounds is terrifyingly majestic;
destroyer of saṃsāric thought, flaunting a garland of fresh skulls;
appearing at the summit for the sake of play, you laugh a a ha;
great in yourself, your head is adorned with the victors of complete empowerment;
your brows and wrathful frowns flicker like lightning,
sending forth and gathering in blood drinkers in accord with the directions.
By such emanations you overpower all.
Though terror arrays great hosts of darkness,
by your hateful eyes, blazing vast as sun and moon,
the heaped hosts of terror and suffering are utterly purified.
In wrath your mouth gapes, great teeth bared and blazing,
as if climbing from the peak of existence down to the realms of hell;
you swallow the world at once and cut off the six paths completely.
With the blazing emblems of wisdom held in your hands,
you reduce the three existences, without remainder, to empty atoms.
Sporting with the sounds of the palate, as if slapping beings,
you spread the teaching of the Dharma through all the three realms.
As food you partake of the great supreme vajra blood drinker,
and hold as your hide the fresh skin of the slain perishing aggregates.
Tamer of nāgas, your qualities are adorned with a thousand black snakes;
even the hosts of nāgas with their terrors are pressed beneath your feet.
Tamer of desire, chief of all desire,
great desire, the very body of desire,
you make many retinues into a terrifying retinue.
Tamer of hatred, chief of all hatred,
malicious hatred, body of all hatred,
melting all the hosts of terror with your rays,
utterly majestic, a form terrifying even to the terrifying.
Tamer of delusion, chief of all delusion,
dispeller of deluded minds, body of great delusion,
undivided blood drinker, great armor, steadfast as Mount Meru.
Tamer of pride, chief of all pride,
having slain the hosts of terrors, abode of many heaps of corpses,
legs spread, fangs bared, never at the end of your time of satisfaction,
you rejoice in coming with terror and are fiercely hateful.
Tamer of envy, chief of all envy,
great supreme envy, body of great envy,
gathering all women, dwelling at the center of the maṇḍala,
you slay with phaṭ those on wrong paths who are malicious.
You wear a skirt of human skin to tame the malicious;
your body is adorned with forms of the hosts of terrors, like your body.
Your legs and arms are beyond imagining.
Your great left legs, many, extended, crush the gods and pervade the māras;
your right legs, with many great means, gather power, casting out and drawn in.
Raising and snapping your vajra fingers, you utterly dispel the great sleep.
From the center of great light, great lightning flickers:
you hero unsurpassed in terror,
great lord of medicine who joins foul food with nectar,
one as terrifying as you is rare indeed.
Upon the stains that grow back though washed for eons,
by the blessing of the light of the sunrays of wisdom
and the magical display of the rain of pure compassion,
in an instant, by a mere sign, you purify from delusion and turmoil;
in the maṇḍala of wisdom that blocks the path of death,
compassion dawns that utterly purifies birth.
Treasury of great kindness, without love or hatred,
to the body, speech and mind of Heruka I bow."

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the forty-eighth chapter, called "The Song Remembering Kindness."

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, in order that this king of secret mantra be steadfast and unchanging, and abide in the world realms, spoke this utterance for that purpose to those of the bhūtas: the mistress of mistresses, the great Manurākṣasī, the Manurākṣasīs, Brahmāṇī, Raudrī, Indrāṇī, Vaiṣṇavī, Kaumārī, the Red One, Amṛtā, Śāntī, Daṇḍī, Rākṣasī, Bhakṣasī, Ratī, Rudhiramadī, Ekacāriṇī, Manohārikā, Siddhikarī, the River Goddess, the Slayer, the Human Woman, Vārāhī, Cāmuṇḍā, the Noseless One, Varuṇānī, Mahākālī, the great red-yellow Goat-Headed One, the great blue-black Kumbhakarṇī, the great yellow-black Lambodarā, and to the queens, ministresses, female commanders, attendants and sub-attendants, servants and sub-servants and the rest, the handmaids, concubines and bondwomen.

"This Secret Essence, Ascertaining,
is the king of all secret mantras.
The wisdom symbolized by great means,
the Essence of the Net of Illusion,
and the many secret tantras
are emanated from my mind.
In the domains of Brahmā and Śakra,
Maheśvara (Śiva) and the rest,
of the commanders and of Hārītī,
in Videha and Godānīya,
in Kuru and in Jambudvīpa,
this essence of my mind
is to be spread through all ten directions.
As for entrusting it to a person:
the abode of the command, rich as a wish-fulfilling treasury,
the outer and inner vehicles and the treatises,
and the eighty-four thousand teachings,
to one skilled in conduct, meditation, recitation,
and in all the vows,
armored in meditation, on the side of the Dharma,
able to uphold the teaching,
entrust the Secret Essence as supreme;
it is not to be told to others.
To one unshakable, profound of mind, able to bear conditions,
devoted to wish-fulfilling union,
teach the root and branch samayas.
With a partner, the samaya is great.
Skilled in both kinds of union,
able to accord with scripture and valid measure,
not telling it to others,
and making offerings to the teaching:
in the samaya of close approach,
in accomplishment, approach and close approach,
strive greatly, as far as one is able.
Having abandoned all the ways of ordinary beings,
whose mind is disturbed by nothing,
rushing with strength to the feast of offerings,
summoning women, offer to the chief.
By one skilled in secrecy, able to take a consort,
let it be fully entrusted to one who is certain;
it should not be taught elsewhere.
Enjoy desire, unobstructed and free.
To a woman who has every quality,
and who acts in accord with all desire,
once she has accepted the root,
show the body of the deity fittingly;
but the secret instructions of close approach
should never be given.
Having shown her that way of union,
nothing else is taught to be shown.
Faithful, gentle and sharp-witted,
of great faith and respectful to the Dharma,
suited to passion, obedient to the command,
happy to remain and listening to scripture,
accomplishing the root and branch samayas,
preparing the place of offering, guarding the vows:
having caused the blessing to descend in the approach,
in the place of offering of the feast of secret mantra,
having caused my secret to descend in blessing,
unobstructedly, in direct perception,
possessing the means of union, joining in union,
in the union that delights the nature
of the forty-two maṇḍalas,
make offering to the feast with the accomplishment of equality.
To those who lack the samaya, coarse in conduct,
who do not hold the teaching from the depths,
do not spread desire,
and do not show the meaning
of The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality.
My empowerments and trainings
are to be given fully, in proper order.
Men and women of low birth who indulge their desires,
of little enthusiasm and very great desire,
weak-minded, envious, given to exaggeration and denial,
fond of sleep, fond of beer, of little diligence,
disrespectful toward training, without earnestness,
not remembering the master, with little thought for their siblings,
even if they abide in the samaya,
cannot truly rejoice
in upholding my secret.
They are weak in view, transgressing the command,
hard to liberate, and weak in power.
In darkness, in secret, and among people,
wherever they meet in solitude they indulge desire,
and to some of those of desire
they speak the secret words.
Except in accord with the actual meaning,
the Secret Essence is not to be given.
Even until the world of the eon ends,
do not let the bliss of the mind radiate,
and never teach it.
If, out of delusion, it is given
for the sake of those who delight in worldly desire,
life is cut off by fever and disease;
it is the abandonment of root and branch,
and one dwells long in burning and torment."

When this had been spoken, all the hosts of women of all existence were tamed by this samaya. Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, brought forth this secret vajra word from the vajra of body, speech, mind, qualities and activity:

"Oh, how wonderful! How wonderful! Phenomena are illusion.
That very state is woman, the secret of the mantrin,
set down in five and six times, the treasure from which appearance and existence arise.
Having found the true path, it abides manifestly everywhere, abides everywhere.
Of however many mudrās of the many phenomena and of the equality of the nature of reality,
this great path, the miracle that turns bad actions into virtue,
the distinction of them all, is the one bliss of woman.
Because it is the very nature of all phenomena, it is taught fittingly, so that the lesser do not disparage it.
The very wealth of illusion is the Sugata himself, utterly clear."

In secret vajra words.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the forty-ninth chapter, the Taming of Women by the Great Samaya.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, spoke this pleasing offering for that purpose:

"Therein the great offering sacrifice is this:
first, liberate oneself through realization and familiarization.
Then, with a mind that is nondual,
with compassion for evil-minded worldly beings,
liberate them nondually.
With the great offering of embrace, in union,
delight others by delighting oneself.
The great substances of the accomplishment of equality
offer to the feast that is equal to oneself.
Food and chewables, drink and clothing,
all that possesses the five enjoyments:
maṇḍala dissolves into maṇḍala.
The wondrous qualities of the sugatas,
gone in the ten directions and the four times,
are not found anywhere else;
what need is there to speak of the existence of the gods and the rest?
For yogins who are fit for the work,
the Great Mudrā becomes complete,
and the clear mind, which is nondual,
becomes the great blazing maṇḍala."

When this had been spoken, great delight pervaded all the ten directions of the six worlds.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the fiftieth chapter, on the Offering Sacrifice.

Then the Blessed One, Great Delight, in order to make it meaningful, spoke the great samaya for that purpose.

"For those who have become certain in the unsurpassed vehicle,
even though they engage in all the actions of the afflictions,
what they do amasses no karma but becomes the accumulations,
and their moral discipline and vows are perfect.
In the samaya of the unsurpassed and supreme,
the moral discipline taught for the sake of discipline,
and however many inconceivable vows there are,
are all, without exception, included and completely pure.
Not even a middle way between existence and nonexistence is apprehended;
in the manner of an illusion or a mirage,
there is no life, and in killing there is nothing to kill;
life and person are mere misconceptions.
Because the two truths are inseparable,
it is the way of delight in magical display, not something separate.
Because there is no other and nothing not given,
there is no taking: all is the very expanse.
Phenomena are like illusions,
and what is designated by names and words is false;
practicing falsehood upon falsehood itself,
'false' is a mere designation and does not exist.
Attachment to what is free of attachment,
and, in attachment itself, no attachment:
that is the king of supreme pure conduct,
the very great attachment.
Do not abandon the unsurpassed; honor the master;
do not break the continuity of mantra and mudrā;
be loving toward those who have entered the genuine path;
for the sake of the secret meaning, do not speak of it.
These are the five roots,
the supreme samayas of practice and of guarding.
Delusion, desire, aversion,
pride and jealousy are not abandoned.
The white elixir, the red elixir, the scented substances and the great flesh
are not discarded, in a pure vessel.
These are the supreme samayas of the ten branches,
practiced with the understanding that all is primordially pure and equal.
The five samayas of guarding and practice
are the roots; breaking them, one becomes cut off from the family.
The five not to be abandoned and the five not to be discarded
are the branch samayas, hard to transgress.
If one abides in the samaya of equality,
joining equality with equality,
one will attain the great perfection of equality;
if one transgresses it, one is not a buddha.
With common people whose root samayas are damaged
and who make no effort to restore them,
do not converse even for an instant or a moment;
the faults of their damage cannot be told.
If the root samayas are damaged,
all practice is turned upside down,
and various unpleasant results
gather, though they are unwanted.
If the branch samayas are damaged,
there is no result, and one falls into the lower realms.
The samayas are the great vajra,
the very nature of all the buddhas.
Of the five root samayas,
each one is divided into two and three tens;
of the ten branch samayas,
each one is divided into two tens.
This samaya is most wondrous.
In the ten directions of the six worlds,
as many beings as there are in the three existences,
so many samayas unfold to tame their thoughts.
Moreover, by the mudrā of all without exception
of the supreme Victor Samantabhadra,
the sublime, without effortful practice, is accomplished without exception.
These and other infinite supreme ones,
holders of the supreme family of the Victors,
are honored by the lords of the world and their retinues.
By the supreme of the sublime and by the sublime ones
they are blessed, regarded as sons and brothers.
They abide in the very domain of the Sugata
and are joined with fearless Samantabhadra.
Suchness and the means of taming,
however many inconceivable vows there are,
are all completely pure and spontaneously accomplished;
if damaged, they are made complete by restoration;
these and other inconceivable supreme ones."

When this was spoken, the Tathāgatas themselves were tamed within the Tathāgatas themselves.

From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, this is the fifty-first chapter, on Samaya.

Then, from the King of Tantras, the Net of Illusion, the Tathāgata, in order to make it meaningful for the mantra holders, summarized the root of the three hundred samayas and spoke this inspired utterance for the sake of the mantra holders:

"In worlds as many as the dust motes of the ten directions,
there are unbearable misconceptions as many as atoms,
and the antidotes that tame thoughts are inconceivable.
The inconceivable disciplines and vows,
and all the many supreme vajra vehicles,
though they stir from the very nature and are without elaboration,
through the deluded thought of one's own awareness
there are inconceivable views, high and low;
to tame them, what suits each was taught.
The genuine and the misconceived, both,
are transformed in their essential nature,
and in that there is nothing to contrive or change.
If from the very beginning there is no essential nature,
there are no afflictions to abandon and no aim to achieve;
all without exception becomes of one taste.
Since the relative is like the moon in water,
it can become anything and accomplish anything.
For the yogin who realizes that suchness,
the ultimate is without arising or ceasing,
and the relative, an illusion, accomplishes anything."

So it is in the secret words of the vajra.

From the Net of Illusion, this is the fifty-second chapter, the concise explanation of the fifteen roots of the three hundred and sixty samayas.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption of the play of the vajra mind and spoke this inspired utterance:

"From the clear center of the sky
arise masses of wish-fulfilling clouds in many forms;
various things arise, and light radiates and blazes.
raṃ This is the fire that burns the three existences;
it blazes as the fire of the wisdom of mind.
haṃ In the clear maṇḍala of the senses,
the substances of the sense fields are gathered
and subside, vivid and radiant, into light.
a From the expanse of peace the mudrā blazes,
the blessing of compassion, most wondrous.
hūṃ Accomplishing the aims of the various beings,
this is declared to be supreme awakening.
ho It destroys the four and purifies the lower realms.
trāṃ ta As the alchemy of medicine purifies poison,
the outer and inner stains are purified;
they are made pure by the two selflessnesses.
hri Clear and pure."

By this all the abodes were made to quake and stir violently, and so their chiefs, together with their retinues and servants, came as though driven by the force of the wind. They were these: Yama, Lord of Life, Mahākāla, with his consort; the Great Black Māra with fiery breath; his consort, the black māra woman Dripping Blood; the king of the rākṣasas, Black Killer, with his consort; the great yakṣa Pūrṇabhadra; his consort, the yakṣiṇī Ekajaṭī, eater of flesh; the king of the gods, Maheśvara (Śiva); the lord of the rudras, Black Liberation; the great king Mujé Tsenpa; the lord of the outer gods, Black Kāla; great Viṣṇu, who roams everywhere; the lord of the nāgas, Fierce Youth; the great king of the gods, Lichin; the great yakṣa Hand Roamer; the great being, Vajra Beauty; the great nāga māra, Yamshü Marpo; and, besides these, inconceivable hosts of gods, nāgas, asuras, devourers, hungry ghosts, bhūtas, and terrifying messengers all gathered together.

Then the Blessed One, knowing that he would thoroughly tame that retinue of great malevolent ones, manifestly emanated the maṇḍala of the great charnel ground:

"The maṇḍalas of the expanse of space,
reflected as forms, are utterly terrifying.
Their sounds are harsh, violent and fierce;
their smells are foul, reeking of rot and decay;
their tastes are sour and burning;
their touch is hot and rough, yet does no harm.
The fire of great aversion blazes;
pride is a crag packed with skeletons;
the black darkness of delusion gathers thick;
desire roars as a great river of blood;
the wind of jealousy rages wildly.
Amid the revelry of the great sthira,
tigers, leopards, beasts of prey, jackals and wolves,
kaṅkas, vultures and flocks of birds,
piśācas and blood-drinkers crunch bones.
Bloated, rotting and decaying corpses,
scattered remains and wailing voices,
cries and roars ring out fiercely.
hūṃ he he phaṭ bhyo ru lu
Meditate on yourself as nondual
with the blazing face of the great maṇḍala
of the assembly of great wrathful ones.
Having recited each one's own mantra,
from all the sentient beings of the six classes,
all the wicked and malevolent ones
are summoned with jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥs;
three times or seven times dissolve them into the mouth;
even if it be a god, he will be destroyed.
hoḥ Moreover, the food offering
is offered with every kind of wealth:
human fat and pungent butter,
and the red argha is offered continuously.
a hoḥ Or else one should offer it three times, a hundred and eight times,
a thousand or a hundred thousand times.
With black thorns or poisonous wood,
or other substances for burning, the three harmful ones,
one should perform every rite of suppression."

From the Net of Illusion, the general teaching of the maṇḍala of the play of compassion, hoṃḥ oṃ hūṃ hrāṃ hrīḥ āḥ. From The Secret Essence, Ascertaining Reality, hauṃḥ, this is the fifty-fourth chapter, in which all without exception are gathered here.

Then the Tathāgata, the king of the wrathful ones, meditating as the assembly of fierce wrathful ones from the teaching of the Net of Illusion, spoke this inspired utterance:

"The essence of supreme awakening
is not something sought through signs;
but through the three purities, without error,
and by familiarity with the king, self-awareness,
this supreme awakening will be found.
To accomplish the violent and fierce,
the liṅga is placed in the bhaga,
and the effigy should be made together with the name.
Whichever wrathful face it may be,
meditate on it constantly, three times or seven times,
and the face that becomes manifest blazes.
Having become yourself the deity of the maṇḍala,
gather the liṅga again and again
and dissolve it into the center of the expanse.
Gather it with hūṃ and cast it with phaṭ.
With the mouth, recite your own mantra
ten thousand or a hundred thousand times in the threefold manner,
and feed it again and again.
All the sentient beings of the five destinies
will dissolve helplessly into the mouth.
Yama too is especially splendid.
Body colors like conch and the rest,
fangs bared and wrathful brows knit,
mouths gaping and blazing mudrās:
these and the rest should be meditated upon.
This is the mudrā of holding oneself.
Having taken the effigy, for the purpose of one's own mantra,
where it can be plainly seen with the senses,
gather the pills and offer them into the expanse;
it is certain to be accomplished without doubt.
Because the hero has need
of the wicked who destroy the sacred Dharma,
do that which benefits all!
hoḥ hūṃ jaḥdaṅ and phaṭ and transference and the rest
he declared to be supreme for guiding them.
The effigy gathered into the name, too,
just as before,
should be set on the path of awakening through aspiration;
make it free of the habitual tendencies of signs.
Emanate it from the expanse and dissolve it into a.
a ho
In the mind itself, the mind is spoken."

From the Net of Illusion, this is the fifty-fifth chapter, the close teaching of the extremely secret wrathful ones.

Then the Tathāgata, the king of the wrathful ones, gathered all activities into the vajra and spoke this inspired utterance:

"Meditating in the manner of the three purities,
abiding in the teaching of the Net of Illusion,
gather everything into hūṃ
and meditate on a blazing triangular maṇḍala.
In the great mass of blazing fire,
summon the fire god with agne jaḥs,
kindle the fire called raṃ,
and meditate on the entire wrathful maṇḍala, without exception,
as vajra aversion.
hūṃ The great assembly of the fierce ones blazes intensely,
the color of black smoke,
churning with many blazing rays,
adorned with many suns, skulls and snakes,
lions, elephants and ferocious tigers,
and holding various blazing implements.
The sounds of phaṭ, hūṃ and ha la,
fierce cries, churn the sky in the ten directions.
phaṭ They are surrounded by hosts of wrathful gods and goddesses,
beautified by the mudrās of the lands and sacred places.
ha he They are surrounded by hosts of attendants, maidservants and slave women,
bhyo and of kiṃkaras and the rest,
with their own seats and implements,
proclaiming ferocious roars and their own mantras.
Meditate on them as having ugly, fierce forms
and as extremely wrathful.
Yourself in the very state of phaṭ,
meditate always with clarity, intimacy and a trained mind.
Having gathered with jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥs,
gather all the sentient beings of the six classes
and offer them to the wrathful assembly;
even if it be a god, he will be destroyed.
The other requisites accord with the general rite;
the means of torment, too, are as above;
the number of offerings also accords with the general rite.
āḥ Moreover, of the inconceivable maṇḍala,
hūṃ meditate on wrathful faces, mouths gaping and eyes glaring,
brandishing vajra bolts,
surrounded by skull cups and hooks,
blazing with the assembly of the six sounds of hūṃ.
Having summoned them with the mudrā of seizing,
if they are dissolved into the mouth, it is accomplished all the more;
not even a particle of dust is left.
The assembly of the retinue, too, is as before;
in the manner of fulfillment, send off the dispatch offering.
hūṃ The fierce red one with long lips,
surrounded by a garland of blazing vajras,
with great light blazing and radiating,
crushes the world realms all at once.
hūṃ The wrathful assembly fully recites
the mantras, and the mudrās of the sacred places and lands,
and the hosts of attendants, maidservants and slave women
recite their own mantras in full.
ha he bhyo Moreover, with these words of praise:
hūṃ By the blessing of vajra compassion
all the world realms are filled,
and all the fierce disturbers, without exception,
are subdued by the power of the vajra.
hūṃ Vajra aversion with its retinue
destroys all the infinite world realms
in an instant, in a moment;
there is nothing at all that is not destroyed."

From the Net of Illusion, this is the fifty-sixth chapter, on the fierce homa.

Then the Tathāgata, the king of the wrathful ones called Lotus Gatherer, for the sake of many beings, spoke this inspired utterance:

"Through the union of wisdom and skillful means,
all without exception are gathered into the expanse;
from the expanse the light of primordial wisdom radiates and blazes,
and all without exception are joined in the expanse.
hrīḥ From an outspread crescent maṇḍala
many rays of light blaze intensely,
and it is encircled by bound vajra chains.
Furthermore, all the most sublime of ornaments
adorn and encircle it.
Inviting the fire god as before,
meditate on the entire wrathful maṇḍala, without exception,
as the wrathful ones of the lotus family,
with the color of fierce crimson,
and with many further emanations
encircling the four directions, the four intermediate directions, above and below,
fiercely proclaiming their own ferocious mantras.
hrīḥ Yourself being the vajra of nonattachment,
having meditated clearly on the maṇḍala above,
all the beings of the ten directions
and all the malevolent-minded, without exception,
summoned with jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ and spho ṭa,
should be offered to the maṇḍala.
The intention is accomplished and the lower realms are purified.
hrīḥ Meditate on the hosts of many celestial palaces and maṇḍalas,
the maṇḍala shaped like the face of a lotus,
with lotus trees at its edge,
and blazing with the light of lotuses and vajras.
Furthermore, adorn every maṇḍala beautifully
with lovely supreme ornaments.
Having become yourself the lotus deity,
if you constantly make offerings
of various divine substances,
the finest food and drink,
red wood and sweet fragrances,
in the numbers of times mentioned above,
whatever action you desire,
of this world or beyond it, will be accomplished.
The mantra is the syllables raṃ and hrīḥ;
the color is crimson, of great radiance;
pervading the ten directions, it is gathered into hrī.
Offer with songs and dances of delight.
hrīḥ By the blazing vajra lotus,
the glory, splendor, life and the rest
of all the endless, infinite ten directions
are gathered into that blazing lotus,
equal to the body of the buddhas;
and further, the supreme enjoyments and the rest,
all the actions one desires, come under one's power;
what need is there to speak of gods, rākṣasas and the rest?
hrīḥ Vajra desire with its retinue
